- [x] **Adding an injury on the Profile screen now offers to rebuild the plan, 30 Aug 2026 — audit §2.1.** Before this, the injury saved and nothing else happened: every squat, lunge and step-up already in the sixteen-week plan stayed exactly where it was, permanently, because nothing re-ran. Same for changing your equipment. **Ask, never silently** — it is a dialog, declining leaves everything alone, and a rebuild only ever touches the current week onwards so nothing you have already logged is rewritten. Offers for injuries and equipment and for nothing else, so the dialog stays worth reading. Gated by `test:rebuild-offer`, five mutations each turning it red.

- [x] **A lift that has stopped climbing now says so, 30 Aug 2026 — audit §6.5.** Ashley's ruling: **ask for one real set.** For a lift you have never logged, the app steps the weight toward its estimate and then holds there — correctly, because going higher would mean inventing strength nobody has evidence for. But the sentence beside it kept saying *"this week goes up by one small step from last time"* while the number sat still, week after week. It now says the estimate has run out and asks for a logged set. **Not one prescribed weight moved** — verified across 6,860 prescriptions, identical before and after. Gated by `test:ramp-arrived`, five mutations each turning it red.

- [ ] **NEXT, and it needs its own plan: make a logged set re-anchor the weight.** The other half of Ashley's ruling. Today the only feedback from logged performance is the block-review suggestion — several sessions, main lifts only, and it fires when you BEAT the target, none of which is the stuck case. It also runs into her earlier standing ruling that the app never bumps load off logged performance without an explicit confirm. Reconciling those two is real work, not a slip-in.

- [x] **The bundle, 30 Aug 2026 — audit §12.** One 1,549 kB file (444 kB gzipped) became a 795 kB app chunk (222 kB gzipped) plus five library chunks. The headline is the second load, not the first: changing one line of app code used to re-download React, Supabase, the markdown renderer and the icon set with it, so a returning user paid 444 kB after every deploy and now pays **222 kB — half**. Onboarding (runs once, ever) and the developer test page (users cannot reach it) are lazy chunks, 70 kB nobody normal fetches. Gated by `test:bundle`, which measures a real build rather than reading the config. **Tab-level splitting was tried and rejected**: it saved 18 kB gzipped and would have put a full-screen spinner in front of every first tab visit.

- [x] **Accessibility, 30 Aug 2026 — audit §11.** Twenty controls contained nothing but an icon, so a screen reader announced them as "button" and nothing more: the send button, both week arrows, every save/cancel/edit/delete on the Profile screen, the tick that logs a set, both grocery-row actions. All twenty now say what they do, and name what they act on where the app knows it ("Remove oats from the list", "Save set 3"). Separately, all **273** font sizes moved from px to rem, so the OS text-size setting finally does something — px ignores it entirely. Verified identical at default settings: every converted value divides exactly by 16, checked pair by pair against the diff. Touch targets deliberately stayed in px — 44px is a floor, not a preference. Gated by `test:a11y`, five mutations each turning it red.

- [ ] **ASHLEY'S CALL: should "New Plan" delete the old plan?** Today it abandons it — the row stays in the database forever, invisible from the app. That was already true; sign-in just makes it visible, because the account still owns it. Options: (a) leave it, and old plans quietly accumulate; (b) delete it, so "New Plan" means what it says and the data actually goes; (c) offer to download it first, then delete. Recommend (c) — the export already exists, so it is a confirmation dialog, not new machinery. Nothing is broken either way; this is about what the button honestly means.

- [x] **Sign-in and row-level security, 30 Aug 2026 — audit §1.1 and §1.2.** Anonymous sign-in on load, so nobody sees a login screen; `owner_id` on every profile, scoped through one `owns_profile()` predicate across 25 tables plus `workout_exercises` via its parent; a `claim_profile` RPC that only ever touches unowned rows, so a profile from before accounts existed attaches itself on the next open. Ashley's ruling: **existing users keep everything and are asked for an email next time they open the app**, dismissibly, coming back in a week. Verified against a real PostgreSQL (`npm run test:rls-local`, 33 checks, five mutations each turning it red) — an anonymous caller now reads zero rows from every user table. **Not deployed: `npm run db:push-both` is Ashley's.**

- [x] **Correction to the spend cap (§1.3), same day.** `REVOKE ALL … FROM PUBLIC` did not close `increment_ai_usage` on Supabase — DEFAULT PRIVILEGES grant `anon` directly, and revoking from PUBLIC leaves a direct grant alone. Anyone with the app's public key could have pushed another user over their daily cap, or driven the global scope and taken the coach down for everyone. Fixed in `20260830130000`, found by running the migrations rather than reading them.

- [ ] **FULL APP AUDIT, 29 Aug 2026 — see [AUDIT-2026-08-29.md](AUDIT-2026-08-29.md).** Report only, nothing built. 14 sections; every finding labelled measured / proven / observed, and the four things I could not test from this machine are listed rather than glossed. Reproducible measurements live in `scripts/audit-probe-*.ts`.

- [ ] **The two critical ones are about data, not features.** All **112 of 112** database policies across **28 tables** are anonymous full read/write/delete, and the key that unlocks them ships in the app bundle — so anyone can download or delete every user's weight history, injuries, allergies and chat transcripts. That was a deliberate single-tenant choice (the migration says so) and it stopped being right when the app got users. Second: there is no account at all — identity is one localStorage value, so clearing browser data destroys it permanently, and on iPhone Safari can evict it after ~7 days of not opening the app. Sign-in fixes both and is days of work, not hours.

- [ ] **The almond-butter defect class is much wider than the almond butter.** No Profile edit triggers anything: **11 measured checks, all failing**. Add a nut allergy and today's meals are never re-checked (permanent); add an injury or change equipment and the plan is never rebuilt (permanent); change weight/age/activity and macros stay stale until the next reload (self-heals). The app's own checker correctly rejects a peanut-butter breakfast under `nut-free` — enforcement just only ever runs at generation time.

- [ ] **Two free-text fields that look saved and do nothing.** Profile > Injuries: **12 of 14** realistic entries are ignored, including `"Lower back"` — the field's own placeholder. Profile > Foods to avoid: **10 of 122** foods the user plainly meant are matched (**8%**); `"mushrooms"` misses `"mushroom"`, and that is also the field's own placeholder. Both are the exact defect the dietary picker was rewritten to fix, left standing two fields away. The app already owns a plural/alias-aware resolver the avoid-list doesn't use.

- [ ] **A coach suggestion older than 10 minutes has a dead Confirm button.** Proven with a probe: `claimPendingAction` returns `already_resolved` (expired) or `not_found`, and `handleConfirmProposal` handles only `stale` — the other two fall through to a bare `return`, so the card is not updated, no message appears, and the button does nothing however many times it is pressed. Three lines to fix.

- [ ] **The deployed coach refuses three injuries the app can handle.** `propose_injury_adaptation`/`propose_injury_as_lasting` still enum only 5 areas and still say "only these 5 have joint-conflict data". Hips/ankles/elbows were wired up (they remove 11/10/11 exercises, gated by `test:injury-coverage`) and the prompt was never updated. Needs a `chat-gemini` deploy, not just a push.

- [ ] **Silent-failure sweep.** The ⇄ swap and the ban button keep an optimistic UI on a failed save (console-only); banning offline throws before doing anything at all; steps logging has no error handling; the three memory-edit saves ignore the returned error. And **four of five local-first queues** (water, grocery, cardio, meal events) can dead-letter with no indicator anywhere — only logged sets are surfaced.

- [ ] **Also found:** the coach's "did I train today" uses the UTC date while every log is stamped local (wrong for most of the world at some hour of every day); the shopping list ignores manual meal swaps; no wake-lock, no notifications and no iOS vibration, so the rest timer can't reach you mid-workout; not a PWA at all (no manifest, no service worker, no icon — `/favicon.svg` 404s on every load); one 1.53 MB JS bundle with no code splitting; 16 unlabelled icon buttons and 256 hard-coded px font sizes. **Text contrast measured clean in all five themes** — the one failing token is a decorative aria-hidden dot, checked before reporting.

- [ ] **THE PLAN-QUALITY SCORER IS ITSELF A FINDINGS SOURCE — added as §6.5 after the first pass was written.** It passes (11.51/12 across **9,216** combinations, 0 below the 7.2 floor) and takes **1,350s / 22m30s**, which is why it never gets run. Its rule-frequency table is the interesting part: **`frozen_week` trips in 5,689 of 9,216 plans — 61.7%**, almost all bodyweight work (pull-ups/chin-ups stuck at "4-6 @ bodyweight" week after week, only the RPE label moving), plus light isolation frozen at 8kg. A user on a bodyweight-led plan sees an unchanged prescription every week while the app calls it progressive. Also `time_fit` 5.0% (worst case 44% under a requested 90-120min → 50min) and `recovery_volume_not_reduced` 4.9% (a deload that doesn't deload). Nothing here is new to the code — the scorer already reported all of it; what's new is reading the frequencies as product findings rather than as a gate that passed.

- [x] **THE HONESTY RULE ALREADY FORBADE THAT SENTENCE — FOR ALLERGENS ONLY.** `ALLERGEN_HONESTY_BLOCK` says never state or imply a meal "is X-free" or "won't contain" X, and it is written about the eight tagged categories. Almond butter arrived as a **preference**, so nothing covered it and the model produced a textbook "is X-free" claim. Extended: never state a food is **absent** except by reading a list actually given, within that list's stated limits. Stating the **action** ("I've filtered that out") is still allowed — that is a thing the app did.

- [x] AND A STRUCTURAL RULE FOUND WHILE WIRING IT, CAUGHT BY `test:coach-rules-sync` DOING ITS JOB: that block is duplicated verbatim into `_shared/coach-rules.ts` and read by **onboarding-chat**, which has no meal plan and no `propose_meal_swap`. The generic honesty half goes to both; **the swap instruction goes only where the tool exists**, or the rule becomes a promise of a button that isn't there. Gated both ways.

- [x] ASHLEY'S FIRST COMPLAINT — *"it didn't even offer to remove it from my meal"* — is the same root cause and is now answered: the coach offers the swap through the existing confirm-card rather than telling her to go to the Nutrition tab herself. New gate `test:coach-sees-ingredients`, 30 checks, **seven mutations bite**, M1 reproducing the original blind summary exactly.

- [x] VERIFIED IN PASSING AND LEFT ALONE: the *other* promise was true. `verifyProposal` (`meal-generation.ts:297`) hard-filters candidates whose ingredient names contain the disliked phrase, so "excluded starting your next meal regenerate" is real. Only the claim about **today** was false.

- [ ] **NEEDS `npm run deploy:functions:prod -- chat-gemini`.** The client half — the coach being able to see the ingredient at all — ships with the Vercel push. Both prompt halves are inert until that deploy, which was **already outstanding for two other rules**. Shipping the client half alone removes the blindness but leaves the licence to guess.

- [ ] **SYNONYMS ARE STILL LITERAL.** "almond butter" will not find "almond paste", and `mealsContaining` is deliberately not cleverer than the generator's own filter — a dislike the generator would act on must be one the app reports, and vice versa. The limit is stated in the coach's honesty rules rather than papered over. Widening it is its own decision.

- [x] **"IF YOU MAKE A WRONG SELECTION AND THEN SEND THE RIGHT ANSWER AFTER IT, IT MESSES WITH THE NEXT QUESTION AND THE NEXT QUICK REPLY."** Ashley. Three faults chained, plus a fourth reachable from the same move. `present_slot` was refused for **any** already-answered slot — right for "stop re-asking what you know", wrong for the one re-ask that matters, so a corrected question came back with the coach saying *"which one is you?"* and **nothing to tap**.

- [x] IT WAS ALSO DECIDED **MID-LOOP, AGAINST A TURN THAT HADN'T FINISHED HAPPENING.** Actions arrive in whatever order the model emitted them, so a `present_slot` listed before its own `set_slot` was judged before the correction it belonged to had been applied, and the message meant to host the chips might not have been pushed yet. Now collected during the loop and resolved after it — the same reasoning the review check in the same file already used, applied one level down.

- [x] AND WITH NO CARD ON THE NEW QUESTION, THE TYPING BOX WENT LOOKING AND FOUND AN **OLDER** ONE. The placeholder takes the most recent pending card, which after a correction is a question from further up the transcript — so it named something other than what she was reading. **Fourth failure of this kind in that block**; it now compares which is *newer*, the pending card or the coach's latest question, and says "Say anything…" rather than naming a stale one.

- [x] **ASHLEY'S RULING: show the buttons again** — *"tapping is how the wrong answer got in and it should be how it gets out."* A slot re-opens **only** when this turn changed it to a different value than it already held; a first answer does not open the door, so the model still cannot re-present everything it has just been told.

- [x] FOURTH FAULT, FOUND WHILE TRACING AND **WIDENED BY TODAY'S OWN CHANGE**: correcting an earlier answer can close a later question (say you're a beginner and the working-lifts question no longer applies — which is only true because of this morning's ruling). The write was already refused as a stale card; the chips carried on **looking tappable and silently doing nothing**. A control that cannot act is no longer offered.

- [x] NEW GATE `test:onboarding-corrections`, 24 checks, five mutations bite. §6 is executable rather than source text — it proves the stranded-card state is real by watching the working-lifts question go live for an advanced lifter and dead the moment they correct themselves to beginner, on both surfaces.

- [ ] **A TYPED CORRECTION CAN STILL LAND IN THE WRONG QUESTION, AND I DID NOT TOUCH IT.** Five texts are valid answers to more than one question (`Functional`, `Moderate`, `Mediterranean`, and yes/no), and a typed answer goes to the **most recent** question showing buttons — right when answering forward, wrong when correcting backward. The obvious fix (refuse ambiguity) is recorded in that code as **already tried and measured to regress**: four multi-selects all accept "none", so whenever two of their cards were live the stricter version captured *nothing at all*. Re-introducing it would trade a rare wrong-slot for a common silent drop. Needs a different idea, not a retry.

- [x] **"I DON'T LIKE THE 'I'M NEW / NOT SURE' — JUST SAY NOT SURE. AND A BEGINNER SHOULD ALWAYS GET A CALIBRATION WEEK."** Ashley, on the live onboarding card. The label called someone new for not having tested lately, and tapping the *other* option set `skip_calibration_week` **whatever experience they had just reported** — so a self-described beginner could start heavy on a figure nobody had checked. Label is now **"Not sure"**, and `generateMesocycle` refuses to skip calibration for a beginner. Measured across all four tiers: beginner week 1 `isCalibrationWeek: true` at 72.5 kg heaviest; novice/intermediate/advanced unchanged at 75 kg and still skipping.

- [x] **THEN SHE WENT FURTHER: "A BEGINNER SHOULDN'T BE ASKED THEIR NUMBERS."** Offered the narrower option — ask anyway, and tell them week 1 still verifies — she chose not to ask at all. I recommended against it once and she confirmed; her version is the more coherent one, because a beginner's number is precisely the number the engine has already decided it will not prescribe off, so asking and then verifying anyway is a question whose answer we half-distrust. `willBeLiftingBarbells` now excludes every beginner, not just the sedentary ones it already excluded, and the three lift-weight questions close behind it.

- [x] AND IT HOLDS ON **BOTH** SURFACES, WHICH THE CHIP CHECK ALONE COULDN'T SEE. `buildSlotCatalog` filters on the same gate, so the model is never told the question exists and cannot ask it in prose — the route by which a "removed" question keeps getting asked conversationally with no chip guard anywhere near it. Gated as its own check.

- [x] **THE RENAME CREATED A LIVE REGRESSION AND THE GREP FOUND IT.** `STUCK_SIGNAL` matches `/^not sure$/`, and `handleResolveSingle` sends the tapped option's **label** as the message text — so every tap on the new chip read as *"I don't know"* and drew the rescue reply *"No problem — here are the options."*, offered to someone who had just answered plainly. Typing the same two words hit it via the exact-label backstop. The answer was never lost, so this was tone, not data — but it is the exact failure `STUCK_SIGNAL`'s own comment warns against.

- [x] FIXED AS THE CLASS, NOT THE LABEL: the rescue now stands down whenever **this message already recorded an answer**, keyed on that rather than on "was it a tap", so a future label colliding with the stuck vocabulary is covered too. Deliberately a **different flag from `immediateCommit`**, which the allergen backstop also sets for an unrelated reason — sharing it would mean disclosing an allergy suppressed a genuine "I don't know". The gate reports which labels collide rather than banning collisions, because the label is Ashley's call and the handling is ours.

- [x] SEVEN CHECKS ADDED, FIVE MUTATIONS BITE (revert the label, drop either beginner guard, restore the stale comment, remove the rescue guard). The `test-onboarding-slots` source checks **strip comments before matching** — this gate family has been broken *and* silently satisfied by its own explanatory prose more than once, and a rule about code should not be provable by a sentence about it.

- [x] ONE STALE CHECK CORRECTED RATHER THAN DELETED: §13 asserted *asked about lifts ⟺ engine prescribes lifting*. Ashley's ruling deliberately breaks the ⟸ half — an active beginner gets a lifting plan and is still not asked — so the safety direction (*a walks plan is never asked about barbell weights*) is now asserted on its own, and the replaced half is restated as its own rule so it cannot quietly rot back.

- [ ] **A BEGINNER CAN NO LONGER VOLUNTEER THEIR NUMBERS EITHER, AND IT HAPPENS SILENTLY.** The `isSlotApplicable` guard refuses a model-driven write into a closed slot, so *"I already squat 60kg"* from a beginner is dropped with only a `console.warn`. Correct safety direction and it follows from the ruling — but silently discarding a volunteered number is the exact defect the slot-ordering work was done to fix. One line of coach acknowledgement is the obvious answer; it is Ashley's call, not a mechanical fix.

- [ ] THE SEEDLING ICON ON THAT OPTION WAS CHANGED TO `❓` UNPROMPTED — flagging it because it goes past the letter of the instruction. 🌱 is also the Beginner chip's icon, so the option still *said* "new" in pictures after the words stopped. Trivially revertible if Ashley wants it back.

- [x] **635 FORM CUES, WRITTEN, SHIPPED IN EVERY BUNDLE, AND NEVER ONCE SHOWN TO ANYONE.** Ashley: *"there's nowhere in the app to see an exercise."* All **158** catalogue entries carry `form_cues` (~4 each) and all 158 carry `coach_note_swap` — and `form_cues` had **zero readers in the entire repo**: not the UI, not a prompt, not a test. The only route to "how does this go?" was chat typing a **YouTube search-results URL** that leaves the app and needs signal in a gym.

- [x] **"How to do it" now opens from the ⋮ menu**, in today's session and the peek, one shared dialog on the `ExerciseHistoryDialog` precedent. Cues numbered, the rationale that previously appeared only on *other* exercises, muscles, equipment, joint load — joints through `jointListDisplay`, never the raw tag. Plus one honest line: cues are a reminder, not coaching, and anything that hurts is a reason to stop.

- [x] AND SEARCHING THE CATALOGUE NO LONGER TELLS YOU **LESS** THAN BROWSING IT. `SwapDialog`'s ranked candidates carried a coaching sentence; its free-search results carried two taxonomy badges and nothing — on the surface where you are *least* likely to know the movement. Now both.

- [x] **A DEFECT I SHIPPED LAST NIGHT, FOUND WHILE EXPLORING THIS.** `exercise/WeekStrip.tsx` **is dead code — no importers.** `d517738` extracted `week-glyphs.ts` so Home and Exercise could not drift and wired it into **the dead file**; the strip users see (`WeekContextRow.tsx`) kept a private `GLYPH` copy. `test-tab-ownership` asserted *"Exercise's strip IS interactive"* against a component nothing renders — **a gate passing on dead code**, the exact failure the M5 mutation was written to catch, reached from a direction nothing checked.

- [x] IT WAS HIDING A LIVE ACCESSIBILITY BUG TOO: `WeekContextRow`'s `aria-label` interpolated the **raw state**, so a screen reader announced *"Monday: before_plan"* — an identifier. `STATE_LABEL` was written to fix exactly that, and the fix went into the copy nobody renders. Both files deleted, the live strip re-pointed, and the gate now **requires every strip file it speaks for to be imported by something**.

- [x] FOUR MUTATIONS BITE — and two of them only after the mutation caught **my own checks** being wrong: one matched the same prop names in a *destructuring* rather than the *forwarding* that makes the menu appear; another passed on `{false && (…)}` because the string was still present in unreachable markup. Both were character-window regexes; the fix was to slice the real function body instead of guessing a distance, which I had got wrong three times.

- [x] **DAYLIGHT'S ACCENT DARKENED TO `#008C72` — Ashley's ruling.** `#00A88A` measured **2.74:1** against its own canvas, under the 3:1 floor the same design brief introduced, so the new theme's first act was warning about its own colour. `#008C72` measures **3.82:1**; the deep step moves to `#00705B` so the CTA gradient keeps its depth instead of going flat.

- [x] **AND MINT'S DARK STEP WITH IT — beyond the letter of the instruction, flagged here because it was.** That value was *also* `#00A88A`, so choosing the Mint chip on Daylight re-created the exact warning the theme fix had just cleared, one tap away. Same colour, same failure, and it only applies to light canvases — of which Daylight is the only one. **All 30 combinations now clear the floor**, up from 28.

- [x] THE GATE'S CLAIM CHANGED SHAPE WITH THE PALETTE. It asserted *"some combination trips the guard"* to prove the guard was not vacuous — a true statement about a palette that shipped a theme warning about itself, and one that went red the moment the palette got better. It now asserts the **stronger** property (every shipped pair clears 3:1) and proves reachability against a **colour chosen to fail**, because "the guard works" and "we ship something broken" are different claims.

- [x] AND A HOLE THE MUTATION FOUND: the css/preview parity check pinned each theme's `--background` and **not its accent** — so `index.css` and `appearance-palette.ts` could disagree about the very value this ruling changed in both files, silently, on the one screen whose job is showing you what you are choosing. Now pinned for all five themes.

- [x] **"I DIDN'T TELL THE APP I COULD DEADLIFT 150 BUT IT CLAIMS I DID."** Ashley's live Exercise tab: **Trap Bar Deadlift 152.5 kg**, labelled **YOU TOLD US**, ramping to 140×1. Her second point is the one that matters: *"150kg is a lot and someone who hasn't specified or shown they can lift that could injure themselves."*

- [x] **THE APP ALREADY HELD THE PROOF AND NEVER LOOKED.** Against its own `STRENGTH_STANDARDS_1RM_PER_BW` for an 86 kg advanced male: the stated **bench of 150 kg is 116% of the advanced one-rep-max estimate** (129 kg) — a *working* weight above what it thinks a top-tier lifter's single max is. And the stated **deadlift ÷ bench = 1.00×** when the **lowest ratio anywhere in that table**, either sex and every level, is **1.67×**. The only validation was `isNumberIn(1, 500)`. The app has a typo guard for what weights you *own* and none for what you can *lift*.

- [x] AND THE NUMBER WAS THEN TRUSTED ABOVE EVERYTHING: `load_source: 'known_weight'` outranks the estimate, skips the starting-light hedge, and skips the calibration week. **One unverified figure became a confident heavy prescription on day one.**

- [x] **ASHLEY'S RULING: "ask once, and never skip calibration on it."** Both halves built. `lift-plausibility.ts` **imports** the standards rather than restating them, so the rule and the prescription can never disagree. Two narrow rules — above the **top-tier** 1RM ceiling (skipped when bodyweight was assumed, since a ratio against a guess proves nothing), and a deadlift under 1.25× bench (needs no bodyweight, so it still protects someone who declined it). **Zero false positives** across five plausible lifters including a bench specialist at 1.4×.

- [x] MEASURED: her week-1 heaviest drops from a **152.5 kg deadlift to a 97.5 kg squat**, and `isCalibrationWeek` flips **true** — while a trustworthy profile still skips calibration and still anchors at 137.5 kg, so the feature is not broken for everyone. Five mutations bite; reverting the calibration guard reproduces **exactly 152.5 kg**, the number from the screenshot.

- [x] ~~**STILL NO WAY TO CORRECT A STATED LIFT IN THE PROFILE SCREEN.** The only route is chat, via `record_goal` → `fact-compiler.ts` → the same columns.~~ **That description was wrong when written** — the 30 Aug audit proved `compileKnownLiftOverrides` had zero callers, so the chat route did not exist either and a stated lift was uncorrectable by any means. Wired 30 Aug; the goal route now genuinely works. What remains is discoverability, tracked in the newer entry at the top, not a dead end.

- [x] **"THE TOUR NEEDS UPDATING SINCE WE CHANGED THE APP LAYOUT."** Ashley, and she was right — `test:app-tour` had just passed. §1–2 check every spotlight **target** still exists, which they did; nothing could see that three steps were describing a screen that no longer existed. *"Calories and water"* over a row of **three** tiles; a Nutrition step that never mentioned the steps row that had just moved onto it; a settings step listing everything behind the gear except the two things the gear had gained.

- [x] AND ONE LOOSE END I HAD TIED BADLY MYSELF: the welcome step now promises the tour is *"in the settings menu"*, and the settings step never pointed at it. A promise that dead-ends one screen later.

- [x] EACH CLAIM IS NOW TIED TO THE SOURCE FACT IT DEPENDS ON — not *"the copy says steps"* but *"the copy says steps **if** the tile renders steps"*. So the check fails when the app changes, which is the only moment it matters. Read from `TOUR_STEPS` as **data**, never by grepping the file: two copy checks in this repo have already gone red on their own explanatory comments. Four mutations bite, one per claim.

- [x] **PACKAGES 1 + 2 — HOME RESTRUCTURED, AND ONE FACT NOW HAS ONE OWNER.** Home's order is what's next → morning check-in → progress: date + streak chip, week-strip label, strip, hero, coach bubble, progress, three tiles, weigh-in. Gone: the duplicate `Streak: N days`, the bottom phase line, the standalone consistency line, `6 weigh-ins` (kept at **one**, where the app must admit the trend isn't real), the tomorrow line, and the second `Nutrition ›`.

- [x] **THE SESSION AT A GLANCE, from data that was already there and never shown.** `Prone Y-T Raises · Trap Bar Deadlift · Chest Dips · +3 more` became `6 exercises · ~52 min · trap bar from 92.5 kg`. The minutes come from **the same `estimateDaySeconds` the plan's time cap and `test:audit` use**, so Home cannot disagree with the plan it is describing.

- [ ] **DELIBERATE DEVIATION:** the handoff specifies `bench 92.5 kg **next**` in progress. The app knows the session's heaviest lift but **not the running order**, so "next" would be a claim it cannot support — the same class of invention as a fabricated weight. The figure ships without the word.

- [x] **STEPS MOVED TO NUTRITION.** `steps-target.ts` had already decided it: the step target derives from the same `activity_level` as the calorie target's PAL multipliers, deliberately, so the two "never disagree about who is more active". Home keeps a read-only tile. **Second deviation:** the handoff sketches an inline `edit` for the target, but `daily_step_target` is a profile column with no setter on that path, and a second place to change one number is how two surfaces come to disagree — the row says where the override lives instead.

- [x] **THE WEEK STRIP IS NOW TWO THINGS FROM ONE VOCABULARY.** Home's is the RECORD (26px, no handler, no cursor, not a button); Exercise's is the NAVIGATOR (38px, tap to peek). `src/lib/week-glyphs.ts` is the single source, so a mark cannot come to mean two things — gated in both directions, and `test:coach-promises` was re-pointed at the shared module rather than the file it used to live in.

- [x] Coach bubble replaces the ⚡ caption: 26px avatar matching chat's, the tip and `whatsLeftLine` as one block, and reply chips **keyed to the rule that produced the tip** — `coach-tips.ts` always carried a `key`, `selectCoachTip` just threw it away. Chips **fill the composer and do not send**: a chip that fires a request the moment it is touched turns a suggestion into a command.

- [x] Tools joined the visual system — hairline tab underline instead of a filled 3-up, grocery off bordered `Card`s, 44px stopwatch, and the two lines the tab was missing: *"Your rest timer runs itself inside a session"* and where the grocery list comes from. VISION-ARCHITECTURE gains **§5.1a** with the ownership rule, and §5.1's "owns no number" is amended to name the weigh-in exception rather than left broken. `test:tab-ownership`, four mutations biting.

- [x] **PACKAGE 3 — APPEARANCE. THE BUG FIRST: there was no way back to your theme's own accent.** `AccentOverride` allowed `'theme'` and `DEFAULT_APPEARANCE.accent` **was** `'theme'`, but `ACCENT_OPTIONS` listed only the five hues — so tapping any accent was permanent short of clearing localStorage. A state the type allowed and no control could reach. **"Match theme"** is now the first chip, default-selected, its swatch showing the *current* theme's accent so it moves when the theme does.

- [x] **DAYLIGHT — the first light canvas**, plus retuned Ember and Field. Field mattered most: its old `#D6DF6B` sat in the **same hue family as its own canvas**, so the one element that must read as "tap this" was the closest thing to the background. New accent set (mint/coral/violet/sky/lime) with stored values migrated — `orange→coral`, `yellowgreen→lime`, `blue→sky`, `purple→violet`, anything unreadable → `theme` rather than a guessed hue.

- [x] A LIGHT CANVAS CHANGES TWO THINGS, BY RULE NOT BY COMBINATION. `data-canvas="light"` drives both the accent's dark step and the glow clamp, so a future light theme inherits both **without a line of CSS**. Glow is clamped at *resolve* time only — pick Daylight and Full becomes Subtle, switch back and Full returns, because the preference was never overwritten.

- [x] **THE SHEET NOW SHOWS YOU THE CHOICE BEFORE YOU MAKE IT.** 90 combinations were picked from inside a dialog covering the app it repaints. A live Home preview sits above the controls, painted from explicit theme values — necessary because the sheet must render a theme it is **not wearing**, and custom properties cannot be read from a theme that was never applied. `appearance-palette.ts` duplicates `index.css` for that reason and a gate asserts the two agree.

- [x] **THE HANDOFF'S CONTRAST EXAMPLE IS WRONG, AND DAYLIGHT TRIPPED ITS OWN WARNING — now fixed on Ashley's ruling (`#008C72`).** Its verification step says the guard fires on *"Field + Lime … 2.4:1"*. Measured: **Field + Lime is 13.9:1** — one of the highest-contrast pairs in the set, and high *because* of the retune the same document specifies. Swept all 30 combinations: exactly **two** fall under 3:1, both **Daylight + deep mint at 2.7:1** — which is Daylight's own default. So a user picking the new theme sees an amber warning about the theme's shipped colour. `#008C72` (its existing `--theme-primary-2`) would measure ~3.5:1 and clear it. **Not changed unilaterally** — it is a token the handoff specifies. Ashley's call.

- [x] **THE QUESTION-BESIDE-THE-BUTTON FIX ONLY COVERED ONE OF TWO PATHS, AND ASHLEY PHOTOGRAPHED THE OTHER.** The review opens by two independent routes: the model calling `complete_onboarding` (swept) and **the client's own safety net**, an effect that fires the moment `readyToGenerate` flips whether or not the model called anything (**not swept**). That net exists because the conversation once went silent on the final answer with no way forward, so it can never be removed — it simply had no sweep of its own. Same *"built in two halves"* defect this repo keeps producing.

- [x] IT NEEDED ITS OWN ENTRY POINT, not a second call to the same function: the net holds the **whole transcript**, and sweeping that would rewrite questions answered ten turns ago into a wall of identical closing lines. `closeOutTrailingQuestions` cuts at the last user message and sweeps only the tail. Gated with a six-message transcript that proves the trailing question closes, the two earlier ones don't, receipts survive, and nothing is lost or duplicated — plus the no-user-turn-yet edge case.

- [x] **"SKIP ANYTIME; I'LL KEEP YOUR PLACE."** True until the previous commit, false the moment Skip started ending the tour — a promise broken **by fixing a bug**, in the app's own voice, against VISION.md's *"never claims a capability it doesn't have"*. Copy and behaviour live in different files and no check joined them. Now reads *"it's in the settings menu whenever you want it"*, and a gate reads `TOUR_STEPS` as **data** rather than grepping the source — the first version went red on its **own comment**, which quotes the retired promise.

- [x] AND THE COPY LIVED IN A DEAD TWIN. `app-tour-steps.ts` held **two byte-identical 2,411-character step arrays** — an unexported `STEPS` and the live `TOUR_STEPS`. Nothing referenced the first, and being first in the file it is the one you would naturally edit: the fix would have read as done and changed **nothing on screen**. Deleted, with a gate asserting exactly one array survives. Four mutations bite across both files.

- [x] **"THE SKIP TOUR DOESN'T ACTUALLY SKIP IT."** Ashley, with screenshots. `skip()` wrote the current step and moved to `'skipped'` — which renders the *"Resume the tour · 1 of 10"* pill — and the **only** route to `'done'` was reaching the last of ten stops. So nothing a person could do would dismiss it, and the pill sat over the Weigh-in row **covering the number**. Skip now does exactly what finishing does.

- [x] AND THE PERMANENCE IS ONLY SAFE BECAUSE OF THE SECOND HALF. Nothing anywhere in the app could restart a finished tour — no Settings row, no reset, nothing reading `fitplan_tour_v1` except the tour itself — so Skip-means-gone would have meant **one mistaken tap destroys it forever**. Ashley's ruling was gone-with-a-way-back: a **Replay the tour** row now sits in the settings menu. Both halves are gated together, deliberately.

- [x] THE RESUME PILL IS KEPT, for the one case it is right for: the app was closed part-way through, where offering to pick up is useful rather than nagging. `test:app-tour` §6, **five mutations biting** — and the sixth found a hole in this gate rather than in the app: the resume check first grepped `status === 'skipped'` and `ResumePill` as two separate strings, and stayed **green** when the whole branch was replaced by `if (false)`, because that comparison also appears in the `stepIndex` expression above. Rewritten to match the render branch itself.

- [x] **REHAB FOR ANKLES, WRISTS AND ELBOWS — 0 of 576 TRAINING DAYS → 576 of 576.** VISION.md already required it (*"an injury should produce a plan that actively rehabilitates it, not one that merely avoids the joint"*) and four of eight injuries did not meet it. A trainee with bad **wrists** lost **21 of 149** exercises and got back nothing; ankles 13, elbows 10. Nine primer-tier entries — bands, isometrics, mobility. Verified **per equipment tier**, so it holds at home and not only in a full gym.

- [x] NOTHING BEARS WEIGHT ON THE INJURED JOINT, and that is a safety property rather than a style choice: `isContraindicatedFor` returns false whenever `isIndicatedFor` is true, so marking a movement indicated **forces it past the contraindication filter** — the one filter protecting these people. A loaded calf raise (already contraindicated for ankles) or a hands-on-the-floor wrist drill would land on exactly the trainee it is wrong for.

- [x] **THE NECK IS OUT OF SCOPE AND THAT IS NOW ASSERTED, NOT MERELY LEFT ALONE.** Ashley's ruling: *"my neck bothers me"* spans a stiff desk neck and a nerve problem that must not be loaded, and the app cannot tell which — so per VISION.md's *"never claims a capability it doesn't have"* it says so instead of prescribing. Two gates go red if neck rehab ever appears, and both say to **ask her** rather than update the test.

- [x] A LATENT BUG THE WORK EXPOSED: `assignSetsRepsFromConfig` returned `"5"` reps for **every** primer, before the `prescription_type` dispatch — invisible for as long as all 25 primers were rep-based. The first two time-based ones came out prescribed **2x5 for a movement you hold**, which `test:audit` caught **1,459 times**. Fixed at the choke point; provably inert for the existing 25.

- [x] AND A REGRESSION I INTRODUCED, THEN FOUND BY MEASURING RATHER THAN REASONING. Giving the nine the obvious `primer_pattern_affinity` put one of them on **40.1% of UNINJURED training days** (231/576). Nobody asked for everyone's warm-up to change. Dropping the affinity took it to **0/576** with the guarantee untouched — `pickRehabMovement` never consults affinity — and that zero is now gated.

- [x] **"SHORTEN THE REHAB SET WHEN A SESSION WOULD OVERRUN"** — Ashley's second ruling. Rehab was **doubly** untouchable (a required slot *and* a primer, and primers were excluded from trimming outright), so a wrist-injured trainee on bodyweight / 30-45 / bodybuilding ran **43min against a 37min budget**. The budget pass may now take a rehab primer from two sets to one, **never below** — the guarantee is presence, not volume. Accessories still give first: trimming a squat to protect two sets of wrist circles would be the wrong way round. `test:audit` back to **13,967 / 0 failed**, its exact baseline.

- [ ] **NOT GATED: which exercise gives way first.** Mutating the trim order so rehab is sacrificed *before* accessories leaves `test:audit` green — it measures session length, not which movement paid for it. The ordering is a judgment call no check currently sees.

- [x] **A QUESTION AND A GENERATE BUTTON, SIDE BY SIDE.** Ashley: *"the questions should be finished by the time the generate plan button appears."* The coach asked *"how much time do you want to spend cooking?"* directly above **Generate My Plan**, so there was no way to tell whether onboarding was over. **Neither half was misbehaving** — that is why it needed a gate, not just a fix. She had answered `dislikedFoods`, the last slot that can block, so `complete_onboarding` was correctly accepted and the review correctly opened; the model meanwhile spent its reply text on `cookingTime`.

- [x] THE CAUSE WAS A CATALOG THAT OVERSTATED ITSELF. The slot catalog is introduced to the model as *"the answers you need"*, and `required` was its only qualifier — but `required: false` does not mean *skippable*: **age and injuries are optional and must still be asked**. Four slots (`cookingTime`, `includeSnacks`, `favoriteCuisines`, `breakfastStyle`) were long ago demoted so they can never delay anyone, and **nothing told the model that**. They now carry `neverBlocks`, render as `BONUS`, and the prompt states that finishing and asking are mutually exclusive — naming the measured incident and the same-turn case that actually fired.

- [x] AND A DETERMINISTIC HALF, because a prompt is advisory. `closeOutOpenQuestions` runs once the review opens and strips every question from that turn — the trailing one becomes the app's own closing line, a second is dropped rather than repeating it, and any chip card offered alongside is removed. **Ashley's ruling was the timing, not the slots**: the coach may still raise cooking time mid-conversation, never once the essentials are done. `test:no-question-beside-generate`, **six mutations biting** — including one proving the gate catches the sweep existing but never being called.

- [ ] **NEEDS `npm run deploy:functions:prod -- onboarding-chat`** for the catalog marker and the prompt rule. The client sweep ships with the Vercel push and is the half that holds regardless.

- [x] **FEET AND STONE ARE ACCEPTED NOW, AND THE APP SAYS WHAT IT STORED.** Ashley's ruling. `5'10` → **178cm**, `13st 2` → **83.5kg**, with the receipt reading `(from 5'10")` — because a silent conversion is only safe if the result is visible next to what was typed, while it can still be corrected. Formats: `178`, `178cm`, `1.78m`, `5'10"`, `5ft10`, `5 foot 10`, `70in`; `87kg`, `180lb`, `13st`, `13 stone 2`.

- [x] A CONVERSION IS ONLY EVER APPLIED WHEN THE INPUT NAMES ITS UNIT. A bare `70` stays 70 and fails the 100–250 bound exactly as before — guessing whether it meant centimetres or inches would be **the same offence as the `100, 150` lift mix-up this session started with**, one field over. `5'13"` and `13st 15lb` are refused rather than silently rolled into `6'1"` and `14st 1lb`.

- [x] **THE PLAN PREDICTED NONE OF THE THREE THINGS ACTUALLY IN THE WAY.** Writing the parser and its 40-case table proved nothing about whether anyone could use it: **(1)** the field was `type="number"`, which physically discards an apostrophe, so `5'10` could never have reached the converter; **(2)** the card's own `isOk` validated the RAW string against `isNumberIn(100,250)` and rejected it with "Give a number between 100 and 250" before the converter was consulted; **(3)** the label still said `cm`, so nothing told anyone the other form was allowed. **Every unit test passed the whole time.** Only driving the real field in Chromium found any of it.

- [x] WHY IT WAS WORTH THE CARE: `height_cm` and `weight_kg` feed `resolveBodyMetrics` → `computeBMR` → `computeStaticTDEE` (every macro target) **and** the standards table that sets starting loads. A misread height is a wrong calorie target and a wrong weight on the bar — and unlike a *missing* metric, which `load_source: 'assumed_body'` hedges every load against, **nothing hedges a wrong one**. Four mutations bite: promoting a bare number, dropping the read-back, restoring `type="number"` (8 red), and validating the raw string again (4 red).

- [x] **THE COMPOSER SAID "YOUR AGE…" UNDER A CARD ASKING THREE THINGS — WITH THE AGE ALREADY FILLED IN.** A grouped numeric card (age / height / weight) put its FIRST member's hint in the box, and the first member is the likeliest to be the one already answered. The hint now names what is still **outstanding**, in the card's own order, and narrows as boxes are filled: with 37 entered it reads *"height and weight…"*.

- [x] AND THE FIX GENERALISED, which the harness caught rather than me: the squat/bench/deadlift card is a group too, so its fixture's expected *"Your squat, in kg…"* **moved** to *"squat, bench and deadlift…"*. Naming one lift under a card showing three boxes was the same defect one screen over. Six composer states are now covered.

- [x] **A LIFT WEIGHT IS NEVER AGAIN ASSIGNED TO A LIFT THE USER DIDN'T NAME.** Ashley's ruling: *"show all three labelled boxes."* **And that card already existed** — `NUMERIC_GROUPS` has had the squat/bench/deadlift trio all along and `SlotNumericCard` renders the group with every field labelled. This was never a missing feature; the model bypassed it, asked in prose, and recorded two `set_slot` calls off the order **its own sentence** happened to use.

- [x] **WHICH LAYER DID IT WAS ESTABLISHED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WRITTEN**, as the plan required: the model. The client's typed-answer path (`tryExactLabelMatch`) has branches for `single` and `multi` only — it never records a typed numeric at all, so it could not have been the one. The last three bugs in this flow were each a different layer than the obvious one, which is why this is checked rather than assumed.

- [x] FIXED IN TWO LAYERS, because a prompt is advisory and these slots write `load_source: 'known_weight'` — the app's most-trusted basis, outranking its own estimate and skipping the starting-light hedge. **The prompt** forbids the guess, names the measured incident, and points at the grouped card by slot key. **A client guard** in `executeActions` refuses a lift write when the turn records more than one lift and the user's own text never named it, showing the labelled boxes instead — and it reads **what the user typed, never the question**, which is the entire distinction the bug turned on. Exercised: `"100, 150"` refuses both, `"squat 100, bench 150"` allows both, one lift with one number allows, `age` untouched. Four mutations bite.

- [ ] **NEEDS `npm run deploy:functions:prod -- onboarding-chat`** for the prompt half. The client guard ships with the Vercel push and is the half that cannot be talked out of.

- [x] **"THE APP GOT COMPLETELY STUCK AND I HAD TO REFRESH" — IT WASN'T STUCK, IT LOOKED STUCK.** The request has a 45s abort and a `finally` that always clears `busy`, so it does recover. But **three animated dots that never change for three-quarters of a minute is indistinguishable from frozen**, and refreshing is the rational response. After 9 seconds the coach now says so: *"Still thinking — this one's taking a moment. Nothing's lost; it'll land or I'll say so."* Deliberately NOT a shorter abort — cutting off a reply that would have arrived at 20s trades a cosmetic problem for a real one.

- [ ] **`100, 150` BECAME SQUAT 100 AND BENCH 150, INFERRED FROM THE ORDER THE QUESTION LISTED THEM IN.** Ashley asked for exactly this to be a confirmation, not a presumption. It matters more than it looks: those slots set `load_source: 'known_weight'`, the app's **most-trusted** basis — it outranks the estimate and skips the starting-light hedge — so a mis-assigned number produces a **confident** wrong load for a whole mesocycle. Same family as "NEVER INVENT REPS OR SETS", one level up: those rules govern **values**, and here the **assignment of a value to a field** was invented, which no rule covers. `docs/PLAN-two-numbers-three-lifts.md` — **plan only, not built**; load prescription gets a plan first, and which layer decided it (model or client) is genuinely unknown.

- [x] **NO QUICK-REPLY BUTTONS ON THE QUESTION AFTER A CORRECTION.** ~~Most likely not a bug at all — very probably the undeployed `onboarding-chat`.~~ **That call was wrong, and it is why this survived to be reported again.** It reasoned from the prompt rule being present in the repo and never looked at the client, which dropped `present_slot` for **any** already-answered slot before the function's answer could matter — so no deploy could ever have fixed it. Ashley deployed `onboarding-chat` to production on 29 Aug and hit the symptom again the next day, which is the evidence. Fixed client-side 30 Aug; the lesson is that "needs a deploy first" is a hypothesis, and this one was recorded as a reason **not to look**.

- [x] **THE COMPOSER SAID "WHICH DAYS?" UNDER A QUESTION ABOUT SQUAT, BENCH AND DEADLIFT** — the third time this placeholder has described a different question than the one on screen. **Two independent faults**, either of which alone would have caused it.

- [x] **FAULT 1: SIX OF 27 SLOTS SHIPPED WITH NO `inputHint` AT ALL** — all three lift questions, plus snacks, cuisines and breakfast. So even when the app worked out correctly which question was live, it had nothing to show and fell through. Now every slot has one, and a gate fails if a seventh appears (or if a hint grows too long for the box).

- [x] **FAULT 2: THE FALLBACK IS A GUESS, AND IT WAS OVERRIDING A QUESTION ON SCREEN.** When no slot could be identified, the composer named "the canonical next open slot" — and that helper **cannot see the lift slots at all** (they are `requiredIf`-conditional), so it skipped them and landed on `trainingDays`. The guess is still right when nothing is pending — after an answered card the next canonical question genuinely is next — but when the coach has just asked something unmappable, **"Say anything…" is true and naming a different question is not**.

- [x] AND A THIRD BUG FOUND WHILE FIXING THOSE: **`asksSlot` never survived a reload.** `toDraftMessages` mapped five fields and silently dropped it, so a question asked WITHOUT a card — the opener's "what should I call you?" is the built-in one — lost that fact on every refresh and the composer went blind. The `DraftMessage` type had always declared the field; only the mapper dropped it.

- [x] MY FIRST FIX PASSED LIVE AND FAILED IN THE HARNESS, which is the useful direction. On resume the app appends a **RESUME_BANNER** ("Welcome back — picking up right where we left off"), so "the last assistant message" was the banner, not the question behind it. Receipts are assistant lines too. Both are now skipped when looking for what was asked.

- [x] AND THE HARNESS WAS READING THE WRONG BOX: `input[placeholder]` matched the **numeric card's own field**, which renders ABOVE the composer, so it reported `1–500` as the composer's placeholder. It targets `.ob-composer-fade` now. Two new fixtures cover exactly Ashley's case (a freehand question) and its opposite (the same question WITH its card). **A fourth check was satisfied by its own comment** — the mapper check matched the prose beside it — so comments are stripped before that one runs. Five mutations bite.

- [x] **"NEW PLAN" DROPPED STRAIGHT ONTO THE REVIEW CARD WITH EVERY OLD ANSWER FILLED IN.** Reported live: tapping reset went to "Generate My Plan" — age, height, weight, sex, meals, all still there — instead of a fresh conversation. `handleReset` wiped the profile and six caches but **not `fitplan_onboarding_draft`**, which is a single GLOBAL key rather than a per-profile one. The next run restored a draft with every slot confirmed, so `readyToGenerate` was true on mount and the review opened immediately. Completion already cleared the draft; **reset was the only path that didn't**.

- [x] SO THE FIX IS A LIST, NOT A LINE. The bug isn't "we forgot this key", it's that **nothing said which keys a reset owns**. `test:reset-clears-draft` now classifies **every global localStorage key in `src/`** as cleared-or-deliberately-kept with a stated reason, and a new global key fails the gate until someone decides which it is. Offline queues (set-log, water, cardio, grocery, meal-event) are kept on purpose — each entry carries its own profile id and can still sync, and dropping them would silently delete logged work.

- [x] AND THE LIST IMMEDIATELY FOUND A SECOND LEAK: **`fitplan_last_tab`** also survived, so the tab the OLD profile was last on would greet the new one after onboarding. Same shape, one screen over. Now cleared.

- [x] **THE GATE FOUND A BUG IN ITSELF FIRST.** Its first run reported set-log keys living in the cardio and grocery stores — because it resolved `const NAME = 'literal'` into ONE shared map, and **five different stores each declare a `PENDING_KEY`** (three a `DEAD_LETTER_KEY`), so the last file read won. Constants are resolved per file now. My own earlier hand audit had made exactly the same mistake and would have shipped a wrong list of "safe" keys. Four mutations bite: dropping the draft clear, dropping the last-tab clear, wrongly clearing an offline queue (the over-fire control), and adding a new global key nobody classified.

- [x] **A NODE WARNING PRINTED ITSELF ON TOP OF THE `yes-production` PROMPT**, during a real production migration push. Harmless in itself (DEP0190, `shell: true` with an args array), but it landed on the single most consequential prompt in the repo — the one whose entire purpose is that reaching production costs a deliberate, legible moment. Two fixes: the Windows branch now builds one command string instead of passing an args array, which is the documented way to silence DEP0190 and is exactly as safe here because every argument is already validated against `/^[a-z0-9-]+$/` or is a literal `--flag`; and the prompt gets a leading newline so async chatter can never share its line again. Off Windows it now passes no shell at all, which is stricter than before. **POSIX path verified here; the Windows path cannot be run from the sandbox and is unverified until Ashley's next push.**

- [x] **THE ONBOARDING QUICK REPLIES ARE STACKED ROWS NOW — option 2b from Ashley's design handoff.** Label + description, hairline dividers, mint tint with a 2px left rail and a check on the selected row, no emoji, no scale animation. The seven training days collapse to a 7-across segmented strip; label-only sets over seven (dietary 22, cuisines 10, injuries 8) become wrapping pills. Measured at 390px: **nothing overflows**, and dietary — the worst case — is **634px, down from 771px**.

- [x] THE HANDOFF NAMED TWO TOKENS THAT DO NOT EXIST, and the real ones were used instead. `#C2BCE8` is **`--text-tertiary`**, not `--foreground-dim` (which is in no theme block); and the hover value `rgba(69,60,142,.28)` is **`--surface-raised`**, not `--accent` (`--accent` is `#2E2662`). Both real tokens are defined in all four themes, so the rows theme correctly rather than being right only on Nightshift.

- [x] **THE SHAPE RULE AS WRITTEN MIS-ROUTED ONE QUESTION.** The table says Strip is "no descriptions, ≤ 7 options with short labels" — but `gender` has **two** options and no descriptions, so it would have rendered a two-answer question as a seven-across day grid. The handoff's own prose lists sex under Rows. The strip now tests for **exactly seven**, `gender` falls through to rows, and the gate asserts that with the reasoning attached so it cannot be "simplified" back.

- [x] THE TYPED-TEXT LANDMINE THE HANDOFF FLAGGED WAS ALREADY SAFE, verified rather than assumed: `sameText` (`ConversationalOnboarding.tsx:179`) lowercases both sides, so the "user typed exactly the chip label" shortcut survives the relabel. **92 option `value`s are byte-identical before and after** — dumped either side of the change rather than eyeballed in a diff, because they are persisted and matched against stored data.

- [x] SENTENCE CASE WENT FURTHER THAN THE HANDOFF'S LIST, flagged rather than done quietly: its examples split on spaces and slashes, so the hyphenated allergen pills ("Dairy-Free", "Low-Carb") were not named. The stated rule is "all `label` fields", so they went too — **Low-FODMAP keeps its acronym**.

- [x] **TWO HARNESS BUGS FOUND EN ROUTE, BOTH SILENTLY PASSING.** `measure.mjs` picked *the first `.html` found by walking dist* — and the moment `composer.html` joined that bundle earlier today it sorted first, so the driver has been measuring **the wrong page**, printing "screens that overflow: none" against a document containing no questions at all. Pinned to its own page, and an empty run now **fails** instead of reporting none. Separately: `captureScreenshot`'s clip is in PAGE coordinates, not viewport, and needs `captureBeyondViewport` for anything below the fold — two blank screenshots before that landed.

- [x] THE GATE MOVED WITH THE DESIGN RATHER THAN BEING LOOSENED. The icon rule got **stronger** — from "an icon repeated on every option is not rendered" to "the chips render no emoji at all, and the row component has no icon prop to pass one through". The stranded-last-card rule is retired *and its retirement asserted* (`grid-cols-2` and `col-span-2` must both be gone, so the two-column grid cannot creep back). New: all three shapes reachable, the strip requires exactly seven, and a multi-select footer reads the picks back in words.

- [x] **26 OF 91 CONTROLS WERE TOO SMALL TO TAP RELIABLY. NOW 4, AND NOTHING MOVED ON SCREEN.** Found by asking the browser the question a thumb asks — *if I land 22px off centre, do I hit this?* — rather than by measuring boxes, because a bounding rect cannot see a pseudo-element hit-slop. Worst offenders: **"Save" on the weigh-in at 20px tall**, the **+250/+500 water buttons at 16px**, **"Add Set" at 24px**. Apple's minimum is 44pt, Android's 48dp.

- [x] THE BIGGEST LEVER WAS ONE FILE: **every `Button` size variant except `cta` is under 44px** (`h-9`=36, `h-8`=32, `h-6`=24, `size-10`=40). `hit-slop-44` on the variants fixed **26 → 17** at a stroke, app-wide, invisibly — it is an `::after` that expands the touch area and moves no layout. The rest were raw `<button>`s in Dashboard, NutritionDisplay, MacroSplitCard, ExerciseRow and ExerciseLine. **Dashboard and Nutrition are now completely clean.**

- [x] **I GOT THE MEASUREMENT WRONG TWICE AND BOTH CORRECTIONS MATTER MORE THAN THE FIX.** (1) The first run reported **38 of 93 and "15 stolen taps"** — `document.elementFromPoint` works in VIEWPORT coordinates and returns null outside it, so every control below the fold counted as both unreachable and stolen. The dashboard page is 1342px against an 844px viewport. Scrolling each control into view first: the real figure was **26**, and the stolen count was **zero**. (2) The bar itself was unachievable: demanding a full 44px reach in both axes fails for any two controls closer than 88px. Probed directly — the "Expand phase context" button's `::after` really is 44x44 and resolves above, below and left; 21px right lands on the next button. **A neighbour owning that point is arithmetic, not a defect.**

- [ ] **THE REMAINING 4 ARE ONE VISUAL QUESTION, NOT A MECHANICAL ONE, so they are not built.** Three are `<input>` elements — the two set-log number fields (28px) and the grocery add field (32px) — and an `<input>` **cannot have a `::after`**, so the invisible trick does not exist for them; the only fix is real height. The fourth is the button beside the grocery field. **Plus 9 more that only pass because a neighbour is pressed against them** (the SetGrid icon row at 28x28, the 14x14 phase-context caret, the 3d/7d/14d chips). Making those comfortable means a less dense set grid — what a user sees, so it is Ashley's call.

- [x] AND THE SWEEP TURNED UP **FOUR ICON-ONLY BUTTONS WITH NO ACCESSIBLE NAME AT ALL** — they announce as just "button" to a screen reader. Two are user-facing (grocery add, ProfileScreen add) and now carry `aria-label`; two are in `DevTestPage`. Found because the harness prints a control's label and two of them printed as `<button>`.

- [x] **THE MAIN CHAT HAD THE SAME BUG ASHLEY REPORTED, AND I HAD CALLED IT "FIXED BY PARITY" WITHOUT SEEING IT.** Now measured against the real component: with a 336px keyboard up, the composer rides to **top 416** while the chat Card is a fixed `h-[600px]` ending at **648** — so it covers **232px of the message list**, and the newest message, at 512-552, was **entirely behind it**. The scroller reserved a static `pb-24` (96px) that never grew. Onboarding grows its padding with the keyboard; this never did.

- [x] THE FIX MEASURES THE OVERLAP RATHER THAN DERIVING IT. Onboarding can use `insetPx + 112` because its scroller ends at the viewport bottom; the chat's ends at the **Card's** bottom, an unknown distance above that which depends on App.tsx's own page padding — a constant there would go stale the moment that padding changed. A `useLayoutEffect` reads the two boxes and pads by the real gap. No feedback loop: padding sits inside the border box, so growing it doesn't move the scroller's own rect. **Padding 96px → 244px; newest message 512-552 → 364-404, clearing the composer by 12px.** Keyboard-shut geometry is byte-identical.

- [x] AND THE PARITY `min-h-0` I ADDED EARLIER WAS UNNECESSARY — measured, the chat scroller was **already scrollable** (`scrollHeight 1410` vs `clientHeight 600`), because its `CardContent` parent carries `overflow-hidden`, which already zeroes a flex item's automatic minimum. Harmless, and now known rather than assumed. **This is the correction: I reported that fix as "applied by parity, unmeasured" and it turned out the thing I fixed there wasn't broken, while a different thing on the same screen was.**

- [x] **THE HARNESS COULD NOT SEE ANY OF IT UNTIL TWO HEADLESS FACTS WERE FIXED.** (1) `real.tsx`'s chat tab was a **stub div**, so the real chat had never been driven at all — `.tour-harness/chat.tsx` now mounts the real `ChatAssistant` inside App.tsx's shell **copied verbatim**, since the Card/composer collision depends entirely on where `<main>`'s padding puts the card. (2) A headless page is **not focused**, so `element.focus()` sets `activeElement` **without dispatching focus events** — measured, `focusin` fired **0 times** — and `useViewportInset` gates on a focused input, so the layout never entered the state under test. `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled` fixes it. Without that, this harness would have cheerfully reported everything fine.

- [x] MY FIRST HARNESS SEEDED THE CONVERSATION UNDER THE WRONG KEY — `fitplan_chat_cache_` with a `{ messages, savedAt }` wrapper; the real one is `chat_history_cache_` holding a **bare array**. The chat restored nothing and I measured an **empty thread**, which would have reported "the scroller doesn't need to scroll" as a finding about the app. Caught by checking whether the fixture had actually loaded before reading any number off it. `verify:chat-shell` now asserts the thread really overflows before it asserts anything else, and `test:composer-focus` §6 pins the copied wrapper to App.tsx's so it cannot drift.

- [x] **I CHECKED WHETHER THE ASSISTANCE LEAK HAD SIBLINGS. IT DOESN'T — AND THAT NEGATIVE RESULT IS NOW A GATE.** The leak was a carry-forward fallback (`load ? … : ex.<field>`) surviving a rotation that changed the slot's identity. **Four sibling fields use the identical shape** — `load_guidance`, `suggested_load`, `suggested_load_kg`, `load_source` — so a barbell lift rotating out for a bodyweight movement could have taken its weight with it. **Swept 74,628 exercises: it does not happen.** Assistance leaked precisely *because* `prescribeAssistance` sits OUTSIDE the load branch (its own comment says so) and had no equivalent guard.

- [x] THE INVARIANT WAS GENUINELY UNGATED, though — `isExternallyLoaded` appears in eight gate scripts and is used as a **filter** in every one, never as an assertion. "A movement with nothing to load carries no kg number" is now checked over the sweep: **11,478 of 19,436 exercises have nothing to load**, so it is nowhere near vacuous, and forcing the fallback to carry makes it fail naming Band Face Pulls, Band Pull-Aparts, Prone Y-T Raises and Standing Band Hip Abduction at 42.5kg.

- [x] **THE COACH NOW GETS EVERY PRESCRIBED NUMBER THE APP HOLDS, AND A GATE SWEEPS 19,436 EXERCISES TO PROVE IT.** The four gaps a review found in `f516748` are closed: **machine assistance** (an assisted pull-up reached the coach as `@ Bodyweight` — a phrase the prompt teaches it to read as *no external load*, while `AssistanceChip` showed "35kg assist" one screen away), the **`assumed_body` hedge**, **primers** quoted as if `Light` were a weight, and **days with no gym session** arriving as a dangling `Sunday: Active recovery - `. Tempo and intensity go too — for a rep-based lift with no weight to add, the tempo IS the prescription, and those are exactly the movements whose load clause reads `Bodyweight`.

- [x] **AND THE GATE'S OWN TEETH CHECK FOUND A BUG NOBODY WAS LOOKING FOR: 84 PRESCRIPTIONS GAVE MACHINE ASSISTANCE TO EXERCISES WITH NO MACHINE.** Asserting that `suggested_assistance_kg` actually *occurs* in the sweep — so its check can't be vacuously true — turned up **Kneeling Band Lat Pulldown (64)** and **Lat Pulldown (20)** carrying a counterweight. Cause: a slot's identity changes week to week, and `suggested_assistance_kg: assistance ? … : ex.suggested_assistance_kg` carried the **previous** exercise's number onto the new one when a rotation swapped Pull-Ups (Assisted) out. `suggested_added_load_kg` on the very next line already nulls instead of carrying; this line was the one that didn't follow the rule. **Worse than a stray number**: `AssistanceChip` renders it as *"40kg assist · less over time = stronger"* — an inverted progress cue on a lift where more weight IS the progress.

- [x] DECIDED WITHOUT ASKING, on Ashley's "fix it all in order", and recorded because it was one of the two open questions: an `assumed_body` load now reaches the coach as `~40kg [STARTING LIGHT — no body details, deliberately low, not a target]`, my recommended option (a). It costs one clause, it keeps chat and the Exercise tab saying the same thing about the same number, and the whole reason `load_source` exists is that a guessed load must never be presented as a known one. The over-fire control is gated too — a `known_weight` or `estimate` load carries **no** hedge, or the hedge would mean nothing.

- [x] ONE DELIBERATE DEVIATION FROM THE PLAN, flagged rather than quietly taken. The plan said *send the progressed added load rather than the stored one*. `getAddedLoadProgression` is an async per-exercise DB read and `buildContext` is synchronous, so doing it properly means a progression query on every chat turn. Instead the coach is **told** the number is the plan's base, that the Exercise tab may show a higher one, and that **the screen is right** if they disagree. That removes the false confidence without duplicating the progression engine into the chat path.

- [x] `test:coach-plan-context` is new and registered. It sweeps real generated plans and asserts that **if an exercise carries a prescribed number in any field, that number appears in what the coach is sent** — the shape that would have caught the assisted pull-up on day one, where the old gate checked a single hand-built object with no load fields at all. Eight mutations bite, including an over-fire control and the leaky fallback (which fails naming Kneeling Band Lat Pulldown and Lat Pulldown by name and week).

- [ ] **THE COMMIT THAT FIXED THE MISSING WEIGHT MISSED FOUR MORE NUMBERS, AND ONE OF THEM STATES SOMETHING FALSE.** A review of `f516748` found the same omission it fixed still live in `suggested_assistance_kg`, the progressed added load, primers, and days with no exercises — each **verified by running the builder**. An assisted pull-up with **35kg of assistance** reaches the coach as `@ Bodyweight`, which the new prompt teaches it to read as *no external load* — worse than sending nothing, and the exact shape of the incident this thread started with. `docs/PLAN-the-coach-still-cannot-see-four-numbers.md`. **Plan only, not built** — load prescription gets a plan first, and the last change here skipped that, which is how these got through.

- [x] TWO HONESTY DEFECTS IN THE PROMPT I WROTE TODAY, FIXED IMMEDIATELY because they are corrections to my own new text rather than new behaviour. **(1)** It said *"the never-invent rule below"* — that rule is **137 lines above**, so the one sentence separating "quote a prescription" from "log a prescription" sent the model looking the wrong way. Now asserted **by position**, not by wording, so it cannot go stale if either block moves. **(2)** It claimed *"you HAVE the prescribed weights"* **unconditionally** — but `buildCoachExerciseSummary({ days: [] })` returns `""`, and the summary carries **only the active week** while Ashley's original question was about a **past** one. Claiming data you were not given is the same failure as denying data you were, pointing the other way. Both limits are now stated and gated; four mutations bite.

- [x] **THE KEYBOARD CLOSED AFTER EVERY ONBOARDING ANSWER, AND THE AUTO-SCROLL HAD BEEN A NO-OP THE WHOLE TIME.** Ashley: *"each time I enter an input and hit send the keyboard closes and I have to reopen it for the next message and the conversation is not at the latest message and it cuts off the bottom so I have to scroll down which is incredibly frustrating."* Two independent causes, both **measured in Chromium against the real screen before anything was changed** (`npm run verify:keyboard`), not reasoned about.

- [x] **CAUSE 1, FOCUS: after tapping send, `document.activeElement` was `<body>`.** Nothing focused — which is a phone putting the keyboard away. A tap focuses the button, and emptying the box flips it to `disabled` in the same tick, so not even the button keeps it. **The input already knew this**: it carries a comment reading *"NOT `disabled` while busy: on a phone, disabling the focused input dismisses the keyboard, so the user had to re-tap the field on every single turn."* The lesson was learned, written down, and applied to one of the two focusable controls in the composer. The other one is this. Same shape found and fixed in the main chat's send **and mic** buttons.

- [x] **CAUSE 2, SCROLL: the scroll container did not scroll.** Measured at 390x844: the canvas was **875px tall in an 844px viewport**, and the scroller reported **`scrollHeight === clientHeight === 799`** — nothing to scroll, so every `scrollTo` in the auto-scroll block was a **no-op**. That block carries thirty lines of comment about ResizeObservers and why watching the message list was the wrong signal; all of it was correct and none of it could ever fire. What actually moved was the **page**, which nothing there controls, parking the newest message **11px underneath** the fixed composer. Cause: `min-h-[100dvh]` plus a flex child with the default `min-height:auto`, which refuses to shrink below its content. `h-[100dvh] overflow-hidden` + `min-h-0`. **Clearance −11px → +20px, and the scroller now scrolls (scrollTop 31).**

- [x] A THIRD FACT THE FILE ASSERTED IS FALSE, and it is why this was easy to miss: the comment above the padding reads *"100dvh above shrinks with the keyboard on Chrome Android."* It does not. Chrome 108 made **`resizes-visual` the default** — the on-screen keyboard resizes the visual viewport only, leaving the layout viewport and `100dvh` at full height ([Chrome for Developers](https://developer.chrome.com/blog/viewport-resize-behavior)). Verified against Chrome's own documentation rather than from memory. The JS inset compensation happens to cover it, so this is a wrong premise rather than a live second bug — corrected in place, and worth knowing before anyone trusts that comment again.

- [x] `test:composer-focus` holds all of it, and six mutations bite: removing the guard from either send button (1 each), removing `min-h-0` (1), reverting the canvas to `min-h` (2), switching the helper to `pointerdown` (1), and hard-disabling the input again (2). `onMouseDown` deliberately, **not** `onPointerDown` — on touch, `mousedown` is the compatibility event that assigns focus, and preventing it stops the focus change without swallowing the `click`.

- [x] TWO OF MY OWN CHECKS WERE WRONG BEFORE THE CODE WAS. The new gate first went red **on its own doc comment** (which contains the words "rather than onPointerDown:") — the third time in this repo a check has been satisfied or broken by prose ABOUT the thing it looks for. And `test:onboarding-style` sliced a **fixed 3400 characters** from `ob-composer-fade`; five added lines of comment pushed `size-[22px]` out of the window and turned a passing check red with the markup untouched. A gate that fails on comment length is measuring the wrong thing — the window now runs to end of file, and the check still bites when the icon size actually changes.

- [x] AND THE HARNESS ITSELF WAS MEASURING A PAGE NO USER LOADS: `.onb-harness/composer.html` had **no viewport meta tag**, so the first geometry run reported the composer at `top: 2029px` on an 844px screen. It reads the app's real meta tag now. The placeholder check it was built for never needed a viewport; the moment it was asked a layout question, it was wrong.

- [ ] **NOT MEASURED: the main chat's scroll geometry.** The focus fix there is by parity with the measured onboarding defect (identical construct, read directly), and `min-h-0` was added to its scroller on the same reasoning — but the tour harness's chat tab is a **stub**, not the real `ChatAssistant`, so nothing drove it. Mounting the real component there is what would close this.

- [x] **THE COACH SAID IT DIDN'T HAVE HER PRESCRIBED WEIGHTS. IT WAS TELLING THE TRUTH.** Ashley, mid-log: *"I don't actually have your past weights on hand to look up what was prescribed"* — with the Exercise tab one tap away reading **Deadlifts 72.5 kg SUGGESTED, S1/S2/S3 all 72.5kg**. `ChatAssistant`'s plan summary sent the day, the focus, the exercise name, sets, reps, rest and the selection note. **It never sent the load.** So the coach asked for a number the app had already computed, printed on the next screen, and withheld — and then apologised for not having it.

- [x] THE FIX SENDS THE FORMATTED STRING, NOT THE NUMBER. `suggested_load` (`~14kg per hand`), never `suggested_load_kg` (`14`) — the per-hand distinction is 47.8% of externally-loaded prescriptions and a bare 14 reads as a total. Measured across a 4-equipment x 3-experience x 4-goal sweep: **26,912 of 26,912 exercises now carry a load clause**, 4,956 (18.4%) of them a per-hand qualifier that a raw number would have lost. The before figure is 0 **by construction, not by sweep** — the old template literal contained no load token of any kind.

- [x] A RAMP SENDS EVERY SET, NOT THE TOP SET THREE TIMES. **923 of 26,912 prescriptions ramp** (`~35kg, ~40kg, ~42.5kg`), so *"I did the prescribed weights"* would otherwise have logged 42.5x3 — the same invented-number defect as filling reps from the prescription, one field over. Weighted bodyweight work sends its `+17.5kg added` too, which `suggested_load` alone ("Bodyweight") loses.

- [x] IT WAS AN INLINE TEMPLATE LITERAL, WHICH IS WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT — a gate can't call a string interpolation inside a component, and a regex over the source would have passed against the broken version, because the broken version was perfectly well-formed. It's `src/lib/chat-plan-context.ts` now and `test:log-correction` §6 **runs it and reads the output**. Six mutations bite: dropping the load turns 6 checks red, sending the raw kg 5, collapsing a ramp 2, dropping added load 1, hand-rolling the summary back into the component 2, softening the prompt rule 1.

- [x] AND A PROMPT HALF, because data alone doesn't stop the sentence being said again. `CURRENT EXERCISE PLAN` now states that the `@` clause **is** the prescribed weight, how to read per-hand/single-side/added/ramped, that the coach therefore has them, and that quoting a prescription is **never** logging it as done — the never-invent rule is untouched. It asks for confirmation (*"The plan had you at ~72.5kg — was that what you lifted?"*) rather than an open question.

- [ ] **NEEDS `npm run deploy:functions:prod -- chat-gemini` ON ASHLEY'S MACHINE.** The client half ships with the Vercel push and is the half that fixes the missing data. The prompt half is inert until deployed — and so is `NEVER ASK THE SAME QUESTION TWICE IN A ROW`, which already sits on `main` carrying **Ashley's exact case** as its recorded example ("What weight did you use for Deadlifts?" asked twice verbatim in her screenshot) and has never reached production.

- [x] **THE PROFILE FORGOT EIGHT ANSWERS ON EVERY RELOAD — FIXED, AND GATED SO A NINTH CAN'T.** `restoreSession` rebuilds the profile column by column, so anything missing from that list was `undefined` for the whole session however faithfully Postgres stored it. Audited by parsing every `fitness_profiles` column out of the migrations and diffing: **51 columns, 42 restored, 9 dropped, 8 with live consumers.** All eight were written correctly — nothing in the database was ever wrong, only the read-back.

- [x] WHAT EACH ONE COST A USER: the three implement ceilings and `load_ceilings_declined` meant **"I'm not sure" never stuck** — the button that exists to stop the asking didn't, and the function's own comment says *"a trainee who says 'I don't know what my dumbbells weigh' must be able to say it once"*. The three macro fields **silently moved a protein target** back to Balanced on every open, which is worse than re-asking because nothing tells you. `water_target_ml` reverted to 2000ml.

- [x] **THE ROOT CAUSE WAS A LOCAL INTERSECTION TYPE.** The ceilings were declared inside `load-prescription.ts` and `load-ceiling-prompt.ts` as `UserProfile & { max_dumbbell_kg?: ... }`, so TypeScript never required `restoreSession` to map them and never complained. They're on `UserProfile` now, and reverting that is caught **by the compiler** — a cast that adds fields the profile doesn't really have will always compile and can never be checked.

- [x] EXISTING PLANS REBUILD, on Ashley's ruling over waiting up to sixteen weeks for it to age out. `ceiling-reconcile.ts` detects **the violation, not a marker** — is any prescribed load above what this person said they own — which is self-correcting and safe to run every load. From the **active week forward only**: rewriting a load someone already trained against would change what their own logs are measured against. No new rebuild path; `rebuildAgainstProfile` already takes the profile and the profile now carries the ceiling. The coach says why, naming their own number back to them.

- [x] MEASURED, AND IN THE DIRECTION I ASSERTED BEFOREHAND: with a 24kg-per-hand ceiling, **8 of 130 dumbbell prescriptions move DOWN, 0 move up** — a ceiling is a cap, so a rise would have meant the fix was wrong. Someone whose dumbbells stop at 24kg was being prescribed **30kg Farmer's Walks**.

- [x] AND A SMALL LIE IN THE COACH'S PROMPT, found because my gate first called `training_time_preference` dead. It isn't dead — `chat-gemini` reads it — but the client never sends it, so the prompt printed a constant **"Training Time: morning"** directly beneath the real `Preferred Time: evening`. Both usages removed; the column is now genuinely unused and flagged for deletion rather than quietly mapped. The gate searches `supabase/functions/` too now: **a check that decides what is dead has to look everywhere the thing could be alive.**

- [x] **THE STEP TARGET IS EDITABLE NOW** — `daily_step_target` on `fitness_profiles` (migration `20260828140000`), nullable, no default. Set it and the ring measures against it; leave it and the band derived from activity level stands.
