The settings row sits directly under Activity level, which is what it overrides, and its placeholder shows the band that activity level produces — so an empty box reads as a considered default rather than a gap. `stepsTargetFor` takes the **profile**, not the two fields, so no caller can read one and forget the other.

- [x] **NEEDS `npm run db:push-both` ON ASHLEY'S MACHINE.** The column does not exist on TEST or PRODUCTION until that runs, and the app tolerates its absence (the derivation stands) — but nobody can set a target until it does.
  **APPLIED — VERIFIED 1 Sep 2026, and the verification is the point.** Ashley ran `npm run db:push-both`; it reported *"Remote database is up to date"* on TEST and PRODUCTION. That message alone proves nothing — it is identical whether the migration is already applied or the FILE IS ABSENT from the local checkout, and this repo has been misled by exactly that shape before. Checked properly: `20260828140000` is present in her checkout at `30f1594`, so for this column "up to date" is a genuine already-applied. Same for `20260831120000_add_deliberate_rest`.

- [x] **CLOSED BY `1226ba2`, and this line sat unticked afterwards** — `restoreSession` now maps all four ceiling fields (`App.tsx:344-347`), verified by reading the current file rather than by trusting this entry. Left in place because the diagnosis below is what the fix was built from. Original finding follows.
- [x] **THE APP FORGETS A LOAD CEILING YOU GAVE IT, ON EVERY RELOAD.** `App.tsx`'s `restoreSession` builds the profile **column by column**, and `max_dumbbell_kg`, `max_single_implement_kg`, `max_improvised_kg` and `load_ceilings_declined` are **not in that list**. `hasStatedCeiling` reads them off that restored object and returns `v != null`, so after any reload they are `undefined` → never stated → **the app asks again**. A trainee who said "my dumbbells go to 32kg", or who tapped "I'm not sure" to stop being asked, gets the same prompt next time they open the app. Not fixed here: load prescription gets a plan before a build. Found while adding `daily_step_target` and checking I was not about to repeat the same shape.

- [x] **A REAL PRE-EXISTING BUG, FOUND BY THE NEW SCORE ON ITS FIRST RENDER: days before the plan existed were counted as plan week 1.** `getActiveMesocycleWeek` clamps with `Math.max(0, elapsedDays)`, and `dashboard-data` looks back 35 days — so on a plan created today, **all 35 landed in "this plan week"**. The consistency score read *"0/19 planned sessions"* for a single week, which is what exposed it. It also let coach-tips' `perfect_adherence` announce *"Every planned session this week, done — 19 for 19"*, and skewed `session_pace`'s week-on-week comparison. Related to but not the same as the earlier "stop marking pre-plan days as missed" fix — that taught the STREAK to ignore them and left these aggregates behind.

- [x] **CONSISTENCY SCORE, NAMED FOR WHAT IT MEASURES.** Ashley asked for a readiness score "combining workout consistency, sleep/rest days, and nutrition compliance" — the app has **never asked how anyone slept**; `recovery_capacity` is one onboarding answer that never changes. Her call when the choice was put to her: build it from what's real and name it honestly. Sessions and protein days over the current plan week, ratios averaged (not raw counts, so 3/3 sessions can't drown out 1/6 protein days), components spelled out so a lone percentage can't read as a verdict, and **absent rather than 0%** when nothing measurable has happened yet. Renders `Consistency: 50% · 1/1 planned sessions · 0/2 protein days`.

- [x] WATER IS DELIBERATELY OUT OF THE SCORE — the app has it for today only, and seven more per-day reads would break `dashboard-data`'s stated read-cost discipline. A one-day component beside two week-long ones would also be quietly mis-weighted. **Better absent and explained than present and wrong.** The protein loop was restructured to collect days rather than break on the first miss, so the week count costs **zero extra reads**.

- [x] **THE WATER COACH TIP — ASHLEY'S OWN EXAMPLE — AND THE NaN I PUT IN A USER-FACING SENTENCE.** Written as `if (ctx.waterTargetMl <= 0) return null`, the rule ran on a context carrying no water fields at all (`undefined <= 0` is false), fell through to the arithmetic, and produced **"You're about NaNml behind on water for this time of day."** Caught by the existing coach-tip gate's "zero supporting data anywhere → null" check, which is exactly what that check is for. Every input is now checked for being a finite number, not just for its value.

- [x] THE TIP ITSELF IS PRO-RATA AGAINST A WAKING DAY (08:00–22:00, not midnight, or everyone is "behind" every morning), silent before 10am and after 10pm, silent under 250ml, and it **joins the same seeded daily rotation as every other rule rather than jumping the queue** — being behind on water does not outrank a new PR, and a rule that always won would be a daily nag. It is arithmetic on a target the app set and a log the user made, unlike the energy/readiness line in the same request, which would have needed sleep data that is never collected.

- [x] **QUICK REPLIES ARE BACK ON EVERY ONBOARDING QUESTION** — Ashley, reversing this morning's narrowing: *"ive changed my mind and I think actually the quick replies on the onboarding are better for all questions."* The four that had been exempted (do you know your lifts, male/female, how many meals, snacks yes/no) offer their options again — 2, 2, 3 and 2 respectively.

- [x] THE EXEMPTION MECHANISM WAS **REMOVED**, NOT LEFT TAGGING NOTHING. `obviousAnswer` is gone from the slot type, the four slots, the catalogue sent to the model, the client's `present_slot` guard and the prompt's `[NO CHIPS]` rule. A flag no slot carries still reads as meaningful to the next person and does nothing — the same dead-code-with-a-truthful-comment shape this log keeps recording.

- [x] THREE RULINGS ON ONE QUESTION, AND THE GATE NOW CARRIES THE HISTORY so a future session doesn't re-litigate it: **(1)** *"Always, pills or cards as fits"* — options had become invisible; **(2)** *"Some have obvious answers"* — four exempted; **(3)** *"better for all questions"* — back to always. `test:onboarding-chips` asserts the property plainly (a slot with a list offers it, 18 of them) and that no suppression mechanism exists in any of the four places it used to live. Bites: re-exempting one slot turns 2 red, restoring the client guard 1, dropping "every time" from the prompt 1.

- [x] **HALF THE WEIGHTS IN A PLAN SAID THE WRONG THING, AND NOW THEY DON'T.** `formatLoad()` has always produced `~14kg per hand` and stored it on every exercise as `suggested_load`; only `LoadChip` read it. `ExerciseLine` and `ExerciseRow` re-rendered the raw number with a hard-coded "kg", so **1126 of 2356 prescriptions (47.8%)** showed a per-hand number in the same format as a total, sitting directly beneath one — a 14kg RDL reading as a third of a 42.5kg squat when it is two-thirds. Wording is Ashley's: **"14kg per hand"**, chosen from six candidates all measured against the real column width.

- [x] THREE SURFACES, ONE UNIT, verified in Chromium at 390px: collapsed line `3×11-13 · ~14kg per hand`, expanded number `14` + `kg per hand`, logging header `Log weight · per hand`. A barbell lift is untouched at `~42.5kg`. **The logging column mattered most** — it doesn't just tell you a number, it asks you for one, and `exercise_set_logs` carries no unit of its own to catch a 2× mistake with. No prescribed number changed and no schema changed.

- [x] `test:load-display` holds it as **"nobody hand-rolls a kg string"**, not "these two files look right today" — this is the two-halves shape, so a gate pinned to the two files that were wrong would pass while a third surface repeated it. Bites on five mutations: reverting ExerciseLine (3 checks red), hard-coding kg in ExerciseRow (1), SetGrid re-deriving the unit itself (1), the header dropping the label (1), `formatLoad` losing the qualifier (1).

- [x] TWO OF MY OWN MISTAKES WHILE BUILDING IT. The gate fired on **the comment I had just written explaining the bug** — third time this session a check of mine was satisfiable by its own documentation; it strips block comments now, since the explanation lives in a JSX `{/* … */}`. And I reported the header "isn't getting the prop" after reading `<span>Weight</span>` off the page: there are **two** headers on screen, a page-wide search returns the first, and that one belongs to a bodyweight exercise where plain "Weight" is correct. Scoped to the expanded block it read `Log weight · per hand` the whole time.

- [x] **A CORRECTION I OWE: THE "DOES NOT OVER-FIRE" CONTROL I QUOTED THIS WEEK WAS A TAUTOLOGY.** `report-rehab-coverage` printed *"CONTROL — no injury flagged: 0/576 days"* and I reported that zero as evidence the rehab slot doesn't fire for uninjured people. It was evidence of nothing: it ran the sweep with an **empty flagged-joint set** and then counted movements indicated *for the flagged joints*. Nothing matches an empty set, so it read 0 whatever the plans contained — it would have read 0 with the slot firing on every day of every plan. **How I got it wrong:** I added that control in the same change as the hips/lower-back work, reused `sweep(injury)` for it by passing a fake injury code, and never asked what the reused function was actually comparing against. The number looked like a measurement because it came out of a sweep.

- [x] THE PROPERTY IS TRUE ANYWAY, for a better reason: `pickRehabMovement` returns null on an empty joint set before it looks at the pool (`exercise-plan.ts:1638`). Found while looking at a real rendered plan — an uninjured user's screen showed **Side-Lying Hip Abduction** and **Banded Terminal Knee Extension**, which contradicted the 0/576 on its face. They arrive through ordinary primer/accessory slots, which is by design; twelve joint-tagged movements do.

- [x] THE CONTROL NOW MEASURES SOMETHING THAT MOVES, **per joint**, because pooling all four joints gives 88.4% and makes the guarantee look worthless — an artefact, not a finding. Uninjured trainees already meet a **shoulder** movement on 49.3% of days, **knee** 28.6%, **hip** 30.0%, **lower back** 33.5%; the injured rate is 100%, so the guarantee is worth **50.7 / 71.4 / 70.0 / 66.5 points** respectively. That is the first honest statement of what the rehab work actually buys.

- [x] AND THE NEW CHECKS' COVERAGE IS STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED, after the first version of my own comment overclaimed. Deleting the early return fires **only** the source-shape check — the behavioural one still passes, because with no flagged joints the pool filter matches nothing and null comes back by a second mechanism, which makes it nearly the same tautology on that mutation. Adding a `return pool[0] ?? null` fallback — the realistic refactor error, and the one that would actually put rehab in an uninjured plan — fires **four**. Both kept; neither covers the other's mutation.

- [x] **THE TOUR HAS NOW BEEN DRIVEN AGAINST THE REAL SCREENS, AND IT FOUND A REAL DEFECT ON THE FIRST FULL RUN.** `verify:tour-real` mounts the actual Dashboard, NutritionDisplay, MealPlan, ExerciseTab, ToolsTab and BottomTabBar with a real generated mesocycle, and walks all ten stops in Chromium at 390px. Every stop's spotlight now lands on its real target to within 2px, every hole is inside the viewport, and Finish closes it.

- [x] **THE SET STOP TOLD THE USER TO DO SOMETHING THE APP REFUSED.** It says "Leave the fields blank and I'll take the prescribed numbers." Weight had a prescribed fallback; **reps did not** — it fell back to `''` and the save was rejected with *"Enter reps to log this set"*, while the reps box suggested **"0"**, the single value the save explicitly refuses. It could only ever bite on week 1, and week 1 is the only week the tour ever runs in (`tourArmed` is set at exactly one call site — the moment onboarding succeeds), so the two were guaranteed to meet. Blank reps now logs the **bottom** of the prescribed range on Ashley's ruling (9 of "9-11", 33 of "33-48s", 40 of "40m") — bottom not top, because the number feeds the progression engine and erring high would hand someone heavier weights off a set the app invented. The `reps <= 0 never logs` guard is untouched.

- [x] FOUR OF THE FIVE THINGS THAT RUN FOUND WERE THE HARNESS, NOT THE APP, and that is the ratio the `.tour-harness` README already warns about. (1) No `<meta viewport>` in the harness HTML → Chrome laid out at 980px and every rect was off-screen. (2) The harness wrote `#/nutrition` where the app's own `tabHash` writes `#/tab/nutrition`. (3) The driver's spotlight selector matched the tour's own *card* shadow. (4) The driver carried a hand-written copy of the stop list with the chat tab's key as `navChat`; it is `chatfab`, and it reported a target missing that was never missing — it now reads `TOUR_STEPS` out of the page. Plus one of mine in the fake Supabase client: `insert().select().single()` returned the payload rather than the stored row, so it had no generated `id` and every caller needing one gave up silently.

- [x] AND ONE OF MY OWN CHECKS WENT GREEN AGAINST A MUTATION THAT DELETED THE THING IT CHECKED — `/reps <= 0/` matched the **comment I had just written above the fallback** explaining the guard. Same shape as the earlier "no slot is named in the shape decision" false pass. It now matches the condition itself.

- [x] **THE POST-ONBOARDING CHAT OPENER IS ONE MESSAGE, DOWN FROM FOUR** (Ashley: *"we dont need to say that much and it could be 1 message"*). Three went for a reason, not just for length: "I'm your coach" repeated the header that says so permanently; **"Talk to me like you'd talk to a person"** was hers to cut — *"as far as the user is concerned it is a person so I dont like this wording"* — and naming the thing it is pretending not to be is the one line that breaks it; and "nothing moves without your say-so" had already been said minutes earlier in the onboarding opener.

- [x] AND IT NOW KNOWS WHEN DAY ONE ACTUALLY IS. Ashley's sketch was "Day one starts right now with {session}", which is wrong for anyone whose first training day isn't today — and the code it replaced was worse there: it fell through to *"it's a rest day on your plan. How's the recovery going?"*, asking someone who finished onboarding ninety seconds ago how they were recovering from nothing. Four branches now: today, a training day past their usual training hour ("ready when you are — tonight or tomorrow, your call"), a named future day, and a no-session fallback that invents nothing. The first quick reply follows: **"Talk me through day one"** when it isn't today, because "Talk me through today" under "Day one is Monday" is the app contradicting itself on one screen.

- [x] **NOT EVERY QUESTION NEEDS BUTTONS — Ashley narrowed the always-offer ruling after living with it:** *"I dont think every question needs a quick reply always. Some have obvious answers."* The line she picked: buttons stay wherever the app has its own wording for the answer — nobody can guess where "moderate" ends and "active" begins, or that `home_gym` means barbell + dumbbells + bench — and go where a person already knows what to type. Four drop them: **do you know your lifts, snacks or meals only, male or female, how many meals a day.** The other fourteen keep them. This narrows the earlier ruling rather than undoing its reasoning: options had become invisible, which is not the same as every question wanting them.

- [x] IT SUPPRESSES THE FIRST OFFER ONLY, and that is the whole safety of it. Two doors stay open, both the original "chips as a rescue" idea: an answer that fails to map re-asks with the real options attached, and the stuck-rescue offers them to anyone who says "I don't know". Nothing becomes unanswerable by tapping — it stops being the opening move. **`injuries` and `dietaryPreferences` can never be marked obvious**, whatever they look like: both are enforced downstream, so a missed tap there is a filtering miss or a food the plan will serve them, and `test:onboarding-chips` fails if either is ever flagged.

- [x] **THE COMPOSER WAS DESCRIBING A DIFFERENT QUESTION THAN THE ONE ON SCREEN**, photographed twice in one run: *"what should I call you?"* with `Tell me your goal…` underneath, and a question about training style with `How active is your day?`. Reproduced in a real browser before touching it (`npm run verify:composer`, which mounts the actual `ConversationalOnboarding`, not a replica) and both rows came back wrong. The name one could never have been right: `displayName` is in `NEVER_BLOCKING_SLOTS`, so it is in neither open-slot list, so on the app's very first question the hint was **guaranteed** to name a different question. Now the placeholder reads the conversation — the live chip card, or the opener's own tagged name ask — and only falls back to the slot list when nothing is on screen. All three cases match after.

- [x] **THE QUESTION ORDER THIS APP CAREFULLY MAINTAINS WAS BEING RE-SORTED BEFORE THE COACH EVER SAW IT.** Every caller built the outstanding-question list as `[...missingRequiredSlots, ...unconfirmedOptionalSlots]`, and that list is `remaining` — literally what the model is told is left to ask. Required-first is not the app's order. Measured: **injuries 5th → 11th**, mealsPerDay 15th → 10th. Injuries is the one that matters — it is `required: false` only because a plan can be built without it, and Ashley moved it from 16th to 5th precisely so it could not be stranded behind the questions people abandon on. A safety ruling was being quietly undone by a concatenation. `openSlotsInOrder` now returns the same membership in the file's own order; injuries is 5th as sent.

- [x] SAYING "I'M STRUGGLING TO LOSE WEIGHT" GOT NO ACKNOWLEDGEMENT — the coach took the 12% number, said what it meant for the plan, and asked the next question. The prompt already had a "be on their side out loud when something sounds hard" rule; it lost to the anti-flattery ban sitting next to it, which is emphatic and carries a live example about this exact "12% body fat" message. The two are now ordered explicitly: **a difficulty always gets a reaction and it outranks the no-grading rule**, one clause, no pep talk and no promise about results, then straight on with the question. **Needs the edge-function deploy to take effect.**

- [x] **THE SAME DEPLOY WENT TO THE WRONG SUPABASE PROJECT TWICE, AND THE SECOND TIME THE SANCTIONED ROUTE COULD NOT RUN ON ASHLEY'S MACHINE AT ALL.** `npm run db:link-prod` was `bash scripts/db-link-prod.sh`. Her shell is Windows PowerShell, where `bash` is not on PATH — it printed `'bash' is not recognized`, the link never happened, and `npx supabase functions deploy` ran anyway against TEST. The first time had a different cause (the docs said the ambient link was PRODUCTION; it is TEST) and the identical outcome: a confident `Deployed Functions on project vswuurrtbzbrgubddefv` line that reads exactly like success. **The always-offer-options prompt is still not on production.**

- [x] THE COMMON CAUSE IS THE TWO-STEP, NOT THE TYPO. Nothing connected "link to production" to "deploy", so when step 1 failed, step 2 still ran against whatever was linked before. Fixed mechanically rather than by another instruction to read the output line — "read carefully" had already failed twice as a control. **`npm run deploy:functions:prod -- onboarding-chat`** now asks for the phrase, links, deploys with `--project-ref` naming the target *on the deploy call itself*, and relinks to TEST on the way out. Proven with a recording stub standing in for the CLI: a failed production link produces **zero** deploy calls and still returns the CLI to TEST.

- [x] ALL FOUR `scripts/db-*.sh` ARE NODE NOW — Node is a hard dependency of this repo, bash was an assumption about the developer's shell, and it was false for the only person who needs to reach production. The npm command names are unchanged, so CLAUDE.md stays true. `test:deploy-path` holds it: no npm script may shell out to bash, production costs the typed phrase, a non-interactive stdin is refused rather than left hanging, and exactly one file names the live project ref.

- [x] ONE TRANSLATION BUG THE PORT ALMOST SHIPPED WITH, CAUGHT BEFORE COMMIT: bash's `trap … EXIT` fires on every exit path; its obvious Node equivalent, `try/finally`, **does not** — `process.exit()` skips finally blocks entirely. A script that bailed out mid-production-leg would have left the CLI pointed at the live database, the exact state these scripts exist to prevent. The relink is a `process.on('exit')` handler, and the gate asserts that specifically.

- [x] MY OWN GATE FIRED ON FIVE SMOKE SCRIPTS AND THE GATE WAS WRONG, NOT THE SCRIPTS. "Only one file holds either project ref" went red on `smoke-check-week`, `smoke-regen-week` and three `verify-*-live` scripts. Reading them showed every hit is a *refusal guard* — `if (!url.includes(TEST)) { Refusing… }` — a script declining to run anywhere but TEST, and a guard that imports its own definition of "safe" is one a bad edit can switch off everywhere at once. None held the production ref. The check is now the narrower, stronger property: nothing but `db-target.mjs` may name PRODUCTION, and every other ref mention must sit in a refusal guard.

- [x] **THE TYPEWRITER WAS TOO SLOW AND NOT SMOOTH — TWO PROBLEMS, DIFFERENT CAUSES, AND GUESSING WOULD HAVE FIXED THE WRONG ONE.** Measured in a browser with a MutationObserver recording when each word actually lands, not when its timer fired. **Slow** was the tick: 110ms/word, so one 67-word coach reply took **8.9 seconds**. **Not smooth was NOT timer drift** — min 110ms, median 110ms, p90 111ms, the tick was rock steady. It was the sentence-end pause: **380ms on top of the tick, producing 491ms gaps four times a message.** Steady, steady, steady, stall.

- [x] FIXED BOTH, AND MEASURED AGAIN: total **8907ms → 3681ms**, gap max **491ms → 133ms**, jitter (std dev) **90ms → 19ms**, gaps over 150ms **4 → 0**. The pause ratio came down from ~3.45× the tick to ~1.6× — still a beat at a full stop, no longer a halt. `normal` lands at 50ms, near the app's *original* default; it had been doubled to 110 on an earlier "roughly double it" request and the request came back the other way after living with it. `slow` sits at 7.0s, close to what the old default did, so anyone who liked that pace has it one tap away rather than lost.

- [x] **THE REVEAL IS A FRAME LOOP AGAINST AN ABSOLUTE CLOCK NOW, NOT A CHAIN OF setTimeouts** — and that half is for real devices rather than the bench. The old version scheduled each word from the moment the previous one *rendered*, so every late timer pushed the rest of the message further behind with no way to recover; error accumulated. Now the whole schedule is computed once up front and each frame asks "which words are due by now" — a stalled frame catches up on the next instead of falling permanently behind, and updates land on frames rather than between them. Headless Chromium has far more even timers than a phone mid-scroll, so the honest reading of that steady 110ms median is *"this bench cannot see drift"*, not *"drift does not exist"*.

- [x] `test:reveal-timing` holds both halves — the frame loop and the timing budget — and `npm run verify:reveal` re-runs the measurement. Proven to bite: reverting `normal` to 110/380 turns four checks red, and swapping the frame loop back to a setTimeout chain turns two more.

- [x] ONE CHECK OF MINE HAD THE WRONG SHAPE AND I LOOSENED IT, SAYING SO. An absolute "worst gap under 150ms" fired on `slow` at 245ms — but against slow's own 95ms tick that is 2.6×, the identical proportion to normal's 130ms against 50ms. The absolute number was encoding *"the default must feel fluid"*, which is a claim about `fast` and `normal`; the ratio check already covers all three. Slow is exempt by name, with the reason in the file, rather than the threshold being quietly raised.

- [x] **SECOND PASS ON THE ONBOARDING — WALKED ALL 27 SCREENS AT 390px AND FIXED WHAT THE WALK SHOWED.** Nothing overflows a phone now, which is the structural check; the rest were things only visible by looking.

- [x] **THE TRAINING HALF WAS SPLIT DOWN THE MIDDLE.** Style, cardio and recovery are training questions and sat at #17-19, *after* the four body metrics — so the flow went training → body → training → food, a context switch out and back for no reason. They move up with days and session length. The body block stays intact and stays ahead of the food half, so the rule the order gate enforces is untouched. `dislikedExercises` came with them from **#27**, where it had been stranded seven questions past anything it related to.

- [x] **THE LAST REQUIRED QUESTION WAS #22, BEHIND THE TALLEST SCREEN IN THE FLOW.** `mealsPerDay` sat after `dietaryPreferences` and `dislikedFoods`, so the biggest friction point stood between the user and a plan — give up there and nineteen answers bought nothing. It moves to **#21**, leaving six genuinely optional questions after it: abandon any time from there and a full plan still generates.

- [x] TWO QUESTIONS READ AS THE SAME QUESTION ASKED TWICE, and moving them next to each other made it obvious: dietary's composer hint was literally *"Anything you avoid?"* and the next question was *"Any foods you just won't eat?"*. Dietary's hint now names what the tag list is for (*"Vegan, gluten-free, an allergy…"*) and the follow-up opens with **"Anything else"**. Deliberately not narrowed to "don't like the taste of" — `detectAllergenTags` reads that field, so it must stay somewhere an allergy can be typed.

- [x] AND ONE QUESTION WAS ITS OWN PLACEHOLDER: `conditioningPreference` showed *"How do you feel about cardio?"* in the composer, under *"How do you feel about cardio?"*. `dislikedExercises` had no placeholder at all. Both gated now, across every slot.

- [x] **FOUR LAYOUT FIXES THE MEASUREMENTS COULD NOT HAVE FOUND** — they needed the screenshot. (1) An odd number of cards stranded the last one at half width beside an empty cell; five questions have exactly three options, so it was most of them. The last card spans now. (2) A full-width 44px "Pick at least one" bar under one tidy row of pills was the heaviest thing on the lightest screen — the footer follows the option shape now, pill under pills. (3) `mealsPerDay` showed the **same plate emoji three times** under "2 meals / 3 meals / 4 meals" (364px → 276px once dropped) — same judgement as the pill rule, applied to icons. (4) Numeric fields carried no unit: a box labelled "Height" with placeholder "100–250", under a question that has scrolled — and the grouped cards put three fields under one question, so at most one could ever have taken its unit from the sentence above.

- [x] ONE THING I NEARLY REPORTED AS A DEFECT AND WAS NOT: the three barbell questions each render a card showing all three fields. That is `NUMERIC_GROUPS` working — Save resolves the whole group in one call, so the coach asks once. The harness renders each slot standalone, which is a harness artifact. Checked the resolve path before writing it up.

- [x] AND THE GATE CAUGHT ITSELF AGAIN: `no slot is named in the shape decision` fired on a **comment** that named `mealsPerDay` as an example of what the rule produces — the opposite of the failure it exists for. It strips comments now and asserts against the shape logic only.

- [x] **"SOMETIMES THE QUICK REPLIES COME UP AND SOMETIMES THEY DON'T" — THEY WERE DOING EXACTLY WHAT THEY WERE TOLD.** `present_slot` read *"This is a RESCUE, not the way questions are asked… Never call it on the first asking"*, so whether you got help depended on the model judging you stuck. Unpredictable from the user's side and unmeasured from the codebase's — nothing asserted anything about it either way. Ashley's ruling: offer them on every closed-set question, first asking included.

- [x] **THE OLD RULE WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE CAUSE AND WRONG ABOUT THE FIX.** It existed because chips-under-everything made onboarding feel like a form. Measured at 390px, the culprit was SIZE, not existence: **goal 213px, cardio 260px, days 308px, injuries 308px, dietary 771px** — the dietary question alone taking 771px of an 844px screen, before the coach's message above it or the keyboard below. And all seven training-day cards carried the **same 📅**: seven identical icons, each in a box sized to hold one, to say "Mon Tue Wed".

- [x] SO THE SHAPE IS NOW READ OFF THE SLOT'S OWN DATA: **a card when the options carry a description, pills when they don't.** Not a hand-list — the description is exactly what a card exists to show. It lands where it should: cards keep goal, experience, activity, equipment, session length, style, cardio, recovery, meals, cooking, snacks, breakfast and the lifts question (all ≤4 options); pills take days, injuries, dietary, cuisines and sex. **Dietary 771px → 223px, days and injuries 308px → 96px.** The pill is the coach chat's quick-reply shape exactly — rounded-full, surface-raised, text-xs, 44px tap target — because a new user meets both surfaces within minutes.

- [x] **INJURIES MOVED 16th → 6th** (Ashley's ruling), straight after equipment. It is the only question whose absence can HURT someone rather than cost accuracy, and at 16th it sat behind the age/height/weight/sex block people most often abandon on — so someone who answered eleven questions and stopped got a complete plan with no injury filtering. Above the barbell chain deliberately: asking for a deadlift number before someone has had a chance to mention their back is the wrong order.

- [x] **THE OPENER: 88 WORDS → 46, THREE MESSAGES → TWO.** The one that went was the capability pitch. Not because it was bad but because its job moved: the post-onboarding tour now demonstrates those same four things on the real screens, and arguing for the app before showing it is the weaker half of that pair. *"Nothing changes without your okay"* was kept deliberately and moved beside the name question — the one line that is reassurance rather than sales.

- [x] A GATE WAS ASSERTING THE OLD RULE AND WENT RED, CORRECTLY. `test:onboarding-conversational` §2–3 locked in *"chips are described as a rescue"* and the three stuck cases. Inverted rather than deleted, with the reversal and its reason recorded in the gate itself — the fact that a documented decision was overturned is the interesting part, and a deleted section says nothing. New `test:onboarding-chips` owns the shape rule, the word budget on the opener, and the injury position.

- [ ] **NEEDS `npx supabase functions deploy onboarding-chat` ON BOTH PROJECTS** — the always-offer rule is a prompt change and is inert until deployed. Sanctioned path in PROJECT-LOG §7.3: bare deploy hits TEST, production needs `db:link-prod` first.

- [ ] **NOT VERIFIED, AND IT IS THE THING THAT DECIDES WHETHER THIS WORKED**: model compliance. The prompt now says call `present_slot` every time, but no conversation has been run against it — `*.supabase.co` is unreachable from the sandbox. If the model skips the call on some turns, the inconsistency Ashley reported survives in a milder form. A deterministic client-side backstop was considered and NOT built: the client would have to guess which question the sentence asked, and the code is explicit that options under the wrong question are worse than none. Measure first, then decide.

- [x] **THE POST-ONBOARDING TOUR IS BUILT — "TAP-TO-LEARN", TEN STOPS.** From Ashley's design handoff. It never renders a mock of a screen: it measures a real element by `data-tour` key, cuts a hole in a scrim, and lets the real app take the tap. So every stop shows the CURRENT app with the user's own plan in it, and the nav stops advance because the hash actually changed. The set at stop 7 is a genuine week-1 calibration set — the point, not a side effect. Ends in Chat where `buildFirstRunIntro` takes over.

- [x] **TWO REAL DEFECTS, BOTH FOUND BY DRIVING IT IN A BROWSER, NEITHER VISIBLE TO ANY STATIC CHECK.** (1) The tour detects "the set saved" by `data-tour="setrow"` disappearing — and detected "today is a rest day" by *the same absence*, so logging the set skipped the stop the user had just completed and renumbered to "of 9" behind them. (2) `bringIntoView` tried to bring the fixed tab bar to 130px from the top; being fixed it never moved, and the whole page scrolled 650px underneath it instead.

- [x] SO `npm run verify:tour` EXISTS: the real component driven in the pre-installed Chromium over CDP, using Node's built-in WebSocket so it adds no dependency. 34 checks — the tap phase blocks every non-target and passes the tap through the hole, the set stop advances only on a real save, skip stores the place and the pill resumes it, Finish writes `done` and a reload does not restart it. `test:app-tour` covers the half a browser cannot: that the keys exist on the right elements.

- [x] A THIRD FINDING WAS THE HARNESS'S OWN FAULT, and it is worth recording because it looked exactly like a real bug: rooted at the harness folder, Tailwind never scanned `src/`, so `inset-0` and `z-[60]` were absent from the bundle and the overlay rendered unpositioned — reported as "clicks are not blocked". **A harness has to be built the way the app is or its verdict is about the harness.** Diagnosed by diffing the two CSS bundles for `.inset-0` rather than by reasoning.

- [x] **THE GATE CAUGHT ITSELF FIRST.** `test:app-tour` scans for `data-tour` literals, and the tab bar sets its own through a `TOUR_KEY` map so a new tab cannot silently go untagged — so the first run reported all four nav targets missing. They were not; the check was. It reads the map now, and checks BOTH directions: an orphan attribute nobody points at reads as wired and is a failure too. Proven to bite: renaming `hero` turns two checks red.

- [ ] NOT VERIFIED HERE, AND IT IS THE HALF THAT MATTERS MOST: the tour has never run against the real screens. The harness uses stub targets, and `*.supabase.co` is unreachable from the sandbox, so a fresh TEST profile through the actual onboarding UI — the handoff's own instruction — is still Ashley's machine. What is unproven: that each key sits on the element the copy describes, the rest-day variant, and the callout's placement against real content.

- [ ] The handoff names `exercise/ExerciseRow.tsx` for the set-log ✓; it is actually in `exercise/SetGrid.tsx`. Tagged there.

- [x] **`test:injury-rebuild` WAS TESTING RANDOMNESS, AND MY FIRST FIX FOR IT WAS TOO.** Its "the two modes genuinely differ" check read `rebuiltSlots > subSlots` — a proxy that tested "rebuild is not a no-op" by way of substitution DROPPING slots it could not safely fill (436 vs 432). The four new hip movements are safe for a shoulder injury, so substitution can now fill those four and both modes return 436. The check went red **because the app got better**: a shoulder-injured trainee's substituted plan is fuller than it was.

- [x] I REPLACED IT WITH A CONTENT DIFF AND THE MUTATION SAID NO. Comparing the two modes' exercise names showed 332 of 436 slots differing — but mutating the substitute call to `'rebuild'`, so both did the same thing, **still showed 252 differing** and stayed green. `executeLastingInjury` is non-deterministic: two identical rebuilds diverge by shuffle alone. The check was measuring the RNG. Both calls now run under one seed, and the same mutation reads 0 of 452 and turns two checks red. Comparison is over the common prefix only, since the modes legitimately return different lengths and counting positions past the shorter array against `undefined` is not a content difference.

- [x] THAT IS THE THIRD COUNT-PROXY IN THIS AREA TO GO FALSE WITHOUT ANYTHING BREAKING — after `test:band-slots`' frozen 335 and its rehab total. The shape is consistent: a number that stands in for a property drifts when the catalogue grows, and the honest repair is to assert the property. Worth checking the remaining gates for the same shape.

- [x] **A BAD HIP AND A BAD BACK GET REHAB NOW, NOT JUST SUBTRACTION.** Both measured at **0 of 576 training days** and **144 of 144 plans with none at all**; both are now **576/576** and **0/144**. Knees and shoulders unchanged at 576/576, and the uninjured control still 0/576 — the slot does not fire for people who reported nothing. The back rotates near-perfectly evenly (Bird Dog 193, Side Plank 192, Dead Bug 191); hips rotates across Clamshell, Side-Lying Hip Abduction and Standing Band Hip Abduction.

- [x] THE LOWER BACK WAS TAGGING; **THE HIP WAS NOT, AND I SAID OTHERWISE.** I told Ashley both were "mostly labelling what's already there" — true for the back, false for the hip, and she chose scope on that basis before I corrected it. How I got it wrong: I searched the catalogue for hip-rehab-sounding *names* (Glute Bridge, Hip Thrust, Step-Ups all matched) and reported the count without checking them against the mechanism that would prescribe them. `pickRehabMovement` excludes `tier2_compound` outright — Ashley's own ruling — and **all four are tier2_compound**. Tagging them would have been a no-op that read as a fix. Same shape as the Hip Thrust and tone-probe errors: reasoning about a file instead of reading the contract it must satisfy.

- [x] SO THE HIP GOT **FOUR NEW ENTRIES**, not tags: Clamshell, Side-Lying Hip Abduction and Standing Band Hip Abduction as `activation` primers (matching the seven shoulder-rehab entries rather than inventing a shape), plus Bird Dog. All bodyweight or band, so minimalist and bodyweight tiers get them too — a hip is not only injured in a commercial gym.

- [x] **DURATIONS AND TIERS ARE LOAD-BEARING HERE, AND THE BAND IS SHARED ACROSS JOINTS.** The slot keeps everything within 1.25× the cheapest indicated movement, so 30/30/32 gives the hip a three-way rotation. Pallof Press was deliberately NOT tagged for the back: at 30s it would have become the band's only member and excluded Dead Bug and Side Plank, reducing variety to one — the documented 576/576 monotony failure. Bird Dog stays tier3 for the same class of reason: the picker takes the primer tier *exclusively*, so making it a primer would have left it alone in the back's band and collapsed the rotation it was added to widen.

- [x] **THREE GATES WERE ASSERTING THE OLD GAP AS CORRECT** and went red on purpose. `test:rehab-prescribed` used `'hips'` as its example of an unmapped code (now swapped to `ankles`, which is the stronger case — a joint the app knows about but cannot treat) and asserted only "the two joints this feature covers"; `test:joint-tag-states` listed `lower_back` among "injuries not yet reviewed". Its six repeated `['shoulders','knees']` literals became one named list, because six places to update is how hips and the back would have stayed untested while the gate went on passing.

- [x] AND ONE GATE CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT IN MY OWN ENTRIES — *"every indicated movement records the joint it loads, honestly"*. Dead Bug, Side Plank and Bird Dog claimed to be back rehab while recording that they load nothing. Fixed by declaring `loads_joints`, **with an explicit `contraindicated_joints: []` alongside**: that getter is `contraindicated_joints ?? loads_joints`, so naming the joint without it would have marked each movement dangerous for the very joint it treats — the `31b05d7` trap running the other way. Asserted now: a bad back still excludes exactly 12 things, and the legacy path diverges by exactly those three names.

- [ ] **"LOADS YOUR LOWER BACK AXIAL" WAS ALREADY SHIPPING.** Both injury sentences rendered joint tags with `.replace(/_/g,' ')`, and one tag is not a phrase. Anyone with a bad back looking at a conflicting exercise read that today — a pre-existing copy bug this work would have doubled rather than caused. Now goes through `jointListDisplay`, so it reads "Loads your hip, knee and lower back". Same rule as the meal refusals: English, never tag names.

- [ ] FOUND EN ROUTE, NOT FIXED: **core work categorises as `isolation_chest`** — Dead Bug, Plank, Side Plank, Pallof Press, Wall Sit and now Bird Dog, via the `default:` case at `load-prescription.ts:394`. Pre-existing and verified **inert** rather than assumed so: every one prescribes "Bodyweight" with `starting_weight_kg` null, so the wrong category never becomes a wrong weight. Frozen honestly in `test:categorize-precedence` — recorded as it is, not as it ought to be.

- [ ] **FOUR INJURIES STILL GET SUBTRACTION AND NO REHAB**: ankles, elbows, wrists, neck. Down from six. Ashley's call, and content rather than mechanism — for ankles there is nothing suitable at all (the calf raises are exactly what you would avoid), and neck work is the one worth most caution about the app prescribing. `test:band-slots` freezes the covered list so filling any of the four turns it red on purpose.

- [ ] **SIX OF THE EIGHT INJURIES GET SUBTRACTION AND NO REHAB.** Measured across the full grid at HEAD: a knee-injured trainee gets a knee-indicated exercise in **432 of 432** profiles, a shoulder-injured one in **432 of 432** — and a bad **hip, elbow, ankle, wrist, neck or lower back** gets one in **0 of 432**, on every equipment tier. Not a selector bug: `indicated_joints` is tagged on exactly two joints in the whole catalogue (`knee` 10 entries, `shoulder` 9) and the other six have nothing to select. So the app removes what would hurt those joints — the hips/elbows/ankles work just extended that to three more — and prescribes nothing back. **Ashley's call**, and a content decision, not a mechanical one: which exercises, and whether the app should be prescribing rehab for a neck or a wrist at all. `test:band-slots` now freezes the two-joint list so filling any of the six turns it red on purpose.

- [x] **`test:band-slots` HAD BEEN RED ON MAIN, AND ITS NUMBER IS NOW ROOT-CAUSED.** It froze a raw total — rehab bands in main slots ≥ 335 — which drifted three times (348 → 340 → 318), each drop benign, each one re-breaking the gate. Bisected: it held at 340 through `a1b894e` and fell to 318 at **`987531f`** ("Prefer the best tool the trainee owns"). `isEquipmentQualityExempt` spares a rehab band the −1 penalty, but an exemption only zeroes the band's *own* factor — it does not stop a barbell rival collecting +1, so 22 placements lost a main slot to a better implement. Every one was Spanish Squat.

- [x] IT COST NOBODY THEIR REHAB, which is the distinction the total could not draw — 318 reads identically whether every trainee got one or half got two. Per profile it is 432/432 covered and 432/432 in a main slot; the drop is weeks that carried Spanish Squat twice now carrying it once. **Scale change:** the headline is a share of profiles now, not a placement count, so it is not comparable to the 348/340/318 series and the 335 floor is gone. Denominator went **up**, 48 profiles on one injury → 432 on each of two, read off the sweep that already runs. Proven to bite on three mutations.

- [x] **A CORRECTION USED TO SIT ALONGSIDE THE MISTAKE INSTEAD OF REPLACING IT** — the worst of four bugs Ashley hit in one exchange, because it silently corrupted her training log. She logged deadlifts, the reps came out wrong, she said *"No 3x10 deadlifts"* — and the app wrote sets 4, 5 and 6 next to the wrong 1, 2 and 3. Her Exercise tab read **"3 working sets · 6 logged"**, and every future weight for that lift would have built on three sets she never did. `log_workout` now takes `corrects_previous`, which clears that exercise's sets for the day before writing, so the log refills from set 1.

- [x] APPENDING IS STILL THE DEFAULT AND MUST BE — "I did three more sets" is the common case and `allocateSetNumbers` was right about it. The bug was never the appending; it was that nothing upstream could tell a correction from extra work. The gate asserts BOTH directions, and asserts the flag cannot half-apply: with no `deleteSet` to hand it appends and honestly reports nothing replaced, rather than claiming a correction it did not make.

- [x] **THE RECEIPT NOW SAYS WHICH IT DID.** A correction and an addition write identical rows, so an identical receipt makes a mis-called correction invisible until the numbers are wrong weeks later. A replacement is titled "Corrected ·" and names the count replaced, while Undo is still one tap away.

- [x] **THE APP INVENTED A NUMBER SHE NEVER SAID.** Asked "how many sets and reps?", she answered **"3"** — and it logged **3×8**, because 8 was the PRESCRIBED rep target. `weight_kg` had carried a hard guard for a while (*"ONLY if the user actually stated one… do NOT guess or default to 0"*) and `exercise_name` had one too (*"Never invent/guess one from context or history"*). **Reps had none**, and were required, so the model had to supply something. Now guarded identically on both logging tools, and required precisely so an unstated rep count blocks the call instead of being filled in.

- [x] TWO MORE FROM THE SAME SCREENSHOT, both prompt-side: *"No 3x10 deadlifts"* states the exercise, the sets AND the reps, and the coach discarded all three to ask for a weight — so a rule now says carry every stated value into the call and ask only for what is genuinely missing. And it asked **"What weight did you use for Deadlifts?" three times running**, taking "100kg" each time, so a rule now forbids repeating an identical question: say what you have and what is missing, or ask something narrower.

- [x] THE BEHAVIOURAL HALF IS TESTED FOR REAL, not grepped — `test:log-correction` runs the executor against an in-memory set store and asserts the log holds 3 sets and not 6, that they carry the corrected reps, and that they refill from set 1. Proven to bite on three mutations; disabling the replace path reproduces Ashley's screenshot exactly, `[8,8,8,10,10,10]`.

- [x] **CELERY, SESAME, MUSTARD, LUPIN AND SULPHITES ARE ENFORCED NOW** — five legally-declarable allergens that could previously only be REMEMBERED. And it was worse than "no mechanism": **celery, mustard, sesame oil and sesame seeds were real, servable entries in the food database with empty tag sets.** Not theoretical. All five now have tags, rules, options and prompt text, taking the app from 17 dietary preferences to 22.

- [x] **THE PARITY GATE WAS WRITTEN FIRST, BEFORE ANY TAGGING, AND WAS RED ON ALL EIGHT CHECKS.** The food database exists TWICE — `src/lib/food-db.ts` (332 foods) and `supabase/functions/_shared/food-db.ts` (323), because a Deno edge function cannot import across the `src/lib` boundary — and nothing kept them in step. A tag added to one protects nothing in the other, and the result would have looked finished. `test:food-db-parity` proves both copies agree on every allergen tag; proven to bite by untagging sesame seeds on the server side only, which is precisely the half-safe failure it exists for.

- [x] IT TOOK FOUR PLACES, NOT TWO, and `test:diet-tag-sync` caught the one I missed: `FORBIDDEN_TAGS`, both food-db copies' tags, **generate-meals' prompt block**, and **chat-gemini's prompt block**. A preference absent from either prompt is one the model is never told about however well it is tagged. The 17→22 count assertion in that gate is a deliberate tripwire and it worked exactly as designed.

- [x] **A GATE WAS ASSERTING THE GAP AS CORRECT.** `test:onboarding-slots` contained *"the five untagged allergens (no enforcement mechanism exists) are correctly never returned"* — and passed. A gate that describes a hole as intended is how the hole survives: it makes the missing thing look deliberate and defended. Now inverted: the same disclosure must return all five.

- [x] THE DETECTOR MISSED AN ORDINARY PHRASING, and the fix reaches all twelve allergens rather than the five. "Sulphites give me a reaction" scanned completely clean — the signal list matched the noun form "reaction to" but not "gives me a reaction" or "I react to". Broadened deliberately: this governs every allergen, and a missed disclosure is a worse failure than a tagged food someone could have eaten.

- [x] LUPIN FLOUR WAS ADDED TO THE DATABASE, and that is a safety decision rather than a gate-satisfying one. **An ingredient absent from the database cannot be tag-checked at all** — a proposal containing lupin flour was unfilterable by construction. `test:diet-tag-sync` [11] independently forbids a rule with zero data (the `is_grain` bug, where a rule read correct and never fired), and this satisfies it honestly rather than by exemption.

- [x] **AND ONE OF MY OWN NEW CHECKS WAS A LIAR.** A section landed AFTER the `if (failures > 0) process.exit(1)` line, so `test:onboarding-slots` printed a ✗ on screen and exited 0. A gate that reports pass while showing a failure is worse than no gate. The epilogue now sits at the end of the file, and the same run exits 1 as it should.

- [x] FOUND EN ROUTE: **choosing "Mediterranean" did nothing whatsoever.** It has no hard exclusions by design — it is a style, not a restriction — but it was also absent from the meal generator entirely, so the option was offered and silently ignored. It now gets a positive style steer, phrased so it cannot read as a ban. The new coverage check found this on its first run.

- [ ] `test:meal-quality` cannot run from the sandbox — it creates a throwaway profile against the TEST project and `*.supabase.co` is unreachable here. Verified pre-existing by stashing the changes and re-running. It is the gate that would prove end-to-end that a tagged allergen never reaches a plate, and it needs Ashley's machine.

- [x] **THE SOFT PREFERENCES ARE READ BY SOMETHING NOW** — they were compiled and read by nothing. "I prefer chicken to fish", "not a fan of burpees but I'll do them" were recorded, compiled by `compileSoftExercisePreferences`, and had **zero call sites** outside the file defining them. The comment above it said *"scoped to swap-candidate ranking only (mesocycle-edit.getReplacementCandidates)"* — describing a consumer that did not exist. A truthful-looking comment over dead code is how the next person gets misled, which is exactly what happened when the capture trace first read it.

- [x] NOT A NEW DECISION — VISION-ARCHITECTURE.md §1.2 already ruled it: soft exercise dislike/like → *"ranking"*, *"scoped to `getReplacementCandidates` only"*, rotation explicitly unaffected (the doc even records why rotation is out of reach — threading a ranker through `rotateVariation` means changing two exported signatures, `generateMesocycle`'s parameter list, and the audit's independent copies). Wiring it executes that ruling rather than inventing one.

- [x] **IT REORDERS, IT NEVER REMOVES, and that is the line the gate defends.** A lean is not a ban: a movement someone is lukewarm about sinks to the bottom of the swap list and is **still offered**, because they may well pick it anyway. A hard dislike is a different channel entirely — it becomes an exclusion — and `test:soft-preferences` asserts a hard fact can never leak into ranking, where it would leave a banned movement on the menu. Proven to bite: making a soft dislike filter instead of sink turns two checks red.

- [ ] **THE FOOD HALF IS STILL UNREAD.** `compileSoftFoodPreferences` has the same zero call sites, and §1.2 says it belongs in `assembleDay` candidate scoring. Not done here — meal generation is a different subsystem and deserves its own pass rather than being tacked onto an exercise change.

- [x] **`test:audit` IS BACK TO 0 / 13,967** — the two `rotation_relative_load` failures Ashley chose to ship around are fixed. But the audit's description of them was wrong, and the real defect is both simpler and a hundred times larger.

- [x] **THE SAME LIFT, IN THE SAME WEEK, WITH IDENTICAL SETS/REPS/RPE, WAS PRESCRIBED TWO DIFFERENT WEIGHTS.** Not a rotation problem at all. `Calf Raises 3×15-20 @ RPE 6-7` came out at **12.5kg on one day and 20kg on another in the same week** — a user opening the app has no way to tell which is right. The audit only compares consecutive weeks at the same slot INDEX, so it saw just the 2 cases where the second instance happened to land in a slot that changed hands. **Measured across the audit's own 4×4×4 sweep: 202 of 1,536 lift-weeks, not 2.**

- [x] IT IS THE BUG THAT WAS ALREADY FIXED FOR REPS AND NEVER FOR WEIGHT, and `exercise-plan.ts` says so in its own words: *"A lift can hold two slots in a week, and the streak is keyed by name, so without this the second slot incremented the streak a second time and came out one rep above the first — the same exercise, the same weight, '4-6' on Monday and '5-7' on Thursday."* That was solved with a per-week memo; there is a second one for carry distance. **The weight had none**, so the second slot re-derived independently and landed an increment out. Third memo of exactly the same shape.

- [x] KEYED ON THE PRESCRIPTION, NOT THE NAME — two slots of one lift at different sets/reps/RPE legitimately differ (heavier for lower reps). Measured: of 203 same-lift different-weight lift-weeks, **202 were identical work**, so the key costs one legitimate case and catches everything else. The LOWER weight wins, matching the file's other one-way rules (`Math.min(name-keyed, SLOT-keyed)`; the main-lift floor that is "a FLOOR, never a ceiling"). Re-prescribed via `forceStartingWeightKg` rather than patching the number, because `display` and `per_set` derive from it.

- [x] **THE REMAINING 96 ARE DELIBERATE, AND THE GATE DRAWS THAT LINE RATHER THAN PRETENDING IT IS ZERO.** `enforceLoadCoherence` caps against THAT DAY's main lift — a unilateral accessory against the day's bilateral main, curls/laterals/shrugs against the day's main press. Different days, different main lifts, legitimately different ceilings, and a safety ceiling outranks week-level consistency. All 96 are one of those two shapes; `test:week-load-consistency` fails on anything that is not, and ratchets the population so it cannot drift back. Proven to bite: disabling the memo reproduces exactly 202 and turns three checks red.

- [ ] **THE AUDIT MISNAMED ITS OWN FAILING CONFIGURATION**, and it cost two reproduction attempts. It reported `home_gym / none / 45-60 / hybrid`; `baseMesocycleProfile` actually uses `session_duration_preference: '60-90'` — the `'45-60'` was a hardcoded string at the point the case is recorded, describing a profile that was never generated. Fixed in both call sites. A failure label that misnames its own inputs is worse than no label, because it is believed.

- [x] **THE COACH CHAT CAN NOW BE MEASURED, WHICH IT NEVER COULD BEFORE.** Phase 1 of the tone work, and deliberately no prompt change: `scripts/probe-coach-tone.mts` + a fixed `coach-warmth` persona, mirroring the onboarding probe that has existed for a while. **`chat-gemini` had no probe, no persona, and — verified today — no reply guarantee either**, while `onboarding-chat` has all three. So the risk is the opposite way round from how it looks: the function that already burned us is the protected one, and the coach chat is the exposed one.

- [x] THE NUMBER THAT DECIDES IT IS PRINTED FIRST AND LABELLED AS SUCH: **turns that spoke at all**. `fa683fc` shipped a voice improvement that fixed 7/7 grading openers and simultaneously silenced the model on 4 of 7 turns — worse than neutral, because an empty reply trips the dead-air guard that prints the slot's canonical form wording. A change aimed at less form-feel produced more of it. Nothing on this summary outranks that line, and the probe says so out loud.

- [x] FOUR SECONDARY MEASURES so a before/after is readable without re-reading two transcripts: verdict openers, longer-than-a-text, lists/headers, more than one emoji. All deterministic text checks, verified against canned replies covering each shape — silent turn, verdict opener, wall of text, emoji pile.

- [ ] AND ONE OF THOSE MEASURES WAS WRONG ON ITS FIRST RUN, caught by the same canned check: a bulleted reply scored 3 sentences because a list has few full stops, so a plainly-too-long reply passed the length test. Now `sentences > 3 || lines > 3`. Stated as what it is — a proxy for "too long to be a text message", not a parser.

- [ ] PHASE 2 IS NOT BUILT, on purpose. The wording change to both prompts waits until this probe has run against the deployed function and answered a question nothing currently can: **does `chat-gemini` ever return an empty reply today?** If it does, that is a live bug independent of tone and it changes what the tone change is allowed to do. Needs Ashley's machine — `*.supabase.co` is unreachable from the sandbox and the probe costs real Gemini calls.

- [x] **ONBOARDING v2 — THE CONVERSATION NOW HAS SOMEONE AT THE TOP OF IT.** A header with a slowly pulsing mint avatar, "Personal TrAIner", and what it is currently doing ("Building your plan"). That header is also what let the per-message COACH label GO: v1 stamped it above every coach line, which said the same thing twice once identity was stated permanently. The run-suppression helper that existed only to stop the label repeating went with it — dead code removed rather than left looking finished, which is exactly the failure the capture-trace report had just named.

- [x] **EVERY HEX IN THE v2 SPEC WAS CONVERTED, NOT PASTED.** The design names `#453C8E`, `#332A6E`, `#1E1941`, `#3BAE8E`, `rgba(91,233,194,…)`, `rgba(69,60,142,…)` — all correct in Nightshift, all wrong in Ember, Field and Graphite. Each maps to something the app already has: `--border` IS `#453C8E`, `--glow-rgb` IS the accent's rgb triplet, `--primary-2` IS the gradient's darker mint, `--input` IS `#241E4E`. The two the app had no token for (`#332A6E`, `#1E1941`) are `color-mix` steps between tokens. **Zero hex in the component**, and the gate fails on any.

- [x] PROGRESS TICKS REPLACE THE HAIRLINE — four bars, one filling per answered slot. Deliberately NOT a step counter: "12 of 18 answered" tells you you're working through a list, which is the exact feel this redesign removes. The real counts still reach a screen reader through the `progressbar` role, which is how the old invisible bar was fixed once already. The gate bites if a visible count creeps back.

- [x] THE SEND BUTTON NOW HAS A REAL DISABLED STATE, and that is the point rather than decoration: it is the only feedback that Enter will do nothing on an empty box. Dim until there is something to send, then accent-on-accent. Also a circle now, not a squircle — v2 overrides v1 on that, on the active-press scale (.92 not .94), on the input fill (filled with a neutral hairline that turns mint on FOCUS, so the accent means "you are here" rather than being permanently on), and on the typing dots (mint, not grey — grey read as the app buffering rather than the coach composing).

- [x] **THE PLACEHOLDER NOW FOLLOWS THE QUESTION** — "Tell me your goal…", "Gym, home, or a mix?", "How long have you got?". With no buttons on screen this is the only standing hint at what KIND of answer fits. Each hint is owned by its slot (`inputHint` on `SlotDef`, 20 of them) rather than hard-coded in the view, and it resolves from the SAME canonical-next slot the stuck-rescue and dead-air guard use, so all three agree on what the coach is waiting for instead of each guessing separately. The gate rejects a hint written as a field label ("Goal:"), which would put the form's vocabulary straight back on screen.

- [ ] FIVE GATE FAILURES ON THE FIRST v2 RUN WERE ALL THE GATE, NOT THE CODE — moved anchors, a search window 270 characters too short, and an unscoped `border-t` check that flagged the review card's own paragraphs. Worth writing down because the sixth was real: the user bubble legitimately stopped using `bg-secondary` when the fill moved into a class, and the gate now asserts the gradient is still `--secondary`-derived rather than just that a class name is present. A sixth mutation test also silently did nothing — the pattern never matched multi-line JSX — and had to be redone; a mutation that fails to apply looks exactly like a gate with no teeth.

- [ ] **`test:audit` IS 2 / 13,967 ON `main` FROM THIS MERGE, AND THAT IS A KNOWN, ACCEPTED STATE — not a fresh regression.** Both failures are `rotation_relative_load`: `home_gym / none / 45-60 / hybrid` and `minimalist / none / 45-60 / hybrid`, where a rotation lands "Band Lateral Raise" → "Lateral Raises" at 200% and "Step-Down (Eccentric)" → "Walking Lunges" at 133% of a fresh estimate, past the 125% category band. **The equipment-quality feature EXPOSES this bug rather than causing it.** Ashley was given the choice — leave equipment-quality on the branch, ship it anyway, or fix the load bug first — and chose to ship now, with the consequence stated. Fixing it is still worth doing and, being load prescription, gets a plan before a build.

- [x] **THE COACH NO LONGER SPEAKS FROM A BUBBLE** — text-only onboarding design implemented from Ashley's handoff. Two bubbles facing each other read as messaging furniture; the point of this screen is a person talking. So the coach is now plain text on the canvas at 19px/1.6 under a small COACH mark, and only the user gets a fill (17px, compact, asymmetric corner). One voice reading as speech and one as a reply is what makes it feel like being talked to rather than like a transcript.

- [x] EVERY COLOUR IS A TOKEN, which is the handoff's own hardest constraint — *"never hard-code hex, the app has 4 themes × accent overrides"*. The design was drawn in Nightshift, so a hex lifted from it looks perfect there and is wrong in Ember, Field and Graphite; `--primary` is an accent override on top, so even the mint is not a constant. **`test:onboarding-style` fails on any hex in the file at all**, and bites when one from the mock is pasted in.

- [x] THE COACH MARK IS SUPPRESSED ON A RUN — **a deviation from the handoff, which labels every coach message.** In the mock each coach turn is separated by a user reply; in the real flow onboarding sends two or three in a row (the intro, the dead-air guard, the stuck rescue), and stacking COACH three times reads as three speakers rather than one person still talking. A receipt does not break a run either, or recording an answer mid-reply would re-label the coach's own next sentence.

- [x] THE TYPING INDICATOR REPLACES A SINGLE "…" CHARACTER, which read as a rendering glitch more than as someone typing. Three 7px dots, 1.2s loop, 0.15s stagger, a 3px lift — deliberately far smaller than Tailwind's `animate-bounce`, which lifts 25% and reads as a toy. Kept OUT of the glow system on purpose: this is motion, not a halo, so the "Off" glow setting must not be able to switch it off. Reduced motion stops the movement and keeps the dots visible — removing them would leave a silent gap where the app is in fact working.

- [x] SEEN BEFORE SHIPPING, not just typechecked: `render:screens` gained an `onboarding-conversation` screen at 412px. The first render pushed the composer and the typing dots off the bottom of the frame — the two things most worth checking — so the fixture was trimmed until they were visible.

- [ ] **ONE THING IN THE HANDOFF WAS NOT DONE, and it contradicts a ruling Ashley gave earlier the same day.** The handoff says *"No tappable answer options anywhere in onboarding — free text only."* Asked directly, she chose **"only when you're stuck"** over "never — always just text", and that is what shipped: the coach asks and waits, and chips appear only on a bare "I don't know", an unmappable answer, or a re-ask. Left as-is rather than silently reversing her own decision — it needs her word, not mine.

- [ ] A SECOND HANDOFF DETAIL IS FACTUALLY OFF: it says the onboarding prompt should *"NEVER emit `[QUICK_REPLIES: ...]`"*. `[QUICK_REPLIES]` is the COACH CHAT's mechanism; onboarding never used it — it renders chips through the `present_slot` tool. The intent was already handled by the earlier change; no prompt edit was needed for this.

- [x] **"NEVER GIVE ME BURPEES" NOW WORKS WHEN SAID IN ONBOARDING** — it previously did nothing at all. Found by tracing where every onboarding answer ends up, after Ashley pointed out that text-only onboarding means people volunteer things the buttons never asked for. Saying *"I won't eat mushrooms"* landed in `user_facts` and was kept out of every meal; *"never give me burpees"*, said in the same breath, had **nowhere to go**. Exercise exclusions compile from `user_facts` rows tagged `exercise_preference`, and the only two writers were the coach chat and the ⇄ button. **Onboarding never wrote one** — so a person could rule out a food and an exercise together and have only the food honoured, until they happened to say it again later on a different screen.

- [x] IT IS THE MIRROR OF `dislikedFoods`, DELIBERATELY, down to the `createFact` shape — same kind, same `source: 'onboarding'`, same hard dislike — so both paths land in exactly one place rather than creating a second concept for `compileExerciseExclusions` to disagree with.

- [x] **NEVER PROACTIVELY ASKED, unlike its food twin.** Onboarding had just been made conversational so it would stop marching through questions; fixing a capture gap by adding a new question would take that straight back. It sits in the catalogue (like `favoriteCuisines`/`breakfastStyle`) so the model records it the instant someone volunteers it, and the prompt rule explicitly forbids asking for it. The gate asserts both halves — that it's never asked, and that `dislikedFoods` still IS.

- [x] **THE HALF THAT IS EASY TO MISS: the first plan.** The `user_facts` rows are written AFTER the plan is generated, and `exerciseExclusions` is `[]` for a brand-new signup — so wiring only the database would have handed someone a first mesocycle containing the exact exercise they had just ruled out, with the exclusion silently taking effect weeks later. The in-memory answer is resolved and passed into BOTH `generateExercisePlan` and `generateMesocycle`. **This is the same half-landed shape the trace report had just flagged elsewhere, caught in the act of building it.**

- [x] AN UNRECOGNISED PHRASE EXCLUDES NOTHING, rather than excluding something approximate. "Those jumpy squat things" resolves to no catalogue name, so it contributes no exclusions — but it still gets a fact row, so the answer is never lost and shows on the Profile screen. Guessing an exercise from an unmatched phrase would remove work someone never asked to have removed.

- [x] THE GATE IS BEHAVIOURAL, NOT TEXTUAL: `test:onboarding-exercise-dislikes` generates a REAL plan with the dislike applied and asserts the exercise is gone — against a control run proving it was there to begin with, so the section cannot pass vacuously on an exercise the engine never picks. Proven to bite on four mutations, including both "plan not told" cases separately.

- [ ] **AND THE GATE'S OWN FIRST RUN WAS A FALSE POSITIVE I HAD TO FIX.** It reported that an unrecognised phrase changed the plan. It hadn't — plan generation draws on a random source, so two identical calls differ on their own, and I had compared one unseeded run against another. Seeded with a fixed key and it passes three runs running. Recorded because `test:injury-rebuild` misfired exactly this way once already: a coin-flip gate is worse than a red one, because it makes unrelated work look guilty.

- [ ] STILL OPEN from the same trace, and NOT built: soft preferences ("I prefer chicken to fish") are compiled into a list that **nothing calls** — the comment claims it feeds swap-candidate ranking and it does not; wire it or delete it, but "neither" is how the next person gets misled. The five untagged allergens (celery, sesame, mustard, lupin, sulphites) and injuries outside the eight body areas both remain remembered-only; by the repo's own rule those are safety-path work and need a plan before a build.

- [x] **THE ONBOARDING WAS A QUESTIONNAIRE BECAUSE THE CODE MADE IT ONE** — fixed. Ashley: *"the onboarding feels too much like a questionnaire because it is that. a real coach wouldn't be sending you buttons to click. they'd be waiting for a text reply."* She was right, and it was literal. **A second Gemini call, with function calling FORCED (`mode: "ANY"`), existed for the sole purpose of stapling chips onto any question the model had chosen not to put chips under.** Its own comment read *"chips must not depend on the model remembering to ask for them"* — exactly right for a form, exactly wrong for a conversation. Deleted. That also removes an entire extra model round trip from nearly every turn, so replies get cheaper and faster.

- [x] THREE FORCES, NOT ONE. The forced leg was the third; the other two were wording. `present_slot`'s own description said *"call this whenever you ask a closed-set question"*, and the SLOT MECHANICS rule said *"Closed-set question → ask it in your own words AND call present_slot"*. Between them, essentially every question in the flow arrived with a menu attached. Chips are now described as a **rescue** with exactly three cases: they said they don't know or asked what the options are, the answer was too ambiguous to map, or the same question has already been asked once and is still unanswered.

- [x] ASHLEY'S RULING WAS "ONLY WHEN YOU'RE STUCK", over two alternatives (never; or keep them for the list-shaped ones). Worth recording what that trades: the honest cost is that "which days can you train" now means typing rather than tapping seven weekdays, and onboarding may take a few more turns. What it does NOT trade is safety — see below.

- [x] **A PROMPT-ONLY VERSION OF THIS WOULD HAVE BEEN THE fa683fc MISTAKE AGAIN.** That commit loosened this same prompt, worked on its own terms, and made the model stop replying on 4 of 7 turns — an effect nobody predicted, and it was reverted. So the stuck case has a deterministic half that does not involve the model at all: `isStuckMessage` catches a bare *"I don't know" / "not sure" / "what are my options"* and the client renders that slot's card itself. Someone who types "I don't know" and gets another paragraph of prose back is worse off than before chips were removed — that is the one moment a list genuinely helps, and withholding it would be stubbornness rather than conversation.

- [x] THE DETECTOR IS DELIBERATELY NARROW, and that is the check with teeth. It must match the WHOLE message: *"I don't know"* is someone asking for help, *"I don't know, maybe three days a week?"* is an answer with a hedge in front of it, and burying that under a chip grid would throw away what they just said. `test:onboarding-conversational` asserts both directions on five hedged answers, and bites when the anchors are loosened.

- [x] **NOTHING ON THE SAFETY PATH DEPENDED ON CHIPS, which is what made this safe to do at all.** `detectAllergenTags` scans every typed message deterministically, independent of the model, and lands the matching "-free" tag on `dietaryPreferences` — the only thing meal generation actually filters against. `tryExactLabelMatch` records a typed answer that matches an option exactly, with the same certainty a tap gives. Both untouched, both asserted by the new gate. Every anti-stall path is untouched too: a value that fails validation still re-asks with chips, the dead-air guard still renders the next question with its card, `pickSlotToForce` still steps in on a stall. Those were rescues already — they were never the questionnaire.

- [ ] MEASURE IT BEFORE TRUSTING IT — needs Ashley's machine, since `*.supabase.co` is unreachable from the sandbox and the probe calls the DEPLOYED function against the real Gemini API. New fixed persona `scripts/probe-personas/conversational.json`: nine turns, every answer phrased the way someone would actually type it rather than as a chip label, with a deliberate bare "I don't know" at turn 6 which is the one turn that SHOULD produce chips. The probe now prints **turns that offered chips**, alongside the numbers it already printed — required slots still missing, and empty-reply turns, which is the metric fa683fc failed on.

- [x] **THE COACH NOW SAYS WHAT IT IS FOR, AND SHOWS IT RATHER THAN LISTING IT** — Ashley: *"I want it to clearly explain in the first few messages what it does, how it can help and how the user can use it."* Half A of `docs`-planned work, client copy only, no model involved. The first-ever chat now opens with four short messages — who this is, what to say to it, what it will not do without asking, then today — and three starter chips under the last one. **Deliberately NOT a feature list**: a brand-new user does not need an inventory of 22 tools, they need to know what KIND of thing to type, so the how-to-use-it half is carried by chips rather than prose.

- [x] A CHIP CANNOT OVERSTATE THE WAY A DESCRIPTION CAN, which is the whole reason they are chips. Each one is a sentence the user actually sends — `handleQuickReply` puts the text in the composer and hits send — so tapping it demonstrates the real thing instead of describing it. One per KIND rather than three of the same: *"Talk me through today"* (explain, answered from plan context), *"Swap an exercise"* (change, `propose_exercise_swap`, a card she confirms), *"There's a food I won't eat"* (remember, `record_fact`).

- [x] **NOTHING ABOUT THE SCHEDULE OR THE VOLUME IS OFFERED, AND A GATE NOW HOLDS THAT LINE.** `adjust_volume` and `update_workout_schedule` are stubs that reply *"I can't safely make plan changes yet"*. A chip is a suggestion in the app's OWN voice — it has no honesty rule the way the model does — so one landing on a stub would teach a new user, in their first-ever interaction, that the coach says no to obvious asks. `test:coach-promises` §6 makes every chip NAME the tool it routes to and proves that tool is declared, executed and not a stub; it also asserts both stubs still decline, so the day either becomes real, the gate turns red and points at the chip list. Proven to bite on five separate mutations.

- [x] **THE MIDDLE MESSAGE WAS A NINE-LINE WALL, AND ONLY `render:screens` COULD SEE IT.** Every check passed — types, gates, tool routing — while the message meant to replace "one block of text" was itself a block of text taller than the other two combined at 412px. Split at the natural seam (what you say / what I'll do with it) into 6 and 3 lines. The words moved to `src/lib/first-run-intro.ts` so the harness renders the REAL strings rather than a replica that drifts: the chrome around them is copied class-for-class and can drift, the copy cannot, because there is only one copy.

- [ ] **A CORRECTION: the intro is not localStorage-based and does not replay on a cleared browser.** I flagged that in the plan. `isFirstEverChat` is set from the Supabase `chat_messages` COUNT (`ChatAssistant.tsx:463`), not from the cache — so it follows the user across devices correctly, and replays only until they send their first message, which is arguably right (someone who never replied has not been introduced). I reached the wrong answer by reading the WRITE path — the intro is `setMessages` only, with no DB insert, which is true — and inferring the read path from it instead of looking at where the flag is set.

- [ ] OBSERVED, NOT CHANGED: the three chips stack one per row on a phone. Quick replies render inside the message's `max-w-[80%]` and behind the `pl-9` avatar offset, leaving ~294px, which no two of these fit in. That constraint is shared chat chrome — it applies to every model-supplied quick reply too, not just these — so widening it is a change I did not make unasked and cannot verify from here. Legible and tappable as it stands; worth a decision if the column starts reading as a menu.

- [x] **THE APP SAID "ACTION FAILED" ABOUT A CHANGE THAT HAD ALREADY LANDED** — fixed. Ashley, minutes after the migration went in: *"I missed the gym but I did muay thai instead"* → *"Done — that day is marked as Muay Thai instead of lifting."* followed immediately by *"Action failed — the change was not applied."* **The database write succeeded.** chat-gemini only emits an action when its own write returned `dbSuccess`, and it emitted one. `swap_session_for_activity` was missing from the client's `PlanAction` union, so `applyPlanAction` fell through to `return false` and the client appended the failure line.

- [x] THE EXACT INVERSE OF THE DEFECT THE TOOL WAS BUILT FOR. `swap_session_for_activity` exists because the coach used to *claim* a change it never made. It now made the change and claimed it had not — strictly worse than silence, because a user who believes it failed will do it again or give up.

- [x] **AND THE LESSON WAS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN, IN THE SAME FUNCTION, TWELVE LINES ABOVE.** `log_workout_set` had this identical bug and carries a comment saying so: *"this type was missing from the PlanAction union, so applyPlanAction fell through to `return false` below and the user was told 'Action failed' about a write that had already landed."* Same file, same fall-through, one action type later. **A comment did not prevent the recurrence, so it is now a gate.** New `npm run test:chat-actions` parses every literal `action: { type: … }` the edge function returns and fails if any lacks a `PlanAction` interface or an `applyPlanAction` branch. Proven to bite: renaming the handler turns it red on two checks.

- [ ] SCOPE STATED RATHER THAN OVERCLAIMED: the gate covers the LITERAL action types — the `dbSuccess ? { type: "…" } : undefined` returns, which are precisely the "already written, now told it failed" class. It does **not** cover index.ts's final fall-through, `action: { type: name, ...args }`, which emits the tool's own name computed at runtime; those cannot be enumerated statically and most route through the proposal/confirm flow instead. A wider audit of that path is a separate piece of work.

- [x] **THE LOAD-CEILING FEATURE NAMED A TABLE THAT HAS NEVER EXISTED** — fixed. Migration `20260826140000` said `alter table profiles` and `load-ceiling-prompt.ts` said `supabase.from('profiles')`. **The table is `fitness_profiles`, and all 46 other migrations say so.** Both halves were mine, written in the same round. Found only because Ashley ran the migration against a real database and it threw `relation "profiles" does not exist` — on TEST, loudly, before production, which is exactly what the TEST project is for.

- [x] IT WOULD HAVE FAILED SILENTLY, FOREVER. The write path's `isMissingColumnError` guard matches `/schema cache/`, and PostgREST's reply for an absent TABLE is *"Could not find the table 'public.profiles' in the schema cache"* — so the guard I wrote to make a pending migration harmless would have swallowed a permanent bug and reported "needs migration" to a screen that shows nothing. A guard meant to tolerate a temporary absence was hiding a typo.

- [x] NOTHING COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT, so a gate now does. `tsc` cannot — a table name is a string. The other 55 gates cannot — they exercise the pure engine and never touch a database. New `npm run test:table-names` parses every migration for the tables it CREATES, then checks every `alter table` and every `supabase.from('…')` in `src/` and the edge functions against that set. 28 tables created, 28 altered, 24 queried, all resolve. Proven to bite: reintroducing the typo turns it red on two checks naming the file.

- [ ] **A CORRECTION: the Supabase dashboard's "LAST MIGRATION" is not the newest applied one.** Both projects displayed `allow_absent_body_metrics` (17 Aug), from which I told Ashley to expect FOUR pending migrations. The CLI then listed exactly ONE. The other three were already applied; the dashboard field means something else. **The CLI's pending list is the only reliable answer** — do not infer migration state from that panel again.

- [x] **`tier1_compound` MEANT "NEEDS A BARBELL", NOT "IS A MAIN LIFT"** — and that single conflation was the whole 37.5%.
