All 8 tier-1 entries are barbell lifts or the two pull-up-bar movements, so **Pistol Squat Progression, Nordic Hamstring Curl and Chest Dips — three of the hardest things a person can do without a gym — sat at tier-2 alongside Air Squat, Incline Push-Ups and Glute Bridge**, and Dumbbell Bench Press ranked level with a Push-Up. The catalogue was not missing the hard movements; it was mis-ranking them. That also exposed a limit in the promotion shipped an hour earlier: it broke ties on LIST POSITION, so it could not tell a Pistol Squat from an Air Squat.

- [x] RANKED WITHOUT RETAGGING — Ashley's call, taking the safe half of two options. New `movement-difficulty.ts` carries an anchor-only difficulty bump (2 = "this IS the main lift for whoever can only reach it", 1 = "a harder-than-baseline variant"), read by exactly ONE thing: which exercise a day nominates as its anchor. **`mechanics_tier` is untouched**, because it drives sets, reps, rest brackets, load ceilings and several quality checks — retagging would have moved prescriptions for gym users who never had this problem. Tier still multiplied by 10 so it always dominates: a tier-2 can never outrank a tier-1 however hard it is.

- [x] **MY OWN COMMENT PREDICTED THE BUG AND WAS WRONG.** The per-exercise floor reads an anchor computed from the day's BASE names, and I had written that this was safe because *"rotation is within-tier, so the RANK the anchor is chosen on cannot change — only the name."* True while the anchor ranked on tier alone; false the moment difficulty entered the ordering, since a rotation swapping Air Squat for Goblet Squats is within-tier AND changes which movement is hardest. **97 days landed the floor on the wrong exercise.** Fixed with one post-pass over the day as it finally stands — `trimWeekRestForBudget` re-derives correctly but only visits days that are OVER budget, so it could not cover this.

- [x] SPANISH SQUAT IS DELIBERATELY UNRANKED, and the gate says so by name. It is a patellar-tendon rehab tool the injury pass places on purpose; promoting it to a day's MAIN LIFT would turn a rehab prescription into the centrepiece of the session. **0 promoted across 1,088 anchored days.** The difficulty list is hand-maintained and unavoidably editorial — there is no property of an entry to derive "a Nordic curl is brutal" from — so the mitigation is a check that every one of the 39 ranked names still exists in the catalogue, making a rename fail loudly instead of silently dropping a movement back to baseline.

- [x] Verified: `test:audit` **0 / 13,967**. Sessions past the stated maximum **0 / 0 / 0**, under-minimum 5 / 18 / 6 — both unchanged from the promotion round. 1,088 anchored, **0 under 60s**, 0 in a superset, 55 already above 60s left alone. `test:main-lift-rest` sections 5-7, plus 24 further gates.

- [x] 37.5% OF TRAINING DAYS HAD NO MAIN LIFT — promotion built. Found because `render:screens` put a bodyweight day on screen with no MAIN LIFT label. Measured: **96 of 256 days contain no tier-1 exercise at all** — full_gym 0, home_gym 0, minimalist 48 of 64, bodyweight 48 of 64. The cause is arithmetic: **only 8 of 145 catalogue entries are `tier1_compound` and 6 of the 8 need a barbell**, so without one the only tier-1s reachable are Pull-Ups and Chin-Ups. A home trainee's pull day had a main lift; every squat, press and hinge day had none. Plan: `docs/PLAN-the-day-with-no-main-lift.md`. Ashley's ruling: **both, promotion first** — promote now, add real non-barbell tier-1 movements as a separate content round.

- [x] IT WAS BEHAVIOUR, NOT DECORATION. `MAIN_LIFT_REST_FLOOR_SECONDS` keys on `tier1_compound`, so on those days **nothing received the 60-second floor**. The round that shipped that floor was called "the day's hardest lift keeps its rest" and Ashley's ruling was *a full minute, every hard lift, bodyweight included* — it protected a bodyweight trainee's pull-up day and left their leg day on accessory rest. Now the day's hardest STANDALONE movement is promoted: 1,088 of 3,072 days had no tier-1, **all 1,088 got an anchor, 0 still rest under 60s**.

- [x] **THE FIFTH PATH — the same lesson, one round later.** Wiring the four call sites was not the job. Measuring the outcome found **227 promoted anchors still under 60s**: `trimWeekRestForBudget` trims last and floors anything not `isMain` at 30, so anchors were floored upstream and walked straight back down. The previous round's own comment reads *"a constraint asserted at three paths, missed at the fourth"*. Caught only because the verification asked what the plans actually contained rather than whether the edits compiled.

- [x] TWO INVARIANTS THAT STOP THE APP CONTRADICTING ITSELF. **No superset member is ever promoted** (0 of 1,088) — a superset prints *"alternate — no rest between"* directly under its members, so promoting one and giving it a 60s floor would print both claims in one card; 128 days whose hardest movement is paired keep no main lift instead. And **promotion is a FLOOR, never a ceiling**: 49 anchors already resting above 60s are left alone rather than pulled down, the same one-way rule the stated load ceilings follow.

- [x] **A REGRESSION I CLAIMED AND HAD TO RETRACT.** I reported that this broke `test:injury-rebuild`'s "the two modes genuinely differ" check. It did not. **That gate does not seed its RNG** — three runs of identical code gave 452, 452 and 436 rebuilt slots against a fixed 448, passing twice and failing once with nothing changed. I compared one run against one run and concluded a regression. Seeded, the answer is **436 / 448 both with and without the change** — no effect, failure genuinely pre-existing (and the profile is full_gym, where every day already has a tier-1, so promotion cannot fire there at all). Seeding added: a coin-flip gate is worse than a red one because it makes unrelated work look guilty. What it ASSERTS is untouched.

- [ ] FLAGGED, not fixed — **a carry or an isometric hold can now be labelled MAIN LIFT** (Loaded Backpack Walk 112 days, Farmer Squat Hold 80). On a day whose focus is literally "Squat & Carry" the carry genuinely is the hardest thing, so it is defensible, but no coach would call it a main lift. Also: the promoted anchor sits wherever the generator placed it, so MAIN LIFT can appear third in the list, below a superset. Both are consequences of "the day's hardest movement" being a simple rule, and inventing per-prescription-type exceptions is how a simple rule becomes unreviewable. Ashley's call.

- [x] ONE ICON MEANT BOTH "I EAT FISH" AND "I CANNOT EAT FISH" — fixed. The new screen-render harness put the real onboarding questions on screen for the first time, and 🐟 was serving **both Pescatarian and Fish-Free** in the same 17-option list, on the allergen path. 💪 was serving **both Shoulders and Elbows** in the list that drives injury filtering. `value` was correct in both cases — only the picture was wrong, which is exactly why no existing gate could see it: every other check in this repo reasons about tags and values. Ashley's call, offered against three alternatives: change the odd row out. Pescatarian → 🍣 (every other exclusion already wears the food it excludes, so Pescatarian was the row breaking the convention); Elbows → 🤜 (💪 is a flexed bicep, which reads as upper arm); Lower Back → 🧍, replacing 🔙, the "BACK" arrow — a pun on the English word that means "go back" everywhere else the user has seen it.

- [x] NO VALUE MOVED, and the gate asserts it. Icons are presentation; `value` is the enforcement contract (diet-rules' `FORBIDDEN_TAGS` keys, and the injury codes every filter matches on). New `npm run test:option-icons` checks no two options in one question share an icon, **exempting uniform lists by the property "all of them are identical"** rather than by naming slots — `trainingDays` is seven 📅 and `mealsPerDay` three 🍽️, where the label carries all the meaning and no one can be misled about which is which. A named allowlist would have to be edited to stay honest and would eventually be edited to stay quiet. Proven to bite: restoring 🐟 on Pescatarian turns it red on two checks, then green again. `test:diet-tag-sync`, `test:meal-roundtrip`, `test:injury-separation`, `test:injury-adaptation-safety`, `test:joint-tags` and `test:rehab-prescribed` all pass.

- [x] AND A SUSPICION THAT DID NOT SURVIVE LOOKING. From the contact sheet I thought the 17-option dietary list and the 10-option cuisine list would push their Done button below the fold on a phone. Rendered at full height: both fit, allergen disclaimer and all, with room to spare. Recorded because the harness is as useful for killing a plausible worry cheaply as for finding a real defect — the alternative was a speculative layout change to something that was never broken.

- [x] I CAN SEE THE APP NOW — `npm run render:screens`. Three UI defects this week were found the same way: Ashley opened the deployed app on her phone, hit something broken, sent a screenshot, and we spent a deploy round-trip on it. Two of them were sitting in the markup the whole time — the composer below the fold behind the keyboard, and a row reading **"B · 3&times;9-11 &middot; 25kg"** where "B" was Backpack Row. **Every gate passed while the app showed a one-letter exercise name**, because every gate reasons about DATA (is the load right, does the rest floor hold, is the audit clean) and a layout defect is invisible to an assertion about kilograms. This renders the REAL components with REAL generated plans at a REAL 412px and takes a picture. 23 screens.

- [x] IT FOUND A REAL ONE ON ITS FIRST RUN. **A peeked day never told you a superset was a superset.** Today's shows a glowing mint rail, `A1`/`A2` numbering, and the line *"alternate — no rest between"*; another day showed two plain rows both badged a bare "A", no rail, and **no alternate line at all** — which is an instruction for how to train the pair, not decoration. PeekPanel's own header comment promised the two surfaces "cannot drift apart again" because `ExerciseLine` is shared; that was true of the ROW and false of everything around it. Fixed by extracting `SupersetShell` — the chrome, taking a render function, since the two callers genuinely cannot share a row type (today's knows about logged sets; the peek's must not).

- [x] AND ITS FIRST FINDING WAS ABOUT ITSELF, which is worth more than the bug. The first run showed "Barbell Squats" labelled ACCESSORY with no MAIN LIFT anywhere — a convincing screenshot of a defect that does not exist. `generateExercisePlan` returns exercises with `tier` **undefined**; only `generateMesocycle` assigns them, and the app only ever renders mesocycle days. I was photographing a state no user can reach. **A screenshot of the wrong data is a confident picture of nothing** — recorded in the harness so the next reader suspects the props before the component.

- [ ] WHAT IT DELIBERATELY CANNOT DO, stated so the coverage is not overread: it uses `renderToStaticMarkup`, so **effects never run** and every component renders in its initial state — anything that only appears after an effect is out of scope, as is anything needing live session context (`RestDayCard` is skipped BY NAME rather than silently). No touch, no scrolling, no keyboard. It would not have caught either of this week's onboarding bugs. It is the cheap pass before Ashley's phone, not a replacement for it. Also **not** pixel-diffed against goldens on purpose: a golden gate on a UI still being designed fails on every intentional change and gets muted within a week.

- [x] AN EXERCISE ROW COULD CRUSH ITS OWN NAME TO ONE LETTER — fixed. Ashley, from a phone: *"you can't see the exercises until you click into it to display it."* Literally true — a row read **"B · 3&times;9-11 &middot; 25kg &middot; 2s down &middot; drive up"**, and "B" was Backpack Row; expanding the row was the only way to learn its name. CAUSE: the summary was `shrink-0` while the name was `min-w-0 truncate`, so 100% of any shortfall came out of the name. The summary is unbounded — sets, reps, load, assistance, added load AND a tempo phrase — so a tempo'd accessory ate the whole line. It looked fine on today's screen only because those rows happened to carry shorter summaries; **same component, same bug, and today truncated too** ("Single-Leg Dumbbell C…"). Now `flex-wrap` with a name box that is NOT `min-w-0`, so the name's nowrap text sets the row's min-content and the SUMMARY drops to a second line instead.

- [x] EVERY DAY GETS TODAY'S HEADLINE. Ashley: *"it shows the focus of the day ie squat and carry in nice big bold writing. I want all days to look like that but still make it obvious which is the current day's plan."* A peeked day was one `text-sm` line ("Friday · Full Body Power") against today's 36px bold hero. **What still separates today, now that the type matches:** the eyebrow is mint-with-glow reading "Today · Wednesday" versus a muted grey "Friday"; today has Start session where a peeked day has an X; today has the session-progress line and a day that is not today has no progress to show. The headline is 30px rather than 36px on purpose — identical size in a different colour reads as a bug, a clear step down reads as hierarchy.

- [x] VERIFIED BY RENDERING IT, not by reasoning about flexbox. No phone here and no Supabase, but Chromium is installed — built a static harness with the app's own compiled CSS, the real class strings and the real 412px width, and screenshotted before/after. It reproduced Ashley's exact "Bac…", "Archer Pus…", "Step-Up (E…" from the code alone, then showed them whole. Also settled a judgement call by rendering both: a wrapped summary right-aligned floats awkwardly mid-row, left-aligned it reads as a caption under the name. **Chose from the picture rather than from a guess.**

- [x] ONBOARDING WOULDN'T STAY AT THE BOTTOM OF THE CHAT — fixed. Ashley, testing the deployed keyboard fix on a real phone: *"the box stays above the keyboard but I have to keep scrolling to the bottom of the chat."* Auto-scroll was `useEffect(..., [messages, busy, reviewOpen])` — it scrolled when the message list changed, but in this screen the height keeps growing AFTER that: the question renders, THEN its `SlotChipsCard` renders the option grid beneath it, so **the buttons that answer the question land off-screen**. Multi-selects grow as options are toggled with no new message at all, the review card opens, and the keyboard both shrinks the container (`100dvh`) and grows its padding — none of which is a message.

- [x] AGAIN, THE FIX ALREADY EXISTED ONE COMPONENT OVER, and its comment says so in as many words: `ChatAssistant`'s reads *"ResizeObserver-based scroll instead of setTimeout/useEffect[messages]"*. Onboarding was still running the approach that had been tried and abandoned. Ported the same pattern — observe the CONTENT BOX rather than the data, `requestAnimationFrame` so the scroll runs after layout, `instant` rather than `smooth` so repeated fires don't fight each other, plus a near-bottom guard (120px) so someone re-reading an earlier answer is never yanked away. `sendMessage` forces past that guard: they just answered, so the reply is what they are waiting for.

- [ ] **THE PATTERN WORTH NOTICING: three bugs in a row were "the fix exists elsewhere in this app, onboarding didn't get it."** The keyboard inset, the auto-scroll, and (earlier) the composer's soft-disable. Onboarding is the first screen any user sees and the one with the least in-app traffic from us, so it drifts behind the screens we actually use. Worth a deliberate sweep comparing `ConversationalOnboarding` against `ChatAssistant` for anything else the latter learned and the former never heard about — rather than waiting for a fourth screenshot.

- [x] THE ONBOARDING TEXT BOX HID UNDER THE KEYBOARD — fixed. Reported by Ashley from a real phone while testing the live app: *"I have to scroll up to see the text input field."* Not a browser quirk. `ConversationalOnboarding` was wrapped in `min-h-screen` (100vh) with the composer as an ordinary last flex child, so when the soft keyboard opened the layout stayed full height while the visible area halved and the composer went below the fold. On Android 100vh also counts the collapsing URL bar, which is why it could need a scroll before anything was even focused. **This was the ONE screen in the app without the fix** — `useViewportInset` already exists and `ChatAssistant`, `BottomDock` and `BottomTabBar` all use it; onboarding, the first screen a new user ever sees, was missed.

- [x] FIXED WITH THE HOOK, NOT WITH `100dvh`, and the reason is written down in the hook already: iOS Safari resizes the VISUAL viewport but not the LAYOUT viewport, so a bottom-anchored element still ends up under the keyboard there. dvh alone would have looked right on Android and stayed broken on iPhone. The container moved to `min-h-[100dvh]` anyway (it fixes the URL-bar half), the composer is now `fixed` with a `useViewportInset` offset, and the scroll area yields `insetPx + 112` while the keyboard is up so the last chip card or the Generate button is never covered on iOS.

- [x] THE ONBOARDING FLOW — built. Ashley asked for an honest opinion of the whole thing: order, content, adaptivity, whether the quick-reply buttons are ugly. The verdict was that it is better than most fitness onboarding and had **one structural problem: the order**. Body metrics (age, height, weight, sex) sat at positions 22-25, behind NINE consecutive nutrition questions — the four values that drive every prescribed weight in the app, collected at the point of lowest attention. They now sit at 12-15, and the rule enforced is *everything the training half needs comes before anything the food half needs*. Plan: `docs/PLAN-the-onboarding-flow.md`. New gate `npm run test:onboarding-order`, new report `npm run report:slot-impact`.

- [x] THE ORDER GATE CAUGHT MY OWN PLAN BEFORE IT SHIPPED. `ONBOARDING_SLOTS` is an ordered array, and a slot whose `requiredIf` reads a LATER answer silently never appears — `isSlotApplicable` returns false rather than erroring. That defect has already happened once in this file (activityLevel sat last while gating the barbell chain, so an experienced lifter's volunteered numbers were discarded). The reorder plan's own written-out sequence **dropped activityLevel from before the barbell chain, recreating it exactly**. The gate derives each slot's dependencies by handing its predicate a Proxy and recording which keys it reads — a hand-written list would have been written from the same wrong mental model that produced the mistake.

- [x] **A CORRECTION: the plan's §4 was wrong and the file had already warned about it.** It proposed moving `favoriteCuisines` and `breakfastStyle` "out of onboarding" to first use. They were **already** in `NEVER_BLOCKING_SLOTS` and already filtered out of `trackedSlots`, so they were never proactively asked in the first place — §4 was solving a non-problem. Deleting them removed the only path by which a volunteered cuisine could be RECORDED, which the code's own comment warns about in as many words. It surfaced as a TypeError three files away; both are restored, and `test:onboarding-order` now fails BY NAME if a never-blocking slot is deleted rather than demoted.

- [x] NOT ONE QUESTION IS A CANDIDATE FOR CUTTING — measured, not asserted. New `report:slot-impact` varies each slot across all its values on a FIXED RNG seed (a per-value seed would measure the ±0.3 tie-break jitter, not the question) and counts distinct 16-week mesocycles, per equipment tier. Result: `fitnessGoal` 4/4, `trainingExperience` 4/4, `sessionDuration` 4/4, `trainingStyle` 4/4, `conditioningPreference` 3/3, `trainingDays` 5/5, `gender` 2/2, `weightKg` 3/3. The three that read inert for training — `activityLevel`, `heightCm`, `age` (bodyweight only) — all feed BMR/TDEE, so cutting one blanks somebody's calorie target. **Candidates after the nutrition filter: NONE.** That is the answer to "does it collect too much".

- [x] MEASURING KILLED BOTH OF MY "OBVIOUS" ADAPTIVE SKIPS. I proposed not asking a bodyweight trainee about training style (4 styles → **4 different plans**) and not asking a conditioning trainee about cardio (3 preferences → **3 different plans**). Both wrong, both refuted in two minutes. Which is why the deliverable is the report rather than a list of questions I felt confident about.

- [x] A STALE COMMENT WAS ARGUING TO GIVE TIRED PEOPLE MORE VOLUME. `recoveryCapacity` carried *"measured to have zero effect anywhere in the generated plan"* — true when written, false once `RECOVERY_SET_MULTIPLIER` landed. Re-measured on full_gym/intermediate/hypertrophy/4-day: base week **81 sets for all three** (that half still holds), 16-week mesocycle **low 912 / moderate 1125 / high 1125**. So "low" removes 213 sets, 19% of the block. NOTE THE DENOMINATOR CHANGE: an earlier note in this session quoted "58 vs 77 sets" from a different profile — those numbers are not comparable to these and should not be read as a regression.

- [ ] FLAGGED, NOT FIXED — **moderate and high recovery produce a byte-identical mesocycle** (1125 sets each) despite distinct multipliers (0.9 vs 1.0); the difference is absorbed by set-count rounding. So the question has three answers and two outcomes. Whether someone who reports high recovery should earn more volume than someone reporting moderate is a training call for Ashley, not a bug.

- [x] THE BUTTONS ARE NOT UGLY, and the "obvious" fix would have made them worse. `OptionCard`'s `compact` variant did two jobs at once — tighter padding AND no description — because every compact caller happened to have no descriptions. Adopting it on the four-option questions, as the plan proposed, would have silently deleted *"New to this, or coming back after a long break"* from the goal, experience and activity questions: the lines that let someone place themselves honestly instead of flatteringly, and which the same plan named as one of the three best things about the flow. Split into `compact` (size) and `showDescription` (content); four-option questions now tighten and keep their descriptions.

- [ ] FLAGGED, deliberately not decided — **the emoji**. 🌱📈🎯🏅 render differently on every platform and age badly. They do real work (fast visual scanning), so this is not a "rip them out" recommendation. It is the single thing most likely to make the app read as cheap beside a polished competitor, and it wants a designer and a deliberate call rather than a diff.

- [ ] FLAGGED — **every claim about WHERE people abandon onboarding is reasoning, not evidence.** The app has no funnel analytics, so "nine food questions is where drop-off happens" is a plausible hypothesis and nothing more. The reorder is defensible on its own terms (the most load-bearing questions should not be asked at the point of lowest attention), but it has not been validated against a single real user.

- [x] ASK WHAT YOU CAN ACTUALLY LOAD — built. The previous round stopped the app ASSERTING what it did not know; this one makes it ask. Ashley's ruling: **at first use, not onboarding** — someone who has never trained cannot answer "how much can you load", and onboarding is where people drop out. At most three questions ever exist (dumbbell, kettlebell, backpack) because `loadingMode` already classifies every exercise and a home trainee owns only those three; a barbell user is already asked their squat/bench/deadlift and a cable stack means a gym. Each appears ONLY when an exercise using that implement is in front of them, and at most one per session. Plan: `docs/PLAN-ask-what-you-can-actually-load.md`. New migration `20260826140000`, new gate `npm run test:load-ceilings` (28 checks).

- [x] DOWNWARD ONLY, and that asymmetry is the whole safety argument. A stated 15kg replaces the invented 50kg — a figure that described a *commercial gym rack*, which `LOADING_CEILING_KG_PER_HAND_OR_TOTAL`'s own comment already admitted and had deferred. A stated 200kg changes **nothing**, because those tables are also formula-regression backstops and this is the loading path. Same one-way rule the weigh-in offer used: new information may correct a load, never inflate one. PROVEN across 532 ceiling checks — **0 raised**. Verified end to end: 10kg dumbbells prescribe 10kg, an 8kg bag prescribes 8kg, and a claimed 99kg rucksack still prescribes 20kg.

- [x] THE PROPERTY THAT MATTERS MOST WHILE THE MIGRATION IS UNAPPLIED: **an unanswered profile prescribes identically to today, 0 differences across every loaded exercise x every equipment tier.** Ashley cannot run migrations from her machine right now, so this ships before the columns exist. Reads use optional chaining, writes fail soft through the same `isMissingColumnError` guard `added_load_kg` uses, and the prompt simply never appears. Nothing breaks; the app keeps guessing exactly as it always has.

- [x] DECLINING IS A VALUE, NOT AN ABSENCE — the body-metrics lesson, applied before it could bite again. `load_ceilings_declined` is its own column because null would otherwise mean both "not asked" and "refused", and the prompt would return forever. It silences **every** implement, not just the one on screen: someone who does not know what their dumbbells weigh does not know what their kettlebell weighs either, and asking again next session with a different noun is the same nag wearing a hat. Gated both ways, including that a declined trainee still gets a full, unchanged load — a refusal that degrades the product is not a real choice.

- [ ] FLAGGED, deliberately not built — **"which weights do you own", rather than "the heaviest"**. Far more useful: it would let the app round to a 10 or 15 the trainee actually has instead of a 12.5 they do not. Also a much harder question to answer and to build. The ceiling is the 80% version and choosing it was a decision, not an oversight. Likewise **re-asking when kit changes**: someone who buys heavier dumbbells has no way to tell the app.

- [x] THE APP TOLD PEOPLE TO ADD WEIGHT IT HAD NO REASON TO THINK THEY OWNED — fixed. Ashley's question: *"how do we know the user's backpack is 20kg or that they can add weight to it each week?"* **We do not, and we never ask.** Onboarding has one equipment question (four boxes) and the only weights a user can ever state are squat/bench/deadlift. The 20kg is `IMPROVISED_IMPLEMENT_CEILING_KG` — a hardcoded 8/12/20/25kg safety ceiling about straps and posture, not a fact about anyone's bag — and for most bodies the CLAMP is the number. Yet the guidance shown was identical to a barbell's: *"Starting estimate from strength standards for your bodyweight... too easy, add load next set."* Both halves untrue for a rucksack: the figure came from a ceiling rather than from them, and the instruction assumes weight that may not exist.

- [x] **A CORRECTION I OWED MID-ANSWER: I said "nothing checks". Wrong.** `getDoubleProgressionRecommendation` already reads the last logged sets and works from THAT number — `maxWorkingWeight(sessionSets)`, not the estimate — and `TodayPanel` calls it for every exercise carrying a load. So someone who logs 10kg is recommended 10kg next time, with a note saying why. The gap is far narrower than I first described: **session one only**, before anything is logged. Which is why Ashley's ruling was right and cheap — say the first number is a guess, and let the machinery that already exists do the rest.

- [x] TWO MESSAGES, SPLIT BY WHO ACTUALLY HAS A RACK. Improvised implements: *"A starting guess — I have no way to know what your bag actually holds. Load what you have, log it, and I will work from your number from next session."* home_gym/minimalist: *"...I do not know which weights you actually have. Use the closest thing you own..."* **full_gym is deliberately untouched** — there the original sentence is simply true. The second message closes a gap the code already documented and had deferred: `LOADING_CEILING_KG_PER_HAND_OR_TOTAL` records that its values *"are full-gym ceilings and do not scale down by equipment tier — a home_gym/minimalist trainee's actual adjustable dumbbells realistically top out well below 50kg"*. The app still prices home kit against a commercial rack; it now at least stops instructing them to add weight it has no reason to think they own. Verified reaching the screen: `basis` -> `load_guidance` -> `LoadChip`'s explainer, not a string that dies in the engine.

- [x] ONBOARDING IS ADAPTIVE, BUT BARELY — measured while answering Ashley's second question. Of 22 slots, exactly **4** branch, and they are one chain: equipment -> *"will you be lifting barbells?"* -> *"do you know your lifts?"* -> the three numbers. Goal, style, experience, cardio, recovery and diet change nothing about what is asked next. So the precedent for an equipment-conditional weight question already exists — which is why "ask in onboarding" was a real option, and why Ashley's steer that it need not go there was worth taking: the same fix lands with no new question at all.

- [ ] FLAGGED, still open — the app prices home dumbbells against a 50kg commercial rack. The wording is now honest but the NUMBER is still full-gym-shaped. Asking for a real ceiling (at first use, or in chat, rather than onboarding) remains the actual fix; this round only stops the app asserting something untrue while it does not know.

- [x] A BACKPACK AT ITS LIMIT — built. Someone whose only load is a weighted backpack runs out of progression and the app had nothing left to offer. MEASURED (`report:frozen-weeks`, 5,731 transitions): `Backpack Row` **91** and `Loaded Backpack Walk` **42** were 133 of 217 repeated weeks — 61%, the largest single contributor. Ashley's ruling: slow the movement down. Chosen over more sets (fights the time budget and the set-count rules), harder variations (the right answer in principle, but those movements do not exist yet — a content request wearing a progression hat), and telling the trainee to put more in the bag (honest, but reads as giving up on the person who picked bodyweight *because* they have nothing to add). Plan: `docs/PLAN-a-backpack-at-its-limit.md`.

- [x] THE CONDITION ALREADY EXISTED AND MEANT THE WRONG THING. `applyTempoPrescription` was built on the principle that *tempo is how a lift progresses when it has no load to add*, and it gated on `ex.suggested_load_kg != null` — "has no weight". A backpack row at its ceiling HAS a weight (20kg) and was skipped, even though 20kg is every kilogram it will ever have. Widened to "has no MORE weight": `isImprovisedLoadImplement(entry)` AND at/above `IMPROVISED_IMPLEMENT_CEILING_KG` (8/12/20/25kg by experience). **Ashley's earlier ruling survives untouched** — chin-ups and dips are still excluded because a belt can always take another plate, so slowing them would paper over a gap instead of closing it (and that gap was since closed by the added-load round). A backpack cannot. That is the whole distinction: one implement has more to give, one does not.

- [x] VERIFIED BY READING A REAL PLAN, not just a counter. bodyweight/upper_lower/intermediate `Backpack Row`: weeks 1-8 the weight climbs 12.5 -> 17.5 with NO tempo, week 9 it reaches the 20kg cap and tempo appears, `4-1-1` through the strength block and `3-0-1` through hypertrophy, absent on every deload. Gate extended with the over-fire check that matters: **675 backpack lifts at their ceiling get a tempo, 0 below their ceiling do**, and the original "no tempo on a lift that already shows a weight" keeps its teeth for every other lift (still 0). `test:audit` 0 / 13,967; `test:session-length` clean with no session past its stated maximum — the duration risk was real (the tempo round caused a regression once) and it did not recur.

- [ ] **THE METRIC CANNOT SEE THIS FIX, and the number is unchanged at 217.** `frozen_week` compares load AND reps; tempo is a third lever it does not read. So a trainee's backpack row genuinely gets harder from week 9 and the score is byte-identical — exactly the shape already flagged for added weight (`quality-score` reads `suggested_load_kg`, not `suggested_added_load_kg`). Two levers now progress lifts invisibly to the scorer. Worth fixing as one job rather than twice.

- [ ] FLAGGED, THE BIGGER HALF IS UNTOUCHED — `Loaded Backpack Walk` (42 of 217) gets nothing here and that is deliberate. It is a carry: both its levers are exhausted at once (weight at the improvised ceiling, distance at the 55m carry cap), and tempo is meaningless on a walk — you cannot take three seconds to lower a carry. So the honest ceiling on this round is **91 of 217 (42%)**, not 61%. Stretching tempo to cover it would have been worse than leaving it named.

- [ ] FLAGGED, a different defect found while tracing — `bodyweight/beginner` showed `Backpack Row` going **8kg -> 7.5kg while reps climbed**, a rep bought with a lighter weight. `test:frozen-weeks` already tracks this class (9 bad against a HEAD baseline of 10) and it is separate from running out of load.

- [x] A CORRECTION TO MY OWN FIRST READING, made before building rather than after. I reported these lifts as "pinned at the ceiling from day one" on the strength of one profile. Wrong: `Backpack Row` at bodyweight/intermediate climbs 12.5 -> 17.5 -> 20 with reps rising inside each block, and the novice cases (12kg flat, reps 8-10 -> 10-12 -> 12-14) are textbook double progression, not a defect. The genuinely stuck transitions are narrower and cluster at week 10->11, after the load has actually converged. Measuring one profile and generalising is what produced the wrong version.

- [x] TIME FIT WAS SCORED AGAINST A NUMBER NOBODY WAS PROMISED — fixed. **METRIC CHANGE, say it out loud: Time fit readings before this are NOT comparable with readings after.** A trainee says "30-45 minutes"; the generator aims at the 37-minute MIDPOINT, and the scorer judged every day against that midpoint — so a 45-minute session, which is exactly what they said they had, was reported as *"21% over budget (expected: 37min)"*. It was the largest single source of deductions after frozen weeks, and most of it was measuring against a target the trainee never agreed to. The promise is a RANGE, so that is what is judged now: anywhere inside [minimum, maximum] is full marks, overrun is measured from the MAXIMUM and underrun from the MINIMUM, both keeping their existing tolerance shapes. The generator still AIMS at the midpoint — that was never the wrong part, and aiming mid-range is what leaves room to land inside it. MEASURED: `time_fit` **2,749 (29.8%) -> 490 (5.3%)**, i.e. **82% of those flags were the ruler, not the plan**; Time fit **1.62 -> 1.92 / 2**; overall **11.28 -> 11.57 / 12**. The 490 that remain are genuinely outside what the trainee asked for. The deduction TEXT was fixed in the same pass — it reported "% over budget (expected 37min)" against a promise of 30-45, so the sentence contradicted the score.

- [ ] FLAGGED — **the same wrong denominator sits in a second place.** `dev-constraint-audit.ts` CHECK 3 uses `DURATION_BUDGET_SECONDS * 1.15`, which for a "30-45" trainee is 37 x 1.15 = **43 minutes**, so the audit rejects 43-45 minute sessions that the trainee explicitly said were fine. Deliberately NOT changed in this round: loosening a gate is a different risk from correcting a score, and this is the check that caught the Preacher Curl duration regression earlier today — which, on this reading, may not have been a regression at all. Worth deciding rather than assuming, and worth noting the honest implication for that entry.

- [ ] FLAGGED, attribution not isolated — `frozen_week` read **5,722** before this round's work and **5,855** after. The likeliest cause is the one-rep-target-per-lift-per-week fix: forcing a second slot to show the first slot's reps removes apparent week-to-week variation that was really just two slots disagreeing. If so it is the truth surfacing rather than a regression. NOT PROVEN — the catalogue additions landed in the same window and each sweep is ~26 minutes, so no clean single-variable measurement was taken. Named rather than attributed.

- [x] A GATE FOR THE UNIT OF EVERY LOAD CEILING — `npm run test:ceiling-units`, Ashley's call after the per-hand/total mix-up cost a correct exercise. Four properties, each of which would independently have caught it: every ceiling sits above the worst legitimate TOTAL in its category; with real headroom; the ceiling can actually FIRE; and each category's unit composition is printed so a category gaining a differently-united movement is visible in a diff. TEETH PROVEN by re-halving `isolation_shoulder` to 25 — **three checks fire**, each naming the cause in a sentence (*"ceiling is a TOTAL: 25kg >= 2x the 20kg worst per-hand value"*). That is the whole point: last time the same fault surfaced as 56 unexplained failures next to a stale comment blaming the loading formula, and the wrong explanation was believed. The gate reads the real exported table, not a copy — a gate duplicating the numbers it guards is the same defect class it exists to catch.

- [ ] FLAGGED BY THE NEW GATE, bigger than expected — **10 of 20 ceilings can never fire at all**, because the implement's physical clamp is lower and always gets there first (`deadlift` clamp 300 vs ceiling 325, `pulldown` 100 vs 180, `isolation_calf` 100 vs 140…). Half the load backstops are decorative. Not a defect today (the clamp is doing the work and warns when it fires) but it means the ceiling check is largely redundant with the clamp, and the two should probably be one mechanism. Reported per-category by the gate rather than failed, since it is a design question.

- [x] HOME-GYM AND MINIMALIST DEPTH — `Front Raises`, `Band Lateral Raise`, `Band Shrug`. MEASURED: `isolation_shoulder` at home_gym and minimalist went from **0 same-pattern alternatives (fallback-served) to 2**; full_gym 1 -> 4; `isolation_trap` 1 -> 2 or 3 at every tier. Across all four equipment tiers: **0 true dead ends, 0 patterns leaning on the nearest-pattern fallback, 0 patterns where the cap hides options** — the first time all three read zero. Both band entries are reachable at home_gym AND minimalist, which is why a band and a dumbbell were chosen over another machine. Neither carries a ceiling risk: a band is not in `LOADED_EQUIPMENT` so no kilogram figure exists, and Front Raises is a per-side dumbbell pair like the Lateral Raises beside it.

- [x] ONE REP TARGET PER LIFT PER WEEK — a pre-existing defect fixed to **0 from a HEAD baseline of 19**, not merely back to baseline. Observed directly: `Backpack Row` week 11, Monday 16-18 @20 / Tuesday 14-16 @20 / Thursday 16-18 @20 — same weight, same sets, same RPE, one rep bracket apart. CAUSE: the frozen-load rep bump memoised its SIZE per lift per week (and its comment claimed both slots would therefore agree) but the frozen VERDICT is resolved per SLOT, so a dissenting slot never entered that branch to read the memo. **The obvious fix made it worse** — memoising the verdict took the count 22 -> 37, because forcing a later slot into the frozen branch buys reps for lifts whose own figure genuinely moved. Measured, then reverted. What works is memoising the OUTCOME: the rep string itself, applied after every rep lever has settled and after the load is resolved, so it cannot move a single weight. `test:audit` 0 / 13,967.

- [x] **I SHIPPED A REGRESSION TO MAIN AND CAUGHT IT A ROUND LATE.** `test:band-slots` reads **340 against a 348 floor** — rehab-indicated bands reaching main slots — and it was already 340 at `083c749`, the machines commit that is now on main. That round ran twelve gates and this was not one of them, on a gate written earlier in this same session. VERIFIED BEFORE TOUCHING THE FLOOR, because lowering one is how a real regression gets hidden: `test:rehab-prescribed` passes in full, and **64 of 64** knee-injured profiles still receive a knee-indicated movement. Nobody lost their rehab; 8 instances stopped being the day's MAIN lift, from pool churn as the catalogue went 135 -> 145. Floor moved to 335 with the reasoning written into the file, AND a new property check added that catalogue size cannot move: every knee-injured profile gets something indicated for the knee (48 checked, 0 without, denominator asserted so it cannot pass vacuously).

- [x] THE SAFETY NET COMPARED PER-HAND WEIGHTS AGAINST TOTALS — fixed, and **this is a retraction of what I reported one round earlier.** I said the shoulder loading fraction (0.19 of bench) was over-prescribing and that `Machine Lateral Raise` had exposed it. **Wrong.** Swept across both sexes, 50-120kg, all four experience tiers and every rep bracket: the heaviest per-hand lateral raise the app ever prescribes is **20kg against a 25kg ceiling**. The fraction is fine. `prescribeLoad` stores a dumbbell pair PER HAND and a machine as a TOTAL, so the machine's honest 40kg total — the *identical real load* to the dumbbell's 20kg per hand — was compared against a ceiling calibrated in per-hand units. **The entry was correct and I deleted it as unsafe.** HOW I REACHED THE WRONG ANSWER: I found a comment in `dev-constraint-audit.ts` asserting the fraction was the real defect and the ceiling was deliberately left failing, it cited "40kg for a 120kg advanced male", I saw exactly 40kg, and I treated the match as confirmation instead of checking it. The per-hand sweep takes two minutes and says 20. The comment predates `isPerSideLoad` landing (the per-side round) — stale, not wrong when written. I also walked into the per-side defect class *while explicitly checking for it*: I verified `unilateral` against neighbouring entries and never against the ceiling's own unit.

- [x] `SAFETY_CEILING_KG` -> `SAFETY_CEILING_KG_TOTAL`, every value now a stated TOTAL, both sides normalised at the comparison. Ashley's ruling: teach it the difference rather than patch the one symptom. MEASURED: **10 of 20 categories were already mixed-unit**, so those ceilings had been total-shaped by accident (the largest observed value in a mixed category is always a total) while per-side entries went *under*-checked. Three categories contain only per-side movements and were per-hand-shaped: `isolation_shoulder` 25->50, `overhead_carry` 70->140, `single_leg_dumbbell` 60->120 — the same allowance, restated. PROOF NOTHING WAS LOOSENED, old rule vs new rule across the whole population: **14,789 verdicts unchanged, 0 now stricter, 11 now looser — and all 11 are `Machine Lateral Raise` itself.** No existing exercise's allowance moved at all. Headroom after: `isolation_shoulder` sits at 80% of its ceiling, exactly the 20-of-25 ratio it had before. `test:audit` 0 / 13,967 with the entry restored; catalogue 142.

- [ ] FLAGGED, pre-existing, not chased — `leg_press` sits at **exactly 100%** of its 400kg ceiling, because that ceiling and the leg-press implement clamp are both 400. The audit uses `>` so it never fires, which means the backstop is decorative for that one category: the clamp gets there first, every time. Not a defect today (the clamp is doing the work) but a ceiling that cannot fire is not a ceiling, and it should either move above the clamp or be deleted as redundant.

- [ ] FLAGGED — **there is no gate on the ceiling table's units.** The audit proves today's numbers pass; nothing would catch someone re-halving a ceiling, or adding a machine to a pure-per-side category, except 56 mystery failures and (as this round showed) a plausible wrong diagnosis. The transferable lesson from the rest-floor round applies here too: a constraint needs a check at every path, and "the unit of this number" is a constraint.

- [x] THE CATALOGUE RAN OUT IN THE GYM — first round built. Ashley, testing live: a machine was busy, she looked for another machine physically in front of her, and the app didn't offer it. TWO defects, failing in different situations. (1) The swap CAP: `MAX_SMART_REPLACEMENTS = 8` while full-gym chest had 14 legitimate alternatives. **This was the third round of the same defect** — the cap's own comment records it being raised from 5 to 8 after an earlier user dead-ended (flat bench busy, only incline offered, also busy). Raising it again would have been the same mistake a third time, so the COUNT is gone as a mechanism: the ranked list is now unbounded and `SwapDialog` keeps the "don't overwhelm" job it already did properly (top 4, then "Show N more", which now reaches everything). (2) The CATALOGUE: no flat chest press machine, no shoulder press machine, no plate-loaded row, no hip thrust. MEASURED, full gym: chest swap options **8 -> 14**, rows **8 -> 11**, overhead pressing **4 -> 5 and its first machine ever**. New `npm run report:swap-coverage`. Plan: `docs/PLAN-the-catalogue-runs-out-in-the-gym.md`.

- [x] I GOT THE HEADLINE WRONG FIRST AND CORRECTED IT BEFORE BUILDING. I reported that `isolation_shoulder` had **zero** alternatives at home_gym — *"a trainee prescribed Lateral Raises has nothing to swap to"*. **False.** `getSmartReplacements` falls back to `NEAREST_PATTERN_FALLBACK` when same-pattern comes back empty, so it really offers 5 (all vertical_push). **How I reached it:** I wrote my own same-pattern filter and read "0 same-pattern" as "0 offered", and I only ever ran the REAL function against full_gym — where same-pattern is never empty, so the fallback never fired and I never saw it. I had even read that fallback's code earlier (it carries a long comment about a neck-injury shrug regression) and still didn't connect it. `report:swap-coverage` now prints SAME and OFFERED as separate columns for exactly this reason, and flags fallback-served patterns as a quality signal rather than a dead end. **True dead ends across all four equipment tiers: 0.**

- [x] TWO ENTRIES WERE WRITTEN, MEASURED, AND PULLED BACK OUT. Both by gates, neither by reading. **(a) `Machine Lateral Raise` put the audit 56 failures into the red** against the 25kg `isolation_shoulder` safety ceiling — and it did NOT create that defect, it removed the cover from one already documented. `dev-constraint-audit.ts` says outright that the ceiling is *"DELIBERATELY NOT RAISED... the shoulder isolation fraction (0.19 of bench) is what's wrong, not this ceiling."* Every existing lateral raise is a dumbbell or a per-hand cable and BOTH are clamped by a separate implement ceiling first; a machine has neither clamp. Measured on a 120kg advanced male: dumbbell 20kg, cable 20kg, **machine 40kg** — 40kg being the exact figure that comment predicted. Fixing the fraction changes every trainee's lateral raise, so it is its own round with its own plan; the entry came out rather than the ceiling going up. **(b) `Preacher Curl` cost 3 of 13,967 audit combinations**, all the same 30-45min bodybuilding "Back & Biceps" day landing exactly on the +15% boundary. Traced: that day went from FIVE exercises to SEVEN. **Catalogue depth is not free** — a deeper pool lets the day-filler fit one more distinct movement, and biceps already had six entries and no swap problem at all. The additions that earn their duration cost are the ones in THIN patterns or the ones a trainee physically cannot substitute for. Recorded in the file so the next batch measures instead of assuming.

- [x] I CHANGED A TEST AFTER IT WENT RED, AND MADE IT STRONGER RATHER THAN GREENER. `test:added-load` failed on *"reps still climb under the constant weight (none seen)"*. Added load had NOT broken: Chin-Ups (3-5/4-6/5-7) and Tricep Dips (5-7/6-8/7-9) both carried it correctly. The check pinned itself to **Pull-Ups by name** in one seeded plan, and the shifted selection put Pull-Ups outside a strength phase while the other two still worked. The name was never what it meant. It now asserts climbing reps for **every** lift carrying added weight rather than one, plus a separate check that some lift carries it at all — so it can no longer pass by the feature silently vanishing, which is the failure it was really guarding. Verified against the injury-rebuild precedent: that gate is still red and still NOT rewritten, because there the premise was invalidated and needs a decision; here the coupling was incidental.

- [x] THE SEARCH BOX I OFFERED TO BUILD ALREADY EXISTED. I put "let me search the whole catalogue" to Ashley as a fourth option without checking. `SwapDialog` has had `searchExerciseCatalog` the whole time, unfiltered by pattern or equipment, placeholder "e.g. Decline Bench Press". It also explains her report completely: she could search, and `Chest Press Machine` genuinely was not in the app to find. Verified state, not an assumption — and a reminder to read the component before offering to build what is in it.

- [x] QUALITY **11.25 -> 11.28 / 12**, and `main_lift_short_rest` is **553 -> 0** — zero occurrences anywhere in a 9,216-combination sweep, not merely below a display cutoff (grepped the whole report). **Structure reached a perfect 2.00**, which is the dimension that rule lived in. Nothing else moved materially: frozen_week 5725 -> 5722, time_fit 2762 -> 2749, load_incoherent 693 and push_pull_imbalance 566 both unchanged. NOTE ON THE RUN: the first sweep was killed and restarted, because I edited the engine after it started and its number would have described code that was never committed. It produced no summary before being killed, so nothing was lost — but the number reported here is the re-run, against the exact tree in `36018f5`.

- [x] THE HISTOGRAM COULD NOT REPRESENT ITS OWN SCALE, found by disbelieving the report rather than by a gate. `formatHistogram` was hardcoded to 0-10 while the score is out of 12 (six dimensions x 2): `Math.min(10, Math.floor(s))` clamped every plan scoring 10+ into the top bucket, and the render loop then LABELLED that bucket `[9-10)`. So the report read **"9214 plans in [9-10)"** directly beneath **"Overall average: 11.28"** — arithmetically impossible, which is the only reason it was caught. The data was always right; the axis was two points short and the label was actively wrong. Almost certainly introduced when `primerFit` took the total from 10 to 12 and the histogram kept its old constant — the same shape as the rest-floor defect two entries up: a constant updated in one place and not another. Now derived (`DIMENSION_KEYS.length * 2`) so it moves by itself. `OVERALL_FLOOR` deliberately stays a literal — 7.2 is a RATIO decision (60%) that should be re-argued, not drift with the dimension count. `belowFloor` is counted separately and was never affected, so "0 plans below the floor" was true throughout.

- [x] THE DAY'S HARDEST LIFT KEEPS ITS REST — built. **553 of 9,216 combinations (6.0%)** had the day's main lift resting under a minute; every observed case a bodyweight pull-up at 42s or 57s. TRACED, arithmetic and all: hybrid's tier1 base 90s, × conditioning's 0.8 multiplier = 72s, − `stageTimeCap` Phase 2's blanket −15s = 57s, − anatomical adaptation's −15s `rest_adjust_seconds` = **42s**. Ashley's ruling: a flat 60-second minimum under any main lift, bodyweight included, and nothing may take it below — not the time squeeze, not the phase. Chosen over a 45s bodyweight floor (still too short for a set of pull-ups, and would have left the app's own quality check disagreeing with the rule) and over protecting the prescription from the squeeze only (lands at 57s, still under the line). 60 is not a new number: it is the floor `trimWeekRestForBudget` already used and the line `quality-score`'s `main_lift_short_rest` already drew. **Before this the rule and the check disagreed.** New `MAIN_LIFT_REST_FLOOR_SECONDS`; `test:main-lift-rest` gains section 4.

- [x] **THREE PLACES WAS WRONG — IT WAS FOUR, and the entry below saying "three" is the reason I found it.** That lesson (rest is set independently at prescription, at the per-week `adjustRest`, and at `trimWeekRestForBudget`, so a rest constraint must be re-asserted at each) was correct and was followed. What it missed: `stageTimeCap`'s **Phase 2** (`exercise-plan.ts:815`) takes 15s off every non-cardio exercise with a 30s floor and **no main-lift gate at all**. Phase 1 immediately above it does exempt main lifts, and its comment claims *"main lifts keep full rest even under time pressure"* — true only of Phase 1, and I had read that comment as covering the function. The write-up of a lesson is not the same as an inventory of the paths. There is now ONE `mainLiftRestFloor(entry, policy?)` helper and all five sites call it, so a sixth path fails loudly instead of silently undercutting the other five.

- [x] THE OLD RULING STILL HOLDS, ABOVE THE LINE — and this is the half that was easy to break. The three existing floors were all gated on `isExternallyLoaded` because of Ashley's earlier ruling that *"the risk this guards is a bar, not a tier"*, deliberately keeping a conditioning chin-up denser than a conditioning squat. That is preserved: conditioning's 90s floor is still loaded-only, and its bodyweight main lifts land in the **60–90s band, 106 of 108** — asserted as a band in `test:main-lift-rest` §3, not just "still under 90s", so the new floor cannot quietly sweep them up to 90. The universal floor stops being a density question below 60, where it becomes "can the trainee finish the next set".

- [x] THE GATE HAD THE HOLE THE ENGINE HAD. `test:main-lift-rest` §2 asked *"nothing anywhere puts a **bar** under 60 seconds"* and passed green (0 of 1,944) the entire time a bodyweight pull-up sat at 42s. Same blind spot, same `isExternallyLoaded` gate, in the test as in the code — which is why it never fired. New §4 asks the question of bodyweight main lifts and of deload weeks. TEETH PROVEN by stashing the engine change and re-running: **60 of 648** bodyweight main lifts under 60s before, **0** after; **4 of 864** deload cases before, **0** after. The `isDeload` exemption the loaded floor carries was deliberately NOT copied — a light week does not make a set easier to finish 42 seconds later.

- [x] THE FLOOR COST ESSENTIALLY NOTHING, AND ONE TIER GOT BETTER. Mean session duration per equipment tier over 1,728 loading days: bodyweight 52.07 → 52.11min, minimalist 52.82 → 52.84, full_gym 53.45 → **53.40**. Days past the **stated maximum** (not the midpoint — that denominator has misled once already): bodyweight 2 → 2, minimalist 6 → 6, full_gym **12 → 7**. The improvement is the estimator fix, not luck: `computeDurationTopUp` and `sizeBlockToRestBudget` now apply the same floor when forecasting, so top-up stops re-adding sets the day cannot afford. Before-numbers taken by stashing the change and re-running, not from an older log. `test:audit` **0 / 13,967** and `test:session-length` byte-identical (6 / 21 / 7 under-minimum, 0 past maximum on its own grid).

- [x] THE SET LOG CAN RECORD ADDED WEIGHT — built end to end, migration AND write AND read-back in one round. The loop was open at BOTH ends and neither was what I assumed. (a) The log had no column, and `SetGrid` already let you type 15 on a pull-up — writing `weight_kg: 15, is_bodyweight: false`, i.e. *"the pull-up weighed 15kg"*, a row indistinguishable from an ordinary 15kg lift. **Not a missing feature, a wrong one.** (b) `TodayPanel:152` filtered `.filter(ex => ex.suggested_load_kg != null)` before asking for a recommendation, and a pull-up's is null — so `getDoubleProgressionRecommendation` was **never called for these four lifts at all**. The read-back was not merely unwired, it was unreachable. Plan: `docs/PLAN-the-set-log-can-record-added-weight.md`. New migration `20260825120000`; `test:logging-roundtrip` gains sections 8-11 (16 checks).

- [x] I DID NOT REPEAT THE `assistance_kg` MISTAKE, which is the precedent that set the shape. That column was added by migration `20260811100000` and its own comment says the write path "is NOT built in this migration — see the round's decision log for why that's scoped as its own pass". **That pass never happened**: two weeks on, the column exists and nothing writes it. A column with no writer is not a foundation, it is a loose end, so this round shipped the column, the write, and the progression read-back together or not at all.

- [x] THE PRE-MIGRATION SAFETY PROPERTY, and it is the part that could have hurt live users. Both write paths spread a fixed object, so adding `added_load_kg` unconditionally would make **every set write fail** with "column does not exist" until `db:push-both` runs — on the one path where losing data is unforgivable, for a feature nobody is using yet. Two rules instead: the key is **omitted entirely when null**, so an ordinary set's payload is byte-identical to what shipped before and 99.9% of logging cannot be affected; and a write that does fail on the missing column **retries once without it**, so the trainee keeps their reps and loses only the belt figure, only until the migration runs. `test:logging-roundtrip` §9 asserts the key is ABSENT rather than null (on the payload, not the outcome) and §10 drives the degradation through a fake PGRST204.

- [x] SEPARATE COLUMN, NOT A REINTERPRETATION OF `weight_kg` — which means "the weight of the thing you lifted". Overloading it to mean "the weight you hung off yourself", switched on `is_bodyweight`, would be one field answering two questions: the defect class this repo has now hit five times (`loads_joints` answering "is this dangerous", the shrug `isolation_trap`, the slot-inheritance leak). A weighted pull-up is `weight_kg 0, is_bodyweight true, added_load_kg 15`, so `isMalformedZeroWeight`, `maxWorkingWeight`, the ghosts and the PR cache all keep seeing exactly what they saw before — asserted in §8 both ways round.

- [x] TWO DEVIATIONS FROM MY OWN PLAN, both deliberate. (1) The plan specified a new gate `test:added-load-log`; I extended `test:logging-roundtrip` instead, because a new file would have meant duplicating ~200 lines of fake Supabase client and two sources of truth for how the store behaves. (2) `SetGrid`'s PR paths (`checkForPR` and `getTopPRSet`) both read the STORED weight, so a weighted pull-up keeps exactly today's behaviour (bodyweight, PR by reps) rather than half-adopting added weight as the PR metric — passing the typed figure to one and the stored 0 to the other would have left the badge and the check disagreeing inside one function.

- [ ] FLAGGED — **PRs do not yet account for added weight.** A +20kg chin-up and a bodyweight one are the same PR as far as `pr-engine` is concerned. Real, deliberately not half-done in this round, and now a small change rather than a blocked one.

- [x] A CARRY THAT ACTUALLY GETS FURTHER — built. Carries were the largest remaining block of repeated weeks: **100 of 265** frozen week-to-week transitions. Someone carries 22kg for 40m and it is still 22kg for 40m four months later. TRACED, not assumed: the distance is a hardcoded `'40m'` in `fixedUnitPrescription`, and the comment above it stated the design outright — *"a 40m farmer's walk is 40m for everyone; **only the load moves**"*. Coherent until the load converges on its ceiling, after which **nothing** moves: carries are excluded from `rampReps` AND from the frozen-load rep bump, correctly, because `shiftReps` passes `'40m'` straight through. Ashley's ruling: when the weight can't move, walk further. MEASURED: carries **100 → 52**, total **265 → 217 (4.6% → 3.8%)**, with loaded non-carry (128) and bodyweight (37) unchanged. New `FROZEN_CARRY_DISTANCE_STEP_M = 5`, capped at 3 steps.

- [x] QUALITY **11.19 → 11.25 / 12**, the largest single move of the session, and it lands exactly where the change was aimed: `Progression` **1.69 → 1.75**. `Time fit` pays 1.62 → 1.61 for the longer carries, which is the honest cost and is measured against the MIDPOINT budget; against the number the trainee actually gave us, the stated maximum, the change is one day in 6,912. Every other dimension unchanged.

- [x] THE RESIDUAL 52 ARE AT THE CAP, VERIFIED RATHER THAN INFERRED FROM THE EXAMPLES. Counted by the distance they are stuck at: **52 at exactly 55m** (40 + 3×5) and 2 at 40m (no previous loading week to compare against). That is a ceiling reached, not a lever missing — materially different from before, when nothing ever moved. Cap kept at 3 steps deliberately: 40 → 55m is already +37.5%, and without a cap a sixteen-week plan walks to 100m, which is a different exercise.

- [x] THE DURATION MODEL HAD TO LEARN DISTANCE FIRST, and that was the real risk. `computeSetWorkSeconds` returned a flat `fallbackSeconds` for ANY distance — **a 50m carry cost exactly the same as a 40m one**. Fine while distance was a constant; a silent way to blow a session budget the moment it isn't, and `duration` is the audit check that has now bitten three times. Fixed WITHOUT a new pace constant: each entry's own `avg_duration_seconds` already implies one over the default distance (Farmer's Walk 35s/40m = **1.14 m/s**; Loaded Backpack Walk 40s = **1.00 m/s**), so every carry keeps its tuned pace and scales. Measured cost, isolated from the tempo round: mean +0.1min, days over the STATED MAXIMUM 14/48/95/88 → 14/48/95/**89** — one day, in full_gym.

- [x] MY OWN GATE WOULD HAVE PASSED A REAL DEFECT, and finding that was worth more than the feature. The first cut of the carry over-fire check ran ONE profile and asserted *"distance never moves in the same week the load did"*. It passed. Swept across every combo the true figure is **8 of 272**, and reading a real plan showed why: `Suitcase Carry w5:45m@12 → w6:40m@14` — the distance visibly goes BACKWARDS. Measured all 8: **every one has the load going up**, zero with the load flat, so it is the deliberate reset (a carry that earned a heavier weight returns to base distance, exactly as double progression resets a rep range). The check was wrong on both counts — wrong number, and looking for the wrong property. Rewritten to sweep `everyCombo()` (260 transitions) and assert what actually matters: **distance never shortens unless the weight went up** (0 of 260), and never grows in the same week the weight did (0).

- [x] TWO OTHER ASSERTIONS CHANGED MEANING WITH THIS, both deliberately and both recorded rather than quietly flipped. (1) `test:frozen-weeks` §4 read *"carries keep their fixed distance"*, written so the exclusion "can't quietly rot into an accident" — Ashley has now answered the question it was holding open, so its teeth move to the other side. (2) §5 read *"carries are still frozen — the exclusion is real, not accidental"*; it now asserts the halving with a floor under it, so neither a regression nor a silent walk past the cap passes. (3) The rep-ceiling check in §3 started failing because `repLow('45m')` reads 45 and compared it to a 40m baseline as "+5 reps" — correct arithmetic, wrong unit; carries are now excluded from a check that was only ever about reps.

- [x] `fixedUnitPrescription`'S DOC COMMENT SAID SOMETHING THAT STOPPED BEING TRUE the moment this shipped, so it was rewritten rather than left. It still returns the DEFAULT distance, which is what a carry rotated into a slot by swap/ban/injury/equipment must start from — `test:slot-replacement` green.

- [x] THE COACH CAN LOG ADDED WEIGHT — built, and **I had the mechanism wrong when I flagged it.** I wrote that "chat-gemini's tool schema has no field for it" and that it needed an edge-function deploy. Neither is true: `log_workout`'s contract is *"the server does NOT parse anything here — it forwards the model's raw text spans untouched"*, and `src/lib/set-parse.ts` is **the only place a number becomes a number**. So the fix is entirely CLIENT-SIDE and ships with the Vercel push — **no chat-gemini deploy required**. I said the opposite when recommending this work.

- [x] AND THE DEFECT WAS WORSE THAN "NO FIELD FOR IT" — the stated weight was **silently discarded**. MEASURED before: `"3x5 @15kg"`, `"3x5 with 15kg"`, `"3x5"` and `"3x5 @bodyweight"` on Chin-Ups all produced the **identical row**, `weightKg 0`. The cause is one line: `isBodyweight = parsed.isBodyweight || (entryEntry != null && !entryIsLoaded)` — a chin-up is not externally loaded, so the override fired and threw the figure away. **Silently is the worst version**: the check directly above it in `test:pending-actions` shows a LOADED lift blocks on a weight ambiguity and asks rather than guessing. These four just dropped it. Now `@15kg` on a chin-up carries `addedLoadKg 15` with `weightKg` still 0, and the receipt reads **"BW +15kg"** rather than a bare figure. `test:pending-actions` gains 8 checks including the over-fire (a barbell lift's number still goes to `weight_kg`).

- [ ] FLAGGED, PRE-EXISTING, NOT WIDENED — `WEIGHT_RE` requires a literal `@`, so **"3x5 with 15kg" still loses the weight**. That is not specific to these four: `"bench 3x8 with 80kg"` fails to parse too, and for a loaded lift it degrades correctly (asks "what weight did you use?") while for these four it stays silent, because we cannot tell "no weight stated" from "weight stated in a form we can't read". Widening the regex touches every exercise's parse and wants its own round with its own measurement — deliberately not done inside this change.

- [ ] **`assistance_kg` IS NOW A STRICTLY SMALLER JOB.** This round built the exact plumbing its migration comment said it needed — an optional-kg column threaded through `SaveSetInput`, `PendingSet`, both write paths, the server row shape, and a progression read-back. Assistance is the mirror (less over time = stronger) and still wants its own pass, but the pattern to copy now exists and is gated.

- [x] A DIP IS NOT A PULL-UP — built. New `added_load_scale` on the exercise entry, relative to a weighted pull-up at 1.0: **Chest Dips 1.4, Tricep Dips 1.2**, absent (=1.0) on Pull-Ups and Chin-Ups so the change could not move them even by rounding. An 80kg advanced trainee in a strength block now reads `pull-up +15kg / upright dip +17.5kg / chest dip +20kg`; a 110kg one `+20 / +25 / +27.5`; a 62kg woman `+10 / +12.5 / +15`. Ashley chose this over matching the strength charts outright (which would have given the 80kg man +25 / +27.5 and the 110kg man **+37.5kg**).

- [x] I CORRECTED TWO THINGS I HAD WRITTEN ABOUT THESE EXERCISES. (1) I described Tricep Dips as **"bench-style"** — it is not. It is the same dip bars as Chest Dips with an UPRIGHT torso (`form_cues: 'Upright torso (more tricep)'`), `joint_stress: 'moderate'`, and it is the only one of the four reachable at NOVICE (`minExperience: 'novice'`, vs 'intermediate' for the other three). The difference between the two dips is torso angle, not apparatus, which is why 1.2 vs 1.4 rather than something wider. (2) The earlier entry called the single fraction table "deliberately conservative" as though that were a finished decision; it was an unexamined default, and reading the entries is what showed the two dips differ.

- [x] WHY NOT THE STRENGTH CHARTS, with the number that decided it. At a 2.0x chest-dip scale a **50kg trainee's chest dip lands on exactly 17.5kg — the 35% safety cap itself**. A formula that reaches its own ceiling is no longer prescribing; the ceiling is. `test:added-load` now asserts the cap NEVER binds across seven bodies (50-110kg) and all four lifts, so it stays a backstop rather than quietly becoming the prescriber. The shipped 1.4x leaves margin at every body measured.

- [x] ASHLEY ASKED WHAT THE NUMBERS ARE BASED ON, and the answer is now in the code rather than only in chat. It is a percentage of the trainee's OWN BODYWEIGHT — because a dip or a pull-up IS lifting your bodyweight, so what you can add on top is proportional to what you are already moving. Three inputs: bodyweight, experience tier (intermediate 10% / advanced 18% at the pull-up baseline), and which movement (the new scale). **What it is NOT based on: anything the app has watched them do.** That limit is the reason week one of every block is bodyweight-only, the reason the guidance says "drop the weight and keep the reps if it costs you form", and the reason the 35% ceiling exists at all. `ADDED_LOAD_BODYWEIGHT_FRACTION`'s doc comment now says all of this in place.

- [x] A CHIN-UP THAT ACTUALLY GETS HEAVIER — built, closing the gap Ashley named. `report:added-load` BEFORE, across 8,928 slots (4 equipment × 4 experience × 4 goals × 3 splits × 4 bodies): Pull-Ups 3,960, Chin-Ups 3,912, Tricep Dips 608, Chest Dips 448 — and **zero carrying a weight**. The defect that produced, traced on a full-gym advanced 80kg male: `Pull-Ups w1:9-11 w2:10-12 w3:11-13 | … | w9:3-5 w10:4-6 w11:5-7` — **week 9 prescribed THREE reps to someone the plan believes can do eleven, with nothing added.** That is not a strength block, it is less work; the phase note promises *"Heavier and lower rep"* and delivered only the second half. AFTER: **801 of 8,928 slots (9%)** carry weight — the strength and power blocks and nothing else — reading `w9:3-5@+15 w10:4-6@+15 w11:5-7@+15`. Plan: `docs/PLAN-a-chin-up-that-actually-gets-heavier.md`. New gate `npm run test:added-load` (22 checks).

- [x] THE DESIGN I BUILT FIRST WAS WRONG, CAUGHT BY LOOKING AT THE OUTPUT RATHER THAN THE GATE. The first cut scaled the added weight DOWN as reps rose (1.0 at ≤5 reps, 0.6 at 6-8, none above) with a 0.7/0.85/1.0 step across the block. It passed a typecheck and produced numbers, and the trace showed what it really did: `w9:3-5@+12.5 w10:4-6@+15 w11:5-7@+10` — **weight going up then DOWN inside one block**, and vanishing entirely at `w7:8-10`. Cause: reps climb within a block (that IS the within-block lever) and my rep scale cut the weight as they climbed. Two levers fighting, producing more reps at less weight — a deload wearing progress's clothes, the exact failure the frozen-week round already documented and which I walked into anyway. Rewritten to key on the **phase**, which cannot change inside a block, so the weight is constant by construction. The gate now pins both halves — weight constant within a block, reps still climbing under it — so the rejected design cannot come back.

- [x] FOUR REFUSALS, each for its own reason: no added weight on a **deload** (recovery is doing less, not carrying the same belt for fewer reps — same reason tempo skips it), none on the **calibration week** (the app has never seen this person do a single pull-up), none **below intermediate** (unreachable today since Pull-Ups/Chin-Ups/Chest Dips already carry `capability_requirement: intermediate` — measured 0 slots — but asserted so relaxing that gate can't silently load a novice's dips), and none above a **10-rep target** (a guard against an odd phase config, not a scale; never fires today).

- [x] IT IS ADDED WEIGHT AND THE SCREEN SAYS SO. New `suggested_added_load_kg`, deliberately NOT `suggested_load_kg`: putting 15 in the ordinary field would render "15kg" beside "Pull-Ups", which reads as *lift 15kg* — the class of untrue statement `LoadChip`'s own header exists to prevent. New `AddedLoadChip` renders "+15kg added / on top of bodyweight", mirroring how `AssistanceChip` already inverts the framing for assisted pull-ups. Keeping it a separate field also means every consumer of `suggested_load_kg` — `enforceLoadCoherence`, quality-score's `load_incoherent`, the frozen-week report, `isLoadlessWeek`, the tempo exclusion — carries on treating a pull-up exactly as before. No silent reinterpretation of an existing field.

- [x] TWO CEILINGS, and no third discount. 35% of bodyweight absolute (a backstop in the spirit of `SAFETY_CEILING_KG`; max actually seen is 15kg against a 28kg cap), plus `IMPROVISED_IMPLEMENT_CEILING_KG` for a bodyweight-tier trainee since with no gym the added weight IS a loaded backpack — **currently unreachable and kept anyway**, because `BODYWEIGHT_ALLOWED_PHASES` excludes strength and power so a bodyweight plan never enters a phase this loads. Deliberately NO extra conservatism multiplier for an assumed body: `load-prescription.ts`'s calibration comment records that as tried and measured — *"the fix for an unknown body has to be the body… stacking a discount on top of a conservative body compounds to absurdity without adding safety."*

- [x] QUALITY IS UNCHANGED AT **11.19 / 12**, and every rule count is byte-identical to the pre-change run (frozen_week 7102, time_fit 2732, load_incoherent 693, …). That is not a null result, it is a measurement: `quality-score.ts` reads `suggested_load_kg`, and this change deliberately writes `suggested_added_load_kg` instead, so **the scorer cannot see the added weight at all**. No regression and no credit. Also means the added weight costs nothing in session time, which was the risk worth checking — a belt does not make a set longer.

- [ ] FLAGGED, a gap the above exposes — **quality-score has no visibility into whether these four lifts progress.** Its `progression` dimension reads load and reps; a pull-up shows null load, so a strength block that now goes `3-5@+15 → 4-6@+15 → 5-7@+15` scores exactly the same as one that went `3-5 → 4-6 → 5-7` with nothing on the belt. Teaching it `suggested_added_load_kg` is a small change to the scorer, and it should be done before the next round claims a progression number for these lifts.

- [ ] FLAGGED, the tightest number here — the declined-metrics case **ties rather than clears** at the bottom. `ASSUMED_BODY` is 50kg, so a stated 50kg trainee and a declining one both land on 10kg, and below 50kg the declining one would get MORE. That boundary is `ASSUMED_BODY`'s, not this function's: it applies to every load in the app, and `test:assumed-body`'s own comparison set stops at 50kg for the same reason. Pinned in `test:added-load` so the tie is deliberate rather than discovered later.

- [x] ~~STILL OUT OF SCOPE~~ **BOTH NOW DONE.** (1) dips take more than pull-ups — see the `added_load_scale` entry. (2) the logged-history path — see the set-log entry above.

- [x] MAKE THE TEMPO REAL — built. The loadless coach note shipped in `074ad9d` tells a bodyweight trainee *"the difficulty comes from how you move: about three seconds lowering, a pause at the bottom"* — and **that sentence was the only place tempo existed**. No field anywhere in the app, nothing prescribed, nothing tracked, nothing progressed; the session still said `Air Squat 9-12` and stopped. A promise with no delivery, same shape as `update_workout_schedule` and the Muay Thai swap, and worth fixing precisely because we had just made the promise. Tempo now becomes **the phase's voice for a lift that cannot take weight**: `2-0-1` in anatomical adaptation, `3-0-1` in hypertrophy, `4-1-1` in maximal strength; none in power (explosive intent) or metabolic (short rest is the stimulus). One lever at a time — tempo is the BLOCK's and constant within it, reps stay the week's, following `loadStepUnaffordable`'s precedent. Plan: `docs/PLAN-make-the-tempo-real.md`. New gate `npm run test:tempo-prescription` (21 checks).

- [x] ASHLEY'S CORRECTION, and it changed the design: **"Pull ups can add weight."** She is right — a chin-up or a dip takes a belt or a loaded backpack, so *"there is no weight to add"* is simply false for them. Showing no weight on a chin-up is **a gap in this app, not a fact about the exercise**, and a slow eccentric there would paper over the gap rather than close it. New `accepts_added_load` on the exercise entry, set on exactly four — Pull-Ups, Chin-Ups, Chest Dips, Tricep Dips — and excluded from tempo. Everything else on the bodyweight list progresses by LEVERAGE (a deficit push-up, a straighter hanging leg raise) and an assisted pull-up loads the other way entirely. Coverage after the exclusion: bodyweight 30.1%, minimalist 20.2%, home_gym 11.5%, full_gym 6.8% of working sets.

- [x] ~~THE REAL FIX FOR THOSE FOUR, NOT DONE~~ **DONE in `583718a` (`prescribeAddedLoad`), `2398d0c` (per-movement scale) and `484772d` (the set log).** Left open here for three rounds after it shipped — my own note drifting from the code, the same shape as the migration mistake. Original text kept below. ~~if a chin-up can take weight, the app should be PRESCRIBING that weight rather than only cutting reps in a strength block.~~ Needs strength-standards handling for weighted pull-ups/dips (assisted pull-ups already have `suggested_assistance_kg`, so the machinery is half there). Until then those four lifts still show reps falling with nothing to explain it — the original defect, unfixed for that slice, and named here rather than quietly counted as solved.

- [x] I CAUSED A DURATION REGRESSION AND FIXED IT AT THE CAUSE. `test:session-length` went red: **2 of 576 "30-45" sessions ran to 45.8 minutes**, 48 seconds past a ceiling that read 0 before. Both were Maximal Strength weeks with a `4-1-1` push-up at 4 sets. Cause: `enforceWeeklyPatternBalance` is documented as needing to run LAST — after every duration pass — and it BUMPS SETS to balance push against pull, a known couple-of-minutes drift (see the older entry below). Tempo made those minutes cost more, since a `4-1-1` rep is 6s against `SECONDS_PER_REP`'s assumed 3.5. Fixed by a **final safety trim against the STATED MAXIMUM** (new `getSessionMaximumSeconds`), not the midpoint budget the earlier pass aims at — so it is a backstop, not a second opinion: every day already inside what the trainee asked for is skipped untouched, and only a day that broke the ceiling gives up rest. Runs inside the existing `if (!isDeload)` guard, honouring `trimWeekRestForBudget`'s contract, and respects `minLoadedMainLiftRestSeconds` so it cannot erode the conditioning rest floor (`test:main-lift-rest` re-verified green).

- [x] THE DURATION MODEL CAN SEE THE TEMPO, which is the whole risk. `SECONDS_PER_REP` is 3.5 and its own comment says it already assumes a controlled rep, so a `4-1-1` set costs 71% more working time. `DurationSlot` now carries tempo and `computeSetWorkSeconds` REPLACES the generic pace rather than adding to it (adding would double-count). MEASURED across 6,912 days per tier, before → after: mean 48.4→48.5 / 49.4→49.6 / 50.2→50.3 / 50.2→50.3 min, and days over the stated maximum 14→14, 51→48, 97→95, 92→88 — flat to slightly BETTER. Worst-case ratio unchanged at 104/111/109/107%.

- [x] QUALITY COST, NAMED RATHER THAN WAVED AT: **11.20 → 11.19 / 12**. The whole of it is `Time fit` (1.63 → 1.62), with `timeFit/time_fit` firing on **2,645 → 2,732 combinations (+87, 28.7% → 29.6%)**. Every other dimension is unchanged to two decimals. That is the expected and honest cost: a slower rep makes a session marginally longer, and quality-score's `time_fit` measures against the MIDPOINT budget. Against the number the trainee actually gave us — the stated maximum — the same change is flat-to-better (days over: 14→14, 51→48, 97→95, 92→88). Both readings reported because they answer different questions and only one of them is what a trainee asked for.

- [ ] FLAGGED, PRE-EXISTING, NOT CHASED — that same wider grid (4 goals × 3 experience × 3 durations × 3 splits, a 62kg female) finds **~1% of loading days over the stated maximum in every tier**, at HEAD as well as after. `test:session-length` reads 0 because its own grid is a different profile set, so "0 over budget" is true of that grid and NOT of the app generally. The trimmer only pulls rest and gives up once rest is at its floor, which is the likely cause — inferred from its documented behaviour, NOT verified case by case. Worth a round of its own.

- [x] "ADD LOAD NEXT WEEK" — TO SOMEONE WITH NO WEIGHTS. Fixed. A trainee whose equipment answer was **bodyweight** opened a week of Box Squat (Bodyweight), Deficit Push-Ups, Table Row and Low Box Step-Up and was told *"with **load climbing week to week**… **find the weight** where the last rep feels like RPE 6"*, *"**Baseline week — this sets the working weight** every later week adds load on top of"*, and *"**Drop the load** if you need to"*. Every one is an instruction about equipment she does not own — same class as the assumed-body work.
