MEASURED: **369 of 576 bodyweight week notes → 0**; full-gym weeks still carry the load coaching (381 of 576), so the new wording did not leak to people who do have weights. Ashley's ruling: fix the words first, then look at the training. Plan: `docs/PLAN-dont-tell-someone-with-no-weights-to-add-weight.md`. New gate `npm run test:loadless-notes` (20 checks).

- [x] THE NUMBER THAT STARTED IT, AND THE WRONG VERSION OF IT I ALMOST REPORTED. Measuring "does a 16-week plan actually progress" first gave **46.5% of exercises end lower than they started** — a scary figure that is simply wrong. It was measuring PERIODIZATION: `anatomical_adaptation` carries `rep_shift +3` and `strength` carries `−3`, so any plan opening in one and closing in the other shows reps falling by six regardless of progress. Re-measured phase-neutrally (Epley e1RM): loaded lifts **83.5% up / 8.6% lower**. Bodyweight lifts are 84.7% lower AS PRESCRIBED but **77.2% up with the block's own rep shift removed**, 4.9% genuinely regressing. So the engine is fine and the defect was entirely in what the trainee is TOLD — a loaded lift has a weight column to carry "fewer reps but heavier", and a bodyweight lift has no column at all (`LoadChip`: *"Bodyweight renders no chip and no ⓘ"*).

- [x] TWO THINGS I REPORTED AS PART OF THIS DEFECT THAT ARE NOT. (1) *"Heavier and lower rep"* and *"Move the weight fast… bar speed"* never reach a bodyweight trainee — `BODYWEIGHT_ALLOWED_PHASES` already excludes 'strength' and 'power'. The loadless variants cover those two phases anyway so relaxing that restriction can't silently reintroduce it. (2) The per-EXERCISE guidance was already correct: *"Progress by adding reps or slowing the tempo before adding load."* The defect was confined to the WEEK note, assembled from three independent sources (`phaseConfig.coach_note`, `policy.coachNote`, `buildProgressionNote`) — all three needed changing, the same "re-assert it at every path" shape the main-lift rest floor taught.

- [x] THE PREDICATE KEYS ON THE PLAN, NOT THE EQUIPMENT ANSWER, and that distinction is the point. `EQUIPMENT_SETS` includes `'weighted backpack'` at EVERY tier deliberately — it is the one progressive load available with no gym — so a bodyweight-tier plan does contain a couple of real numbers (Backpack Row, Loaded Backpack Walk). Keying on `equipment_access === 'bodyweight'` would have declared those weightless. `isLoadlessWeek` instead asks the week it just built: at most 25% of working sets carrying a load. MEASURED loaded share: **bodyweight 12.2%, minimalist 52.2%, full_gym 77.0%** — the threshold sits in the gap rather than on top of either number, and the gate prints all three every run so it can't drift unseen.

- [x] NO PRESCRIPTION CHANGED, PROVEN RATHER THAN ARGUED. Generated 432 plans (4 equipment × 4 goals × 3 experience × 3 splits × 3 injury sets) with `coach_note` stripped, against both engines: **identical md5 over 163MB**. `test:quality` therefore cannot have moved and was deliberately NOT re-run — a checksum over every prescription is a stronger statement than a score. (First cut of that comparison was a FALSE pass: both sides came back empty because the `grep -v '^\['` I use to strip debug lines was deleting every data line, each plan being a JSON array. Caught because "0 lines vs 0 lines" is not evidence of anything.)

- [ ] SECOND HALF, PARTLY BUILT — Ashley asked for the words first "and then the training". The training half arrived as TEMPO (`c343ca4`), not as a harder movement. Progressing the MOVEMENT (Air Squat → a harder variation) was measured and is **blocked on content, not code**: inverting `capability_requirement.regression` gives 15 entries forming 10 links, so only **53 of 731 (7.3%)** of a bodyweight trainee's weightless working slots have a harder version they can reach, and Air Squat, Box Squat, Plank, Side Plank, Low Box Step-Up and Step-Down have **no rung up at all**. Needs ~15-25 new exercise entries written before the engine change is worth anything.

- [x] A BAND WHERE A BARBELL SHOULD BE — fixed. A **full gym** user was prescribed `Band Shoulder Press` for a tier2 compound slot. The training objection an LLM review raised (bands lose tension at the bottom) is fair but not the one that bites: a resistance band is not in `LOADED_EQUIPMENT`, so the app shows **no weight at all** — the weight column that carries a lift's whole progression story is blank and the lift can only progress by reps, while a barbell sits unused in the same pool. MECHANISM, traced: `scoreCandidate` weighed five factors plus a tier bonus and **not one of them looked at the implement**. `Dumbbell Shoulder Press` and `Band Shoulder Press` are both tier2_compound vertical_push and scored IDENTICALLY, so the winner was decided by the ±0.3 tie-break jitter — a coin flip, every time. MEASURED (`npm run report:band-slots`, 18,909 main/secondary slots): bands holding a main slot with a loaded same-pattern/same-tier peer available **637 (3.37%) → 319 (1.69%)**; `Band Shoulder Press` **123 → 0**. All four ranking call sites funnel through one `orderCandidates`, so unlike the rest floor this was a single choke point. Plan: `docs/PLAN-a-band-where-a-barbell-should-be.md`. New gate `npm run test:band-slots`.

- [x] THE RESIDUAL IS NOT UNFIXED WORK, and was chased rather than waved at. Of the 95 surviving full-gym cases: **90 had the loaded peer's movement family already claimed by that day** (the duplicate-family guard runs ahead of scoring, so the peer was never eligible for the slot), **4 had it already used elsewhere that week**, and **1 is a genuine miss** (`Kneeling Band Lat Pulldown` over a free `Close-Grip Lat Pulldown`). The gate's ceiling is 10, not 0, for exactly that reason. NOTE ON THE NUMBERS: the first cut of `report:band-slots` asked the looser "did the day contain ANY loaded main lift", which counted a band as a defect whenever an unrelated slot elsewhere in the session held a dumbbell. Both figures above are the tightened definition measured against BOTH engines — the looser one read 906 → 615 and is not comparable.

- [x] THREE GUARDS, each load-bearing, because the easy way to drive this number to zero is to break one. (1) The penalty only applies when **another candidate in the same ranked list carries a real load** — a minimalist trainee whose only `vertical_pull` is a band keeps it; 378 such placements survive by design. (2) A band **indicated for a flagged joint is exempt**: `Spanish Squat` is a patellar-tendon rehab tool and the rehab pass puts it there on purpose — measured 348 → **352**, i.e. it went UP, because an exempt band now outranks the non-indicated bands it used to tie with. (3) The penalty is **12**, above the jitter (0.3) and above anything the five factors can swing together (~5), and far below the 30-point gap between mechanics tiers — so it reorders implements within a tier and can never reorder the tiers. The gate reads all three out of the source, so raising the constant past the tier gap fails a check instead of silently restructuring sessions.

- [ ] FLAGGED, PARTLY EXPLAINED — `test:quality` is **unchanged at 11.20 / 12** and `Selection` is unchanged at 1.94, but one rule moved: `selection/load_incoherent` **651 → 693 combinations (+42, +0.46pp)**. Direction of travel is "newly visible, not newly caused": ~318 main/secondary slots that previously showed NO weight now show one, and every branch of `load_incoherent` requires a non-null `suggested_load_kg` — a lift with no weight cannot trip a rule that compares weights. **My first, more specific theory was wrong and is recorded because it was nearly reported as fact**: I assumed a band standing as the day's main press had been silently disabling the rule's whole isolation-vs-main-press branch (which needs `mainPress.suggested_load_kg != null`). Measured instead of asserted — of 855 days whose main press carries no weight, **0** have a band in that position, at HEAD and after, because a band tier2 is almost never the day's FIRST main-press match. The remaining +42 is consistent with the newly-loaded lifts simply being checkable at all, but that has not been proven exercise by exercise. Small, flat headline, flagged rather than claimed.

- [ ] FLAGGED, deliberately not done — the rule is "a real weight beats a **band**", not "a real weight beats anything unloaded". `Pull-Ups`, `Dips` and `Push-Ups` are legitimate main lifts with their own progression (harder variations), and demoting them for a full-gym trainee would be a much larger change than the one asked for. Named here rather than assumed either way.

- [ ] **`test:injury-rebuild` IS RED, AND HAS BEEN SINCE BEFORE THIS CHANGE — I previously reported it fixed and that was wrong.** Bisected across nine commits: it fails at `6646854`, `a6f8e79`, `b3bb0b6` on *"a thinning injury does not trigger a rebuild"* (the wiped-pattern cause I diagnosed and fixed last session), then from `f7939ef` onward on a **different** check in the same file — first *"rebuild beats what substitution would have left"*, then *"the two modes genuinely differ"*. **How I got it wrong: I fixed the check I had bisected to, confirmed that one check passed, and did not re-read the rest of the gate's output.** The failing assertion moved; the gate never went green. CAUSE, and it is not a defect: the six shoulder-friendly variants plus `NEAREST_PATTERN_FALLBACK` made **pointwise substitution stop being lossy** (shoulder substitute slots 388 → 446), so `rebuiltSlots > subSlots` no longer distinguishes the two modes — the gate's premise was invalidated by the content work that fixed the underlying problem. Deliberately NOT rewritten to go green: whether the rebuild path still earns its place now that substitution covers shoulders is a real question, not a test-maintenance chore. Band change measured against it either way: HEAD 448 vs 450, with the change 436 vs 446 — both fail, neither for band reasons.

- [x] A LOADED BARBELL WITH 42 SECONDS REST — fixed. Someone whose goal is **conditioning** was prescribed Barbell Squats and Barbell Bench Press with 42s between sets. Nothing was broken: conditioning's `restSecondsMultiplier` is 0.8 on tier1, and on a short session that lands at 42s — the goal working exactly as designed, on the one exercise where it should not. Ashley's ruling: *the session still conditions, the part with a bar on your back does not.* Short rest stays everywhere else (accessories, machines, bodyweight, carries); a LOADED tier1 lift gets a 90s floor. MEASURED: conditioning's loaded main lifts under 90s **391 → 0** on loading weeks; nothing in any goal now rests under 60s (0 of 1,944). `test:audit` still 0/13,967 and `test:session-length` unchanged — the extra rest did not push sessions over budget. New gate `npm run test:main-lift-rest`.

- [x] SCOPED TO THE GOAL, DELIBERATELY, after measuring the alternative. A blanket 90s floor would have changed **~20% of loaded main lifts for every other goal too** (hypertrophy 87/432, fat_loss 118/432, strength 84/432…), which is far wider than what was approved — and those are not dangerous: conditioning was the **only** goal that ever went below 60s at all, and a hypertrophy bench at 75s is a deliberate, normal prescription. So the floor is a named field on the goal policy (`minLoadedMainLiftRestSeconds`), set only on conditioning, rather than a global rule. Gated both ways: the floor holds for conditioning, and no other goal has one.

- [x] ~~THE FLOOR HAD TO BE ASSERTED IN THREE PLACES~~ **FOUR — see the rest-floor entry at the top of this file.** THE FLOOR HAD TO BE ASSERTED IN THREE PLACES, which is the transferable lesson. Rest is set independently at prescription (`assignSetsRepsFromConfig`), at the per-week phase adjustment (`adjustRest` — a metabolic block's negative `rest_adjust_seconds` took a squat from 75s to 55s), and at the duration trimmer (`trimWeekRestForBudget`, which carried its **own hardcoded `isMain ? 60 : 30`** and dragged the 90s straight back to 60). Fixing only the first left 288 of 432 still under the floor; fixing the first two left 285. Each path sets rest without consulting the others, so a constraint on rest has to be re-stated at each — worth remembering before adding any other rest rule.

- [x] Gated the half of the ruling that is easiest to lose: conditioning's **bodyweight** main lifts keep their short rest (106 of 108 still under 90s). Had the floor been keyed on TIER rather than on "is there a bar", a conditioning trainee's chin-up day would have slowed down too and the goal would have quietly become hypertrophy. `isExternallyLoaded` is the discriminator, matching where the floor is set at prescription time.

- [x] THE COACH PROMISED SOMETHING IT COULD NOT DO — fixed. Plan: `docs/PLAN-swap-a-session-for-what-you-actually-did.md`. Ashley told the coach in advance she was skipping her weights day for Muay Thai; it replied *"I'll make sure today is marked as a rest day for lifting so we stay on track"* and did nothing. It could not have: of 22 tools, **none touched a day's status**. Worse than a no-op — `classifyDay` ends `dateStr < todayStr ? 'missed' : 'due'` with nothing between, so the day she announced IN ADVANCE would show as **missed** the next morning and the Muay Thai was recorded nowhere. Same shape as the pre-plan-days bug: the reward for engaging with the app is being told you failed. **The lesson was already written down and ignored** — `update_workout_schedule` is disabled with this in its own description: *"It used to write to a profile field the app doesn't actually render from, so schedule 'changes' looked applied in chat but never showed up on the Exercise tab."* Ashley's ruling: swap the day AND record what replaced it, over merely not counting it against her (leaves real training invisible) and over merely muzzling the coach (leaves her to do it by hand). Built: additive migration adding `swapped_for_activity` to `workout_sessions` (it is already one row per profile per date, so the fact belongs there, not in a third table); new `'swapped'` day state; new `swap_session_for_activity` tool writing to `workout_sessions` AND `cardio_logs` — the latter meaning the activity counts toward the streak for free, since `streak.ts` already reads it. New gates `test:coach-promises` and six cases added to `test:training-week`.

- [x] THE GENERAL GUARD, which is the more valuable half: `test:coach-promises` asserts **every declared tool has an executor branch**. A tool the model can see but nothing implements is a promise with no delivery — it gets called, falls through, and the reply describes an action that never happened. Checked at the time: all 22 are wired, so this was the only instance. It also asserts the new tool writes ONLY where the app reads (`workout_sessions` for the week strip, `cardio_logs` for the streak) and **never** to `fitness_profiles` — the field `update_workout_schedule` died on — and that a failed write produces a failure sentence rather than a success one, which is the original defect one layer down. Prompt gains NEVER CLAIM AN ACTION YOU DID NOT TAKE: don't speak in past tense about a change until the tool has run; when there is no tool, say so and point at the Exercise tab.

- [x] Ranking decided deliberately: `'swapped'` sits BELOW the logged-work checks and above the date judgement. Someone who announced a swap and then trained anyway has earned the tick — the hook's own comment says logged work outranks every date judgement, and *"anything else would erase real work to make a tidier calendar"*. Asserted both ways, plus the over-fire check that an ordinary unlogged past day is still `missed`.

- [ ] FLAGGED, one judgement call — a swapped day **drops out of the weekly tally entirely** rather than counting as done. Reasoning: the tally counts sessions OF THE LIFTING PLAN, so counting a deliberately-skipped day as done would inflate lifting adherence, and counting it as missed is the bug being fixed; dropping it mirrors `before_plan`. The alternative — count it as done, since Muay Thai is real training — is defensible and is one entry in one predicate if Ashley prefers it. What she actually did is not lost either way: it is in `cardio_logs` and the streak reads that.

- [x] **RESOLVED 27 Aug 2026 — both commands run.** `npm run db:push-both` reported *"Remote database is up to date"* on BOTH projects, so the `swapped_for_activity` column had already been applied at some earlier point and this item had been carrying a stale claim; and `chat-gemini` is now deployed to production. Verified from the command output, not inferred. Original text follows.

- [x] SESSIONS THAT FINISHED BEFORE THE TIME WAS UP — fixed. `applyDurationFiller` triggered on a flat 15-minute gap below the MIDPOINT budget, and that number is right for exactly one tier by coincidence: 75 − 15 lands precisely on the "60-90" tier's 60-minute minimum, so it looked correct. Every other tier had a hole beneath its own minimum — "45-60" (52min budget) topped nothing up between 37 and 45 minutes, so a session could ship shorter than the range the trainee actually chose and nothing noticed. Filler now triggers on the stated MINIMUM as well as the flat gap, via new `getSessionMinimumSeconds`. MEASURED on loading weeks, "45-60": sessions below the 45-minute minimum **77/576 (13%) → 19/576 (3%)**, mean fill 93% → 95%, worst case 69% → 80% of budget. Other tiers unchanged at 1%. `test:audit` still 0/13,967. New gate `npm run test:session-length`.

- [x] **A CORRECTION I HAD TO MAKE TO ASHLEY MID-TASK, and the reason this entry leads with "loading weeks".** I first reported this as "one session in four when you set aside an hour or more, worst case 31 minutes of a 75-minute budget" and recommended it on that basis. That was inflated: I measured every week against the midpoint budget without separating **deload weeks**, which are half volume BY DESIGN and deliberately never filled. On loading weeks alone the real figures were 13% of one tier ("45-60") and 1% of the other two, worst case 69% not 42%. The worst-case example I had eyeballed from the quality report was almost certainly a deload too. The code comment beside the filler says deloads are meant to run short — I should have read it before quoting a number. Ashley was given the corrected figures and chose to proceed anyway. `test:session-length` now asserts on loading weeks only and separately asserts that **no deload is ever topped up**, so the same mistake can't be made silently again.

- [ ] FLAGGED — the residual 3% are all within ~3 minutes of the minimum, and the cause is ordering: `enforceWeeklyPatternBalance` trims sets AFTER `applyDurationFiller` has already sized itself, so a day that was long enough at filler time lands a couple of minutes under afterwards. Not chased: that pass is documented as needing to run last, after rotation, periodization and duration trimming have all had their say, and the gain is minutes.

- [ ] FOUND, NOT CAUSED — 69 "30-45" sessions run past 105% of their midpoint budget (worst 121%). Verified identical at HEAD. Not a defect: 121% of a 37-minute midpoint is 44 minutes, comfortably inside what a "30-45" trainee asked for. Recorded because the first version of `test:session-length` asserted against the midpoint and flagged all 69 — measuring against a number the trainee never saw. The gate now checks the stated maximum instead, and reads 0 across every tier.

- [x] THE WEEKS THAT REPEAT THEMSELVES — **built, on the sixth attempt.** Frozen week-to-week transitions **518 → 264 (9.0% → 4.6%)**; the bucket Ashley's ruling targets, loaded non-carry, **380 → 126 (−67%)**; tier1 main lifts 44 → 14, tier3 isolation 77 → 6. `test:audit` still **0/13,967** and `test:assumed-body` passes — the two gates that decided it. Plan + all six attempts: `docs/PLAN-the-weeks-that-repeat-themselves.md`. THE FIX IS AN ORDERING: **observe first, then pin.** The load is resolved once with BASE reps and no pin, which makes it an honest probe of the underlying prescription — *would this weight have moved on its own?* Only if the answer is no do reps go up (one more than last week's accumulated total) with the weight pinned to the lift's own previous natural figure so the extra rep can't drag it down. The probe must use base reps, never bumped ones: extra reps lower a standards estimate, so probing with them makes a lift look like it dropped rather than froze. Verified by reading a real 16-week plan, not just counters — squats run 52.5 → 55 → 55 with reps 4-6 → 5-7, then 50 → 52.5 → 52.5 with 6-8 → 7-9. New gate `npm run test:frozen-weeks`, measurement `npm run report:frozen-weeks`.

- [x] FOUR CORRECTIONS THE SIXTH ATTEMPT NEEDED, each caught by a gate rather than by reasoning: (1) **the pin must respect the divergence backstop** — the second `prescribeLoad` call passes `forceStartingWeightKg` directly and bypasses the 1.25x clamp every other forced weight goes through, so a rotated-in Walking Lunges held at 8kg against a 6kg fresh estimate (133%) tripped `rotation_relative_load`; when the pin would breach the band the right answer is to **decline the rep**, because buying a rep must never cost weight. (2) **One decision per lift per week** — the streak is name-keyed but was applied per slot, so a lift on two days incremented twice and the second slot came out a rep above the first: same exercise, same weight, "4-6" Monday and "5-7" Thursday. Lift-weeks showing two rep ranges at one weight went 19 → 108; now back to **19, exactly the HEAD baseline**, so this adds none. (3) **Deloads are not recorded as the comparison basis** — a deload is meant to differ, so using it would make every first week of a block look like it moved and no freeze could be detected across a block boundary; week 9 compares against week 7. (4) **Both sides of the comparison are pre-coherence** — mixing bases is what broke attempt 2. Measured to justify it: `enforceLoadCoherence` leaves only 4 of 2,406 loaded exercise-weeks above a clamp, so the bases barely differ, but consistency is the point, not closeness. Also corrected a wrong instruction in my own earlier write-up: "compare post-coherence on both sides" is unachievable, since a probe is necessarily pre-coherence for the current week.

- [ ] FLAGGED — **carries are 100 of the 254 remaining freezes and are left alone on purpose.** `shiftReps` passes '40m' straight through, so a rep bump there is silently inert; a carry's lever is distance, and whether carrying further should count as progress is a product question Ashley has not been asked. The gate asserts carries are STILL frozen, so the exclusion can't quietly rot into an accident. Bodyweight is another 38 — already reps-led, so something downstream flattens them; uninvestigated.

- [ ] FOUND, NOT CAUSED, NOT FIXED — **ten transitions raise reps while the weight drops**: "Lateral Raises wk9→10: 12-17@6 → 13-18@4". A reps-led lift crossing into a new block re-derives a lighter estimate while its reps climb, which is more reps at less weight — a deload wearing progress's clothes. Verified pre-existing by running the new gate against HEAD's engine: the **identical 10 cases**. `test:frozen-weeks` pins the count at 10 so it can't grow while someone decides what to do about it.

- [ ] THE WEEKS THAT REPEAT THEMSELVES — **diagnosed and measured, NOT BUILT.** Full write-up incl. five failed attempts: `docs/PLAN-the-weeks-that-repeat-themselves.md`. Ashley picked this off a measured shortlist and ruled: when the weight can't move, ask for an extra rep instead (6-8 → 7-9), over forcing the weight to the next plate (~5% jumps where the design wants 3.5%, compounding over 16 weeks, in the direction that costs an injury) and over leaving the numbers and just explaining the week. **That ruling stands; it is not what failed.** SCALE, measured — the quality report's 84.1% is NOT the useful number (it counts "at least one instance anywhere in a 16-week plan"); the real rate is **9.0% of week-to-week transitions** identical in load AND reps, worst in tier2 (14.3%). By cause: **73% loaded non-carry** (Ashley's ruling), 19% carries (`shiftReps` passes '40m' straight through — their lever is distance, and whether distance should progress is an unasked product question), 7% bodyweight (already reps-led, so something downstream flattens it — uninvestigated). MECHANISM, traced and NOT what I first assumed: not rounding (`UNVERIFIED_RAMP_STEP_KG` is 5kg vs a 2.5kg plate step) but `load-prescription.ts:1264` — the unverified ramp is a **ceiling approach**, `Math.min(previous + step, estimate)`, deliberately, because prescribing past the standards estimate for someone we've never seen lift would be inventing strength data. Once it converges on that ceiling the load pins there for good, and reps are held flat *because load was supposed to be the lever*. New gate-free measurement `npm run report:frozen-weeks`, split by cause so a fix that drives the total down while leaving a bucket untouched can't read as success.

- [ ] WHY IT WASN'T BUILT, and what the next attempt needs. Best result reached was **518 → 359 frozen transitions (9.0% → 6.2%)**, audit still 0/13,967 — and it **broke `test:assumed-body`**, the item-2b safety invariant that a trainee who declined their body metrics is never prescribed more than one who gave them. A stated 55kg/52/female profile's Romanian Deadlift collapsed to **6kg, below an empty bar**, ending up lighter than the same lift for the declined profile at 16kg — a 2.7x gap between near-identical bodies, so structural, not physiological. Root cause is a flaw in my own design: **the pin makes the freeze self-perpetuating** — pinning this week's load to last week's means this week equals last week, so the lift is still "stuck", so it pins again, and the weight can never escape even once the estimate would afford a real step. The pin is not optional either: reps feed `prescribeLoad` as `repRangeLabel`, so bumping reps WITHOUT a pin lets the weight fall, and more reps at less weight is a deload wearing progress's clothes — invisible, because the frozen-week counter falls either way. Both pinned and unpinned versions fail `test:assumed-body`, for opposite reasons. THE SIXTH ATTEMPT should compute the load first with unbumped reps and only bump-and-re-derive when it actually resolves equal to last week's post-coherence load — the one ordering where the pin cannot manufacture a freeze. Four traps now named and avoidable: compare post-`enforceLoadCoherence` on BOTH sides (comparing pre- against post- made it *worse*, 380 → 272); pin to the NAME-keyed previous load, never `Math.min(name, slot)` (the slot value belongs to last week's variation — pinning to it held a rotated-in lift at the outgoing variation's weight and produced 9 `rotation_relative_load` failures); don't age a lift's streak once per slot (an accessory on two days aged twice, hit the cap in half the time and froze outright — tier3 went 77 → 191); and `shiftReps` has a floor but NO ceiling, so any accumulating bump needs its own cap or a strength block's 4-6 quietly becomes an endurance block.

- [x] THE QUALITY SCORE HAD TO BE TOLD THAT REHAB IS A LEGITIMATE SECOND WARM-UP. **METRIC CHANGE — say it out loud: Structure readings for INJURED profiles before this are NOT comparable with readings after it.** Prescribing rehab in every session cost **0.11 overall (11.05 → 10.94)**, and Structure was the whole delta (**1.95 → 1.81**); every other dimension was flat or up. Attributed by stashing the work and re-running the sweep, not by guessing. One rule did it: `primer_not_first` flagged any primer past position 0, encoding "a session has exactly one warm-up" — true until rehab existed, wrong for injured trainees after, and at 0.4 per distinct rule type roughly a third of combinations newly tripped it. **Ashley's ruling: keep both warm-ups and fix the check.** The rejected alternative (rehab REPLACES the day's primer) would have restored 11.05 with nothing measured differently, at the cost of a shoulder-injured trainee having no leg preparation before squatting on leg day — a metric problem traded for a training one. The rule now accepts a non-first primer only when every exercise before it is also a primer AND it is indicated for a joint **this trainee actually reported**; deliberately NOT "any primer carrying `indicated_joints`", which would pass rehab to someone who never reported the injury — the same tag-answering-the-wrong-question shape as the five defects before it. Teeth asserted in `test:rehab-prescribed` §7: a non-rehab second warm-up still flagged, a rehab warm-up after the main lift still flagged, an uninjured profile with the identical day still flagged. The 1.81 was never plans getting worse and the recovery is not plans getting better; uninjured profiles are unaffected in both directions, so their figures stay comparable.

- [ ] FLAGGED by the new frequency table, pre-existing, NOT from this round — `selection/duplicate_movement_family` fires on **40 of 9,216** combinations (0.4%). Checked rather than assumed: generated the same sweep against the pre-rehab engine (`f7939ef^`) and got the identical count — **2 repeated-family days per 192 for a shoulder-injured trainee, 0 for uninjured and 0 for knees, before AND after**. The repeated family is `hip_hinge`, nothing to do with rehab: a shoulder injury thins the upper-body pool, so a bodyweight full-body session reaches for a second hinge. Small and long-standing; noted because the frequency table made it visible for the first time.

- [x] `run-quality-score.ts` NOW PRINTS A PER-RULE FREQUENCY TABLE across all 9,216 swept combinations, not just the ten worst-scoring plans it samples. Added because attributing that 0.11 drop cost stashing the work and running two 14-minute sweeps to discover a single rule was responsible — the worst-10 section answers "what does a bad plan look like", which is a different question from "what changed", and the second one gets asked far more. Counted per combination rather than per occurrence, to match how `scoreFromViolatedRules` actually penalises. Additive output; no scoring change.

- [x] REHAB IS ACTUALLY PRESCRIBED NOW, not merely permitted. Plan + what actually happened: `docs/PLAN-actually-prescribe-the-rehab.md`. `exercise-db.ts` has always documented `indicated_joints` as "the prep/rehab work a physio would prescribe FOR that joint... **the plan should deliberately include them** when the matching injury is present." Nothing implemented that sentence: `isIndicatedFor` was read in exactly two places, one to keep rehab ELIGIBLE and one to label a movement "Chosen to help your shoulder" — a sentence that was false, because nothing had chosen it. **MEASURED FIRST, and the number was not what I told Ashley:** I said the app "does nothing at all to help them", which described the mechanism right and the outcome wrong. Rehab already arrived on **51.0%** of a shoulder-injured trainee's days and **50.7%** of a knee-injured trainee's, by luck of the shuffle. The defect was never "no rehab" — it was rehab as a coin toss, plus **40 of 144 knee plans containing none at all across sixteen weeks**. AFTER: **100% / 100%**, uninjured control **0% before and after**, knee plans with none **40 → 0**. New `pickRehabMovement` picks the gentlest indicated movement — warm-up tier first, then isolation by ascending duration, **never a tier2 compound** — which is Ashley's ruling ("every session, gentlest option") made mechanical: it excludes Wall Sit and Spanish Squat from a guaranteed slot while leaving them available through ordinary ones. "Gentlest" had to be tier-then-duration because every knee-indicated entry is `joint_stress: 'low'`, so the obvious field cannot discriminate and using it would only look principled. Protected via the EXISTING `requiredNames`/`protectedNames` mechanism rather than a second one invented beside it — "in every session" and "first thing dropped when time runs short" cannot both be true. Deliberately bypasses `primer_pattern_affinity`: a shoulder needs its band pull-aparts on leg day too. New gate `npm run test:rehab-prescribed`, new measurement `npm run report:rehab-coverage`. `test:audit` **still 0/13,967** — the duration risk the plan flagged did not materialise.

- [x] A STYLE PREFERENCE WAS DELETING A SAFETY RESPONSE — found because the guarantee stalled at exactly 75% for knees. `stageStyleFilter` stripped every knee-rehab drill from anyone who picked **'bodybuilding'**: full gym left one survivor (Lying Leg Curl), home gym / minimalist / bodyweight left **zero**, because a seated short-arc quad set is tagged functional/hybrid and nobody tags a rehab drill 'bodybuilding'. `MIN_VIABLE_POOL` never fired — the pool stayed large, just missing the one category that mattered. That function's own comment already drew the line it was crossing ("style is a preference, not a safety constraint — unlike equipment or injury"). Indicated movements now survive the style filter; the exemption is gated to rehab only and asserted to be, so no other off-style movement rides in.

- [x] A REGRESSION I SHIPPED FOUR COMMITS EARLIER, found by running a gate I had not run that round. `test:injury-rebuild` was ALREADY failing before this work — verified by stashing, then bisected to **`6646854` "Shrugs are not shoulder work"**, my own commit, which I reported green because the gate list I ran did not include this one. It has been red on `main` since this morning's merge. Mechanism is that plan's own stated risk: splitting `isolation_trap` out of `isolation_shoulder` left traps holding exactly two movements, both shrugs, **both contraindicated for a NECK injury** — so a neck-injured trainee's 32 shrug slots had no same-pattern replacement, were dropped outright, and that reads downstream as "a whole pattern was wiped", forcing a full rebuild over a genuinely thinning injury. Before the split they fell back to lateral raises, which a neck injury allows. I verified the split did not orphan shrugs for an UNINJURED trainee and never checked an injured one; the plan named the invariant and my verification covered half of it. Underlying bug is general: `getSmartReplacements` demanded an exact `movement_pattern` match **with no fallback at all**, while plan-build has had `NEAREST_PATTERN_FALLBACK` for exactly this since before traps existed — any thin pattern could hit the same wall. Map re-homed to `exercise-db.ts` beside `MovementPattern`, both readers importing it, swap path now falls back instead of dropping. Over-fire check holds: a shoulder injury, which genuinely wipes `vertical_pull`, still forces a rebuild.

- [x] TWO WARM-UPS DOING THE SAME JOB — caught by reading a generated plan, not by a gate. A session opened "Scapular Push-Ups, Scapular Push-Ups": seven of nine shoulder-indicated movements are primers, so the ordinary primer pick and the rehab pick drew from the same small set, and `usedGroups` stopped `main` colliding with rehab while saying nothing about the primer. Where the day's primer is already indicated for the injured joint, no second rehab movement is added — the guarantee is that every session carries rehab, not that every session carries a rehab SLOT. Gate extended: no exercise appears twice in a session, across 384 injured days.

- [ ] FLAGGED — **five of the eight injury codes get nothing.** `getFlaggedJoints` maps only five to a joint at all (hips/ankles/elbows are collected at onboarding and map to nothing), and of those five only shoulder and knee have any `indicated_joints` entries. Someone reporting a hip, ankle, elbow, wrist or back problem sees no change from the work above. Whether the app should SAY so rather than stay quiet is a user-facing question for Ashley, not a code decision.

- [x] SHIPPED TO `main` — 24 commits, 22-24 Aug, merged fast-forward. `origin/main` had not moved since **17 Aug** (`66aeb26`), so everything from the coach-voice round onward was finished, gate-verified and invisible to users: the shrug placement fix, the one-arm press fix, honest loads without a body, the Home-tab spinner, the matching workout-day layouts, and the four onboarding phases. Ashley's ruling when asked: ship the fixes now rather than hold 23 unrelated ones behind a database command. - [x] SHRUGS ARE NOT SHOULDER WORK — fixed, audit still 0/13,967. Plan: `docs/PLAN-shrugs-are-not-shoulder-work.md`. Ashley ran a real generated session past Gemini, which flagged **Dumbbell Shrugs on a "Push & Press" day**. Right, and the cause was a tag: both shrug entries carried `movement_pattern: 'isolation_shoulder'`, a pattern whose four members were two lateral raises (deltoid) and two shrugs (trap), and `'Push & Press'` has an `isolation_shoulder` accessory slot. NOT one unlucky roll — swept over 288 training days, shrugs appeared on 59 and **31 of those were a pressing day**. The codebase already knew the tag was wrong: `categorize()` has carried a name-matched `shrug` special case, with a comment explaining it ("shrugs track deadlift; lateral raises track bench"), for as long as both shared the pattern — that workaround fixed the WEIGHT and never the PLACEMENT. **Third defect of this exact shape in as many rounds** (loads_joints for injuries, vertical_push for the landmine, now isolation_shoulder for placement): a tag answering one question used to answer another. Fix: new `isolation_trap` pattern, both entries retagged, `tier3_isolation` slots on `'Pull & Hinge'` and `'Upper Pull & Core'`, the three pattern-keyed maps updated deliberately (all `Partial<Record<>>`, so the compiler forced nothing), and `categorize()`'s switch taught the value. MEASURED: pressing-day shrugs **31 → 0**; still reachable on 54 of 288 days across two tracks (a fix that orphaned them would be worse than the bug); `categorize()` still `shrug` and the load **byte-identical at 28kg/hand** — this was placement, not load. New gate `npm run test:pattern-tags`, named for the class not the instance. `test:quality` over 9,216 combinations: **Selection 1.94 → 1.95**, every other dimension unchanged — small, but Selection is exactly the dimension a placement fix should move, and it was the only one that did.

- [x] TWO THINGS THE NEW GATE AND THE COMPILER CAUGHT, fixed with it: (1) a tier3 trap movement whose NAME lacks "shrug" resolved to **`isolation_chest`** — trap work priced as a cable fly. `categorize()` checks isolation tier first and that block's own switch has a chest `default`, so the fallback case at the bottom of the cascade is unreachable for isolation-tier entries; both switches now know the pattern. Nothing moved today (the two shrugs are name-matched) but the next trap movement added would have been mispriced silently. (2) `patternLabel` lost exhaustiveness — it is the one place a pattern name reaches a trainee (`pattern_gap_note`), so a missing case would have printed "undefined" into user-facing copy.

- [ ] FLAGGED — `'Back & Biceps'` is the textbook home for shrugs and deliberately does NOT get the trap slot. Swapping its duplicate `isolation_bicep` slot for one put that track's 30-45min sessions at **43min against a 37min budget** — 4 `duration` failures in an audit otherwise at zero, verified as caused (stashing the change returned it to 0), not merely revealed. That track is at its time ceiling at the short tier; shrugs have two other homes carrying all 54 placements. Revisit if the budget ever loosens.

- [ ] FROM THE SAME REVIEW, NOT DONE — a full-gym user was given **Band Shoulder Press** for a tier2 compound slot. Gemini's reason (bands lose tension at the bottom) is a fair training point but not the one that bites: a resistance band is not in `LOADED_EQUIPMENT`, so the app shows **no weight at all** and the lift can only progress by reps. Nothing ranks candidates by implement, so with dumbbells and a barbell available it is a coin flip. Changes candidate ranking rather than one tag, so it wants its own measurement pass. (Third point of that review — session length — is NOT a defect: the app reserves warm-up time before allocating exercises, `budgetSeconds = totalBudgetSeconds − warmupReserve`, and `estimateDaySeconds` counts the warm-up. Noted in passing: `getWarmupReserveSeconds`' comment says 15%/5min/12min while the code is 20%/6.5min/14min — doc drift only.)

- [x] A ONE-ARM PRESS SCALED OFF THE BENCH PRESS — fixed, audit still at 0/13,967. Plan: `docs/PLAN-a-one-arm-press-off-the-bench.md`. `Landmine Press` was prescribed **132.5kg** to a 120kg advanced male whose two-arm barbell overhead press is 80kg — a ONE-ARMED press at **1.66x the two-arm press**. Never the exercise: `categorize()` picks a standard by matching words in the NAME and then falls back on `movement_pattern`, and the fallback collapsed `horizontal_push` and `vertical_push` into one `return 'bench'`. Right for horizontal push; wrong for vertical, which has its own materially lighter standard (0.65 vs 1.0 x bodyweight at intermediate). Only ONE exercise was reaching it, because the other three vertical pushes are caught by the name rules above — correct **by their names, not by construction**, so any future overhead movement missing that substring list fell through the same trapdoor. Nothing caught it either: `SAFETY_CEILING_KG` is keyed by category, so sitting in `bench` gave it a 220kg ceiling instead of overhead's 140kg. Fix is one line (split the case); the landmine now resolves to `overhead` directly with no new scale entry and inherits the tighter ceiling. **132.5 → 80kg** / 75 → 47.5 / 45 → 30. DELIBERATELY NOT halved despite `unilateral: true`: one arm handles ~half what two do, but the floor pivot carries part of the load so the bar-end number reads higher than the hand feels — modelling each separately means inventing a lever coefficient, so the overhead standard asserts only that they cancel. `test:per-side-load` extended with the invariant that needs no coefficient — **a one-arm press never exceeds the same trainee's two-arm press** — plus the trapdoor itself (every `vertical_push` must resolve to `overhead`), the over-fire check (`horizontal_push` still `bench`), and byte-identical numbers for the three lifts that were already right.

- [ ] FLAGGED — the landmine now sits **level with** the two-arm press, not below it: the gate's closest case is 87.5kg vs 87.5kg, holding with zero margin. Least certain number in that change. Conservative alternative is `{ parent: 'overhead', scale: 0.8 }` → 64 / 38 / 24 / 20kg. Revisit if a real trainee finds it heavy; the file's doctrine is that too light costs a boring set and too heavy costs a shoulder.

- [x] THE CONSTRAINT AUDIT IS AT ZERO — 54 failures → **0**, all 13,967 combinations passing. Two defects, one found by fixing the other. Plan: `/root/.claude/plans/help-me-plan-how-declarative-parrot.md`.

- [x] (1) ONE ARM, BOTH ARMS' WEIGHT. `prescribeLoad` decided per-side vs total with `isDumbbell || (mode === 'single_implement' && entry.unilateral)`, written out by hand at three call sites. A cable lateral raise is `unilateral: true` but its equipment lands in `loadingMode()`'s `stack` bucket, so one arm was handed the number derived for two: **37.5kg for a 120kg advanced male against a 25kg category ceiling**, 51 of the 54 remaining audit failures, all one exercise. Same defect already fixed once for one-arm kettlebell carries; the fix never covered stacks. Now one named `isPerSideLoad` covering all three shapes — dumbbell pair, unilateral single implement, unilateral weight stack — keyed on real machine equipment (`'cable machine' | 'machine' | 'leg press machine' | 'hack squat machine'`) and NOT on the `stack` bucket, which is a fallback holding 17 bodyweight/band unilateral entries. `labelModeForEntry` and `estimateEffectiveTotalKg` both route through it: the first so the caption can't say "total" over a halved number, the second so the newly-halved lift isn't read as half its real demand by the rotation guard (that would have traded one audit failure for another). Measured after: heaviest cable lateral raise in the whole sweep **17.5kg**, `load_cap` 51 → **0**.

- [x] (2) A BLOCK BASELINE SURVIVING A ROTATION — found because (1) made it fire more (rotation_relative_load 3 → 9 before this). `blockBaselineKg`/`blockWeek3Kg` are keyed by SLOT POSITION, but an accessory's variation rotates on a two-week sub-cycle WITHIN the block, so from week 3 the slot builds on a stranger's week-1 number. The file already states the rule ("a capped or broken anchor must never propagate through a rotation") and implemented it as a 25% divergence backstop — porous in both directions: the comparison is exclusive so exactly 125% passes, and plate rounding then rounds that value UP after the check. TRACED LIVE (temporary instrumentation, reverted): a 50kg advanced female carried a 5kg Cable Lateral Raises baseline into Lateral Raises, passed the backstop at exactly 5 against a 5.0 limit, and rounded to 6kg — 150% of a fresh estimate. Now name-keyed, the same treatment `lastUnverifiedLoadingWeekKg` needed one tracker over: a rotated slot has no baseline and falls through to a fresh estimate for the exercise actually in it. rotation_relative_load 9 → **0**.

- [x] New gate `npm run test:per-side-load`: the rule on all three shapes and its non-firing on four (bodyweight unilateral, band unilateral, bilateral cable, bilateral single implement); label and rotation-comparison agreement across EVERY database entry; the cable number under its ceiling and still a real prescription; and 286 real mid-block rotations checked for an inherited load. Seconds, versus the full audit's 75.

- [x] WEIGH-INS NOW REACH THE LIFTING WEIGHTS — offer built, Ashley's "ask first" ruling. Plan: `/root/.claude/plans/help-me-plan-how-declarative-parrot.md`. Weighing yourself updated food targets and nothing else: `computeTargets` prefers the latest `daily_metrics` reading, the training side reads only the immutable signup `weight_kg`, and `generateMesocycle` runs once at onboarding — so the 16 weeks of loads written at signup were the loads forever. Item 2b made that cost MORE (a declining 100kg man went from 0.76x to 0.35x of his real loads), so closing it was the completion of that work, not an extra. A weigh-in (or a weight typed into Profile) now produces a durable offer — new `weight_basis_offers` table modelled on `load_suggestions`, NOT `pending_actions` (10-minute expiry; a one-shot that vanished would restore the very problem the ruling avoided) — naming the largest real change ("would take Trap Bar Deadlift from 47.5kg to 95kg"). Confirm rebuilds the live week onward, never a past week, through the injury rebuild's machinery (extracted as `rebuildAgainstProfile` so week-identity preservation isn't re-derived per caller). Decline is permanent. MEASURED (`run-assumed-body-report.ts`, new third column, via the real rebuild path): declined WEIGHT only **0.89x → 0.98x**; declined all three **0.35x → 0.57x** — NOT the ~1.00x the plan predicted, because a weigh-in supplies one of three metrics and sex is the larger term (female standards are 0.53-0.67x male). Supply sex later and the offer fires again, which is why no wording claims the weight was the missing piece. New gate `npm run test:weight-basis`. **MIGRATION NOT APPLIED — `npm run db:push-both` still owed; `*.supabase.co` is 403 from the sandbox.**

- [x] Three bugs the new gate caught, fixed with it: (1) "the plan holds `assumed_body` loads" looks like the eligibility rule and is NOT — someone who declined weight, age AND sex still has an unknown sex after a weight rebuild, so the flag stays true and the offer would re-ask on every app load; `rebuildChangesAnything` is the real test. (2) The headline change was "largest INCREASE", silently skipping the one case with a real safety cost — a trainee LIGHTER than the light stand-in, carrying numbers too heavy for them. Now largest absolute change, either direction. (3) Preview and confirm each called `generateMesocycle` with the default RNG, so they selected different exercises — the offer could name a lift the applied rebuild never contained. Surfaced as a flaky assertion that passed or failed on what `Math.random` returned; both runs are now seeded on profile id + basis weight.

- [x] BUG (prerequisite, found while browser-verifying the above): the Home tab spun on "Loading your day…" FOREVER for anyone who declined a body metric. `computeTargets` returns null for them and `Dashboard`'s load effect required `macros` while `loading` initialises true. It also made the weigh-in card unreachable (it lives inside `Dashboard`) — so the weight-basis offer's main trigger could not fire for the persona it was built for. **Correction to a previous session's claim:** I saw this same spinner during the 2b browser run and attributed it to the local mock returning empty rows. That was wrong — it reproduces with a real database and the mock was irrelevant. `loadDashboardData` now takes `MacroTargets | null` and reports `hasNutritionTargets`; the calorie tile renders "0 kcal · no target yet" with no ring rather than "of 0 kcal" (the placeholder `MissingBodyMetricsNotice`'s own doctrine forbids). Gated in `test:dashboard`. Also fixed in the same run: the weight-basis confirm handler posted "Done — the rest of your plan now uses your real weight" even when the rebuild returned null and nothing was written.

- [x] Item "2b" — HONEST LOADS WITHOUT A BODY, built. Plan: `docs/PLAN-honest-loads-without-a-body.md`. `resolveParentOneRepMaxKg`'s three `||`s (`weight_kg || 75`, `gender === 'female' ? … : 'male'`, `age || 30`) turned a refusal into a 30yo 75kg man for every prescribed kilo in the app. MEASURED FIRST (`npm run report:trim-magnitude`'s sibling, new `scripts/run-assumed-body-report.ts`, matched pairs, same seed): a 55kg 52yo woman who declined all three was prescribed a mean **2.01x** her own week-1 loads, **2.09x** by week 5, 18 of 18 shared lifts >15% too heavy, worst 3.00x — every one labelled "suggested". AFTER: **0.94x / 0.94x, zero lifts over-prescribed**. THE FIX THAT WORKED WAS NOT THE PLANNED ONE: damping-while-assumed was built first and measured to move the week-1 ratio not at all (2.01x → 2.01x — a stated profile's week 1 is *also* a calibration week, so the same multiplier hit both sides and cancelled). The fabrication is in the body, so the body changed: `ASSUMED_BODY` is now 50kg/female/60 — deliberately the light end of the adult range, because prescribing a light trainee 2x what she can lift is an injury and prescribing a heavy trainee half is one boring set he corrects by logging. No second multiplier on top (a conservative body AND a discount compounds to a 100kg man on 2kg dumbbells). Also: new `load_source: 'assumed_body'` (chip reads "starting light", not "suggested"; no migration — JSONB), `isUnverifiedLoadSource()` so adding the state couldn't silently break every `=== 'estimate'` comparison, calibration can no longer be skipped on an assumed body OR on a "yes I know my numbers" with zero numbers entered (that path measured **2.22x → 0.93x**, and now gets a calibration week at all), the basis string stops claiming it came "from strength standards for your bodyweight, sex and experience", `MissingBodyMetricsNotice` stops claiming "your training plan is unaffected" when weight/age/sex is what's missing, and Ashley's ruling 1 — one more ask at the review, with an inline "Add it now". Exercise selection was contaminated by the same substitution (`isolationTargetBelowFloor` handed a 50kg woman a 20kg barbell curl); fixed for free at the chokepoint. New gate `npm run test:assumed-body`. Verified live in the browser against the local mock: review line renders, plan opens on "Wk 1/16 · B1 Calibration", Deadlifts show 22.5kg under STARTING LIGHT.

- [x] BUG found by 2b's own gate, fixed with it: the unverified per-week ramp tracker (`lastUnverifiedLoadingWeekKg`) is keyed by SLOT POSITION, and variations rotate at block boundaries — so a lift returning to a slot it left weeks ago stepped up from whatever had been sitting there. Observed: Romanian Deadlifts 8kg (wk3) → absent a block → **16kg** (wk9), a doubling on an unverified lift inherited from a stranger. Breaks the invariant `UNVERIFIED_RAMP_STEP_KG` is written around, and is the same contamination `forceStartingWeightKg` already guards against. Now also keyed by lift name; the slot value stays as the fallback for a genuinely new variation, since discarding it would reintroduce the block-boundary snap the ramp exists to prevent. Measured effect on `test:quality` across 9216 combinations: overall 11.04 → 11.05 / 12, Progression 1.65 → 1.66 (fewer `frozen_week` findings), every other dimension unmoved.

- [x] PHASE 4 (final phase of the onboarding plan): a plan can't have been missed before it existed. `classifyDay` (useTrainingWeek.ts) ended `dateStr < todayStr ? 'missed' : 'due'` with no plan-start notion — observed live: a profile created MINUTES earlier, opened on a Saturday, showed Mon/Wed/Fri all ○ (missed) and "0 of 3 sessions done". DECISION (Ashley): pre-plan days are not part of the plan and don't count — new `before_plan` state renders a faint "·" (not a rest day the plan chose, not a missed one), and the week tally excludes it via the new exported `countsTowardWeekTally`, so onboarding Thursday with Mon/Wed/Fri gives "0 of 1". SCOPE CHECKED FIRST: the hook is UI-only (TodayPanel/WeekStrip/WeekContextRow; NOTHING in src/lib imports it), so accountability, streaks and block review — which read logged sessions — are untouched. This is display, not progression. No new plumbing needed: App.tsx already threads `planCreatedAt={mesocycleCreatedAt ?? profile.created_at}` to ExerciseTab, and mesocycleCreatedAt is set on restore (from mesocycle_weeks.created_at) AND on generation, so it survives reload and moves on regeneration; converted to a LOCAL date before comparing, since everything else in the hook is getLocalDateString space and a UTC instant would misjudge the plan's own first day around midnight. MISTAKE CAUGHT BY THE NEW GATE: I first put the pre-plan check BEFORE the completed/part-logged checks, which would have displayed a session someone actually logged on a pre-plan day as "not part of your plan" — erasing real work for a tidier calendar. Logged work now outranks every date judgement. New gate `test:training-week` exists precisely because the calendar can't reproduce this on demand (a plan created today only has pre-plan days if the run happens Tuesday or later) and asserts BOTH directions so the guard can't over-fire. Also fixed: WeekStrip's aria-label interpolated the raw state name (screen readers announced "partial", would have announced "before_plan") — now a spoken-English label map. The "0 of 0 sessions" case needed nothing: RestDayCard already guards on planned > 0. All client-side, no deploy.

- [x] PHASE 3 of the onboarding plan: honest progress, and a way back. (1) THE BAR WAS LYING — `requiredCount` counted required slots only while `readyToGenerate` also waits on `unconfirmedOptional`, so after Phase 2 it filled completely with ~7 ask-anyway questions still to come. Denominator is now every slot that can actually hold a plan up (applicable AND not never-blocking = exactly required + ask-anyway), and the gate asserts that identity against its own blocking count so it cannot drift back. Numerator now counts `confirmed` not `values`, which fixes a SECOND bug free: `handleToggleMulti` writes values on every chip tap BEFORE Done, so merely touching one training-day chip nudged the bar and dropped the slot from the missing list. Browser-measured: 0/17 at start → 18/18 exactly when the review opens (max moves 17→18 as knowsWorkingLifts becomes applicable — the requiredIf-aware denominator working). Bar also gained role=progressbar + aria-valuenow (was invisible to screen readers); kept WORDLESS per the existing comment recording why "12 of 18 answered" was removed. (2) THERE WAS NO WAY TO CHANGE AN ANSWER — review rows are now buttons that re-open that question's card. Deliberately WITHOUT clearing `confirmed`: clearing flips readyToGenerate false, so anyone who opened a row then changed their mind would face a disabled Generate with nothing saying why — a NEW dead end in the pass meant to remove them. Leaving it confirmed is safe because applySlot has no confirmed-guard on write and commitWorkingState resolves every card carrying that slot key. The model-side guard in executeActions is untouched, so the coach still can't re-ask an answered question; only the user can. Verified live: Fat Loss → Muscle Growth, Generate enabled before/during/after. SlotNumericCard needed an explicit `editing` mode (it hides confirmed fields, so a re-opened numeric would have rendered empty), and the flag persists on DraftMessage so a refresh mid-edit doesn't leave an empty card. (3) Generate greyed out silently when readyToGenerate flipped — now reachable via an edit (equipment → barbell tier makes working-lifts newly required) — it now names what's outstanding. (4) Composer was hard-disabled while busy, dropping the phone keyboard EVERY turn; now readOnly so focus and keyboard stay (the Enter handler already refused to send while busy), matching the softer treatment chips already used. Opening message now says once that any answer can be changed later. All client-side — no deploy needed.

- [x] The name is now genuinely optional, end to end (Ashley's follow-up to Phase 2). Two of the three properties were ALREADY true and are now verified + gate-locked rather than assumed: continuing without a name works (never-blocking + nullable column), and nothing addresses a nameless user by name — `greetName()` returns plain "Hey", and chat-gemini interpolates the whole "Their name is X, use it occasionally" clause ONLY when a name exists, so the model is never told to use one it hasn't got (browser-confirmed: "Hey — I'm your coach. Good to meet you."). A name offered later DURING onboarding was already capturable because buildSlotCatalog filters on applicability only — now gate-asserted, since demoting a slot without keeping it in the catalog would silently break exactly that. THE REAL GAP, now fixed: after onboarding there was NO way to capture a name at all — ProfileScreen had no name control (while APP_REALITY tells the coach the Profile screen has "editable identity fields", so it could have sent someone to a control that doesn't exist), and chat-gemini had no tool writing display_name (record_fact's kind enum is food/exercise/timing/hard_constraint). Added `set_display_name`, dispatched exactly like record_context_fact — verbatim-quote gated so a name can't be invented, forwarded not written (I1 holds), executed client-side via the existing updateProfileField with onProfileChanged keeping App state in step. DECISION (Ashley): save it and say it back, NO confirmation card — a name mutates nothing about the plan; the receipt is what keeps "no silent writes" true. Deliberately no undo token (correcting a name is saying another one). ProfileScreen gains a Name row — savePatch was already generic, but the input is new because the existing `EditableTextField` is misleadingly named and is actually a NUMBER field. NEEDS `npx supabase functions deploy chat-gemini` for the chat tool; the Profile row works without a deploy.

- [x] PHASE 2 of the onboarding plan: trimmed the ask set to what shapes the first plan (Ashley's ruling). Questions that can block a plan 23 → 18, MEASURED by the gate rather than asserted (17 fresh / 18 for a barbell lifter, was 22/23), and the same scripted persona through the real UI now reaches the same 16-week plan in 10 conversational turns instead of 13. REMOVED: the time-of-day question — onboarding-slots.ts already documented every answer producing a byte-identical plan and mesocycle, its only consumer is a chat greeting that already reads `preferred_time || 'morning'`, and it was lossy by design (5 answers → 2, with 3 of the 5 branches in the greeting map unreachable); preferred_time now written as that same default. DEMOTED to never-blocking (still asked when the conversation goes there, all editable later in Profile, just unable to hold the door shut): cookingTime (was required:true for ONE sentence of a meal prompt), favoriteCuisines, breakfastStyle, displayName (was required:true for a greeting). UNTOUCHED deliberately: goal/experience/activity/equipment/days/session length/style/recovery/conditioning/meals-per-day (each measured to move the output) plus injuries and dietaryPreferences (safety path, stay blocking per CLAUDE.md). DEVIATION from the approved plan: it also proposed folding includeSnacks into mealsPerDay "to save a question" — NOT done, because includeSnacks is already in NEVER_BLOCKING_SLOTS so it was never one of the 23, and folding it would conflate meal count with snacks for zero reduction. The plan's "~15" estimate leaned on that; 18 is the real, measured figure. Observation, not fixed: with displayName never-blocking the model has less pressure to capture a name — in practice the opening message asks for it first, so it costs no turns either way, but a user who opens with something else could finish nameless (the greeting falls back to no name).

- [x] PHASE 1 of the onboarding plan (docs — see the approved plan): the refusal trap, round two. Age/height/weight/sex were `required: false` but NOT in NEVER_BLOCKING_SLOTS, so `unconfirmedOptionalSlots` held them until `confirmed`, which only came from a value passing `validate` — `isNumberIn` rejects '', gender had no third option, neither card had a skip. A user who wouldn't give a weight could answer all 22 other questions and never reach Generate. Same trap the absence work fixed in the calculation layer, still sitting in the completion gate. Fixed with `canDeclineSlot` (exactly "not currently required" — goal/days/equipment and the injuries/diet safety path stay unskippable), `declineSlots` writing null EXPLICITLY (inferring from "confirmed but empty" was tried and is wrong: numerics initialise to ''), a "Prefer not to say" button on both cards, and a new `decline_slot` tool so a TYPED refusal works too — the client re-checks canDeclineSlot so the model's instruction is enforced, not trusted. Also fixed in the same pass: the insert-error message was UNREACHABLE (setupError set, then setProfile runs, and that screen lives inside `if (!profile)`) — now a dismissible banner, so a user whose profile didn't save is actually told; and SlotNumericCard's all-blank Save was a silent no-op directly under a comment promising it never is. Verified in-browser both ways (button + model tool): reaches a 16-week plan, four rows read "Not given", and NO daily_metrics weigh-in row is seeded — no fabricated weight. Gate `test:onboarding-slots` extended with what can/cannot be declined. NEEDS EDGE-FUNCTION DEPLOY (`npx supabase functions deploy onboarding-chat`) for the decline_slot tool to exist live; the button works without it.

- [x] Onboarding free-flow + nonsense handling. Measured defect (browser, real UI, 10 unusable answers in a row): the goal question appeared 3x with a duplicate chip grid each time and the same "Let's lock this one in" sentence every time. Three causes, all fixed. (1) The stuck-slot breaker had no already-live-card guard — `executeActions` has one (`alreadyLive`) precisely because duplicate cards were a bug there before, and the breaker was written without it, so every stalled turn pushed a fresh identical card. (2) It always forced `remaining[0]`, hammering one question; it now rotates past a slot already forced twice, prefers a tappable question over a typed one, and says so ("we'll park that one"). (3) Every app-authored fallback line was a fixed string; breaker leads and the resolver's floor opener now CYCLE (not clamp — clamping repeats the last variant forever, which was the first cut of this fix). STALL_TURN_LIMIT 6→4. Prompt side: explicit unusable-answer handling (record nothing, never reuse your last deflection, make it easier instead; two strikes then move on; "I don't know"/"you decide" get a recommendation to confirm, not the question again), and STILL UNKNOWN restated as explicitly NOT an order. Verified in-browser same script: goal question 3x→1x, identical repeated leads 4→0, four different questions offered instead of one; happy path unchanged at 13 turns to a 16-week plan, no console errors. NOTE: verified against a LOCAL MOCK of the model and DB (this session's egress blocks *.supabase.co), so the app-side behaviour is proven and the model-side prompt rules are NOT — they need the live probe alongside the still-pending warmth measurement.
- [x] DECISION (Ashley, asked 2026-08-23): a required question the user keeps dodging is never skipped or defaulted — the app keeps asking and will not build a plan without it. Options put: (a) keep asking, never build without it; (b) offer a sensible default they confirm after several dodges; (c) let them skip and mark the plan provisional. Chose (a): injuries and equipment genuinely change what's prescribed, and a shrugged-through "no injuries" would be indistinguishable from a real one in the safety filter. This is what the client already enforces (complete_onboarding is refused until every required slot validates and every ask-anyway slot was answered or skipped), so the ruling is a confirmation, not a change — the parking behaviour above defers a question, it never drops one.

- [ ] Coach warmth, take 2 — reply guarantee BUILT and gate-verified, prompt loosened, NOT deployed and NOT live-measured (this session's sandbox egress policy blocks *.supabase.co, so neither the tone probe nor a deploy could reach TEST). The earlier finding — "react every turn" was load-bearing for RELIABILITY, not voice — is now answered in code: onboarding-chat's recovery chain moved to reply-resolver.ts and closes the four holes that let silence through (an entirely-empty model turn skipped recovery; sanitize ran after the legs so leak-shaped replies shipped as ""; the text-only leg was allowed to fail; no deterministic floor). A 200 response can no longer carry empty reply text — proven by construction and by the mocked-model gate `test:reply-guarantee` (47 checks: every measured failure shape, ≤3 extra model calls per turn, 0 on turns that already spoke), NOT yet proven live. TO FINISH (any session with Supabase access): (1) probe the currently-deployed function for the baseline: `npx tsx scripts/probe-onboarding-tone.mts scripts/probe-personas/warmth-measure.json baseline.json` — expect empty-reply 0/7 and the graded-every-answer voice; (2) `npx supabase functions deploy onboarding-chat` at the guarantee commit (1aa451e) → re-probe, expect empty-reply 0/7 and voice unchanged; (3) deploy the prompt commit (5f2eb90) → re-probe, expect graded-answer turns ~0 and empty-reply STILL 0/7 (the guarantee earning its keep — this is the exact configuration that previously produced 4/7 empty). The probe now prints `empty-reply turns: N/M` itself, and counts transport errors separately so an unreachable function is never misread as model silence. Client dead-air guard kept as defense in depth.
- [ ] BUG, exposed (not caused) by the equipment-quality preference: rotating a cable isolation exercise to its dumbbell sibling at the week-3 sub-cycle boundary re-estimates the load ~50% high — "Cable Curls -> Dumbbell Curls at 6kg, 150% of a fresh 4kg estimate", same for Cable Lateral Raises -> Lateral Raises. Already failing 3× before the equipment change; now 5×, purely because more profiles correctly land on the cable version in week 1 and therefore reach the faulty rotation. The `rotation_relative_load` check's own 125% band is what catches it. No new defect class — the same shape, wider exposure. Worth fixing at the rotation load-estimate, not by widening the band.

Time-bounded path rebuilds only its own week window, so a temporary rebuild is exactly as temporary as the adaptation (pre_image restores it). test:injury-rebuild drives the real executor: 432 slots rebuilt vs 320 substituted, zero contraindicated either way.

- [x] RESOLVED, verified 25 Aug — `pattern_coverage` is now **0** in `test:audit` (whole sweep 0 / 13,967). Original entry kept below for the record. ~~NEW, newly-visible (not a regression): pattern_coverage 276 → 683.~~ Every added failure is "plan has zero <pattern> despite the pool having N eligible candidates" for INJURED profiles — previously N was 0 so the gap was honest and silent; now a press exists and the generator still doesn't select it. Dominant case: 432× "zero horizontal_push despite 1 candidate". - [ ] NOT DONE from the queue: knees/lower-back/wrists tag audit (knees still has ZERO indicated movements, so a knee rebuild contains no rehab — same defect shape as shoulders had); rest of BACKLOG below.

- [ ] **JOINT-LOAD TAG AUDIT (shoulder) — done, decision needed before the rebuild.** Ashley's challenge was right: the 146 is *partly* an over-broad tag. How the filter decides: `injuries:['shoulders']` → flagged joint `shoulder` → exclude every exercise whose `loads_joints` array contains it. 42 of 105 exercises carry that tag. THE STRUCTURAL PROBLEM: `loads_joints` answers "does this joint participate?", and it is being used to answer "is this dangerous for an injured joint?" Those are different questions, and conflating them is what makes the filter blunt. Breakdown of the 42: **~29 genuinely contraindicated** (all 11 horizontal_push, all 3 vertical_push, all 5 vertical_pull, dips, lateral raises, overhead carry/tricep work) — a cuff injury really does rule out pressing and overhead pulling, so the plan being gutted is mostly real, not an artefact. **~13 wrongly excluded**, and these are the damning ones: the 9 `activation` movements are Band Pull-Aparts, Wall Slides, Prone Y-T Raises, Band Dislocates, Band Face Pulls, Arm Circles, Scapular Push-Ups — *these are rotator-cuff rehab exercises, the things a physio actually prescribes FOR this injury* — plus Face Pulls and Rear Delt Flyes (standard shoulder-health work), and Hanging Leg Raises / Ab Wheel Rollout (core movements where the shoulder is a passive hang, not loaded through range). So the app currently responds to a shoulder injury by removing shoulder rehab. SECOND FINDING: there are **zero shoulder-friendly pressing variants in the DB at all** — no floor press, no neutral-grip press, no chest-supported row. That is a content gap, not a filter gap; even a perfect filter can't substitute what doesn't exist. Fixing the tag would take the exclusion from 42 → ~29 and, critically, would let a rebuilt plan CONTAIN the rehab work. It would not restore pressing. Ashley's chosen direction (rebuild the plan around the injury) still stands, but should be built on the corrected tag, not the current one.
- [ ] **DECISION NEEDED — injury adaptation removes instead of substituting (the "146 removals" / "drops 10 of 13 slots" bug).** ROOT CAUSE FOUND, not fixed (injury path = plan first). `getSmartReplacements` requires the replacement share the outgoing exercise's `movement_pattern`; the injury filter removes conflicting exercises from the candidate pool BEFORE that. When an injury wipes out an entire pattern, the same-pattern candidate list is empty **by construction**, so every slot in that pattern is dropped — 100% drop rate, not a ranking problem. Measured, full_gym pool 102 exercises: **shoulders wipes out horizontal_push (11→0), vertical_pull (5→0), vertical_push (3→0)** — i.e. all pressing and all vertical pulling, which is exactly the 146 slots. knees wipes knee_dominant/single_leg/isolation_quad/isolation_hamstring (13 exercises). lower_back, wrists, neck wipe nothing (they only thin patterns, which is why those injuries have never looked broken). So this fires hard for shoulders and knees only. What a correct adaptation should look like is a coaching decision — see the question put to Ashley (options: regenerate the program around the injury / allow cross-pattern substitutes / drop-and-rebalance volume / keep dropping but be honest in the card).
- [x] Slot-replacement data inheritance — CLASS fixed (3rd instance of this shape). Audited every path that changes which exercise occupies a slot. Load: recomputed correctly on ALL of them (swap, ban, injury adaptation, equipment adaptation, block/weekly rotation, floor-clamp substitution) — so "load inheritance" was the wrong diagnosis. What actually leaked was everything ELSE: swap/ban/injury/equipment all funnel through `applyReplacement`, which shallow-spread the outgoing slot, carrying over `prescription_type` + `reps` (units — "40m" left on a rep lift and vice versa), `ramp_up` (**the outgoing lift's per-set warm-up kg ladder, displayed under the incoming lift's name** — the actual wrong-exercise-load leak) and the assistance-chip fields. Fixed at the choke point; canonical non-reps prescriptions extracted to `fixedUnitPrescription()` so generator and edit path can't drift. New gate `test:slot-replacement` asserts the invariant (no outgoing-exercise data survives) rather than the three instances — it caught a real gap in the first cut of the fix. Rotation is NOT a leak: its carry-forward is bounded by the incoming exercise's own fresh estimate, plus a 25% divergence guard on the weeks-2/3 path.
- [x] SAFETY_CEILING_KG recalibrated across 50-120kg, both sexes, all experience tiers, all rep/RPE brackets (was: one 80kg male). 7 categories were breached by a legitimate 120kg-advanced-male estimate (carry, isolation bicep/tricep/chest/quad/hamstring, deadlift marginally) — each raised to ~25% above its highest legitimate observed value. 2 categories had NO ceiling at all (`overhead_carry`, `single_leg_dumbbell`) — a missing entry silently exempts a category from the check entirely; both now bounded. **isolation_shoulder deliberately left at 25kg and still failing**: a 40kg Cable Lateral Raise is not a real prescription at any bodyweight, so the 0.19-of-bench shoulder-isolation fraction is the bug, not the ceiling — raising it would silence a genuine defect. load_cap failures 706 → 55, and all 55 residual are that one category. Total audit failures 530 → 343.
- [ ] **Pre-ceiling loads persisted in the DB — count blocked, backfill proposed, NOT run.** The per-implement ceiling only landed 2026-08-12, so every plan generated before then has uncapped numbers already written to `mesocycle_weeks`; a code fix does not clean stored rows. Built `scripts/count-preceiling-loads.ts` (SELECT-only, validated on TEST: 4 profiles / 64 week-rows / 1040 loaded slots → 3 over-ceiling slots on 1 profile, worst 52kg Romanian Deadlifts vs a 50kg ceiling). **Cannot run against PRODUCTION**: prod credentials are not in the repo (`.env` = the old pre-split project `aoksyzjrrikvuhxatljy`, `.env.local` = TEST). To get the real number: `AUDIT_SUPABASE_URL=<prod> AUDIT_SUPABASE_KEY=<prod anon> npx tsx scripts/count-preceiling-loads.ts`. PROPOSED BACKFILL (do not run without a decision): for each affected slot, re-run `prescribeLoad` for that exercise/profile and write the clamped value back, preserving everything else — but note this silently changes numbers a user may already have trained against, so the honest alternative is to surface it in-app ("some loads in your plan were above what that equipment can hold; we've corrected them") rather than mutate quietly. Prefer the in-app route.

- [ ] Item 2 (repeat instruction): base single-week audit grid also extended with bodyweight/gender (6 combos, matching the mesocycle sweep) — total combos 2367→13967, ~38s runtime (not the blow-up worried about). Failures 48→530. Breakdown: `load_cap` 0→706 (brand new — see below), `pattern_coverage` 46→276, `duration` 1→11, `rotation_relative_load` 0→3 (unchanged, mesocycle-sweep-only). Headline finding: `load_cap` checks against SAFETY_CEILING_KG, a CATEGORY-level backstop (isolation_chest 60kg, isolation_bicep 45kg, isolation_quad 90kg, carry 90kg, etc.) explicitly documented as "generous enough that a correct estimate for ANY profile should never approach them" — turns out they regularly ARE exceeded at 100-120kg male / 50kg female extremes, since that ceiling table was only ever checked against the one 80kg-male body the grid used to run. This is a DIFFERENT bug from the per-implement `loading_ceiling` mechanism reported below — that one is caught by a real clamp; this one is a category-level backstop that itself needs raising or rethinking for extreme bodyweights. Report-only, no fix, per the load-prescription plan-first rule.
- [ ] BUG, ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED, NOT FIXED — real week-by-week reproduction added. Built a live trace (temporary instrumentation, fully reverted after) reproducing the exact 62kg-advanced-female mesocycle-safety-sweep combo. For THIS specific profile, Goblet Squats' own trajectory stayed sane (10→16kg over 16 weeks — its 0.35 squat-scale keeps the fresh-estimate ceiling low enough that the ramp never gets dangerous) — could not reproduce the cited 126-154kg figure for Goblet Squats specifically under the current code; if that number came from watching the app live, the exact profile/exercise would help chase it further. But the underlying mechanism is real and dangerous elsewhere: a weekly-ROTATING accessory slot (e.g. Romanian Deadlifts ↔ Good Mornings ↔ Kettlebell Swing (Heavy), all sharing one `lastUnverifiedLoadingWeekKg` tracker per SLOT, not per exercise) climbed a clean, unbroken +2kg-or-so-per-loading-week line for all 16 weeks regardless of which exercise was actually in the slot that week — confirmed empirically: 30→32→37.5→(deload)→42.5→47.5→48(clamped)→(deload)→50(clamped)→50(clamped)→55→(deload)→60→65→48(clamped)→(deload), for an 80kg advanced male. The ceiling clamp fired 4 times in that one slot alone. Answering the original question directly: this is mechanism (a), "progressive overload applying repeatedly without a reset at block boundaries" — confirmed definitively by the code (`Math.min(previous + step, freshEstimate)`, additive, never a percentage) and by every observed trajectory (clean, constant-kg steps, never a multiplicative pattern). Separately reproduced actual raw (pre-clamp) computed values reaching 108-118kg on 48-50kg-ceiling implements (Romanian Deadlifts, Kettlebell Swing) in the wider 96-combo sweep — all safely caught by the clamp before display, but confirms the raw numbers this mechanism can produce are exactly as dangerous as flagged. Report-only, no fix, per the load-prescription plan-first rule. Also still true from the categorize() angle: `n.includes('squat')`/`n.includes('deadlift')` fire before the later `'bulgarian'/'split squat'`/`'romanian'` checks, so Bulgarian Split Squats/Romanian Deadlifts get the FULL barbell standard instead of their scaled-down derived category — confirmed via `cat=deadlift` on Romanian Deadlifts in this round's trace too.
- [x] SUPERSEDED by the item above — earlier pass (16 fixed-80kg-male mesocycle-safety cases → 96 across 50-120kg/both genders) is now folded into the base-grid extension.

Mitigating (and why this was NOT rushed into the nutrition slice): for anyone without verified working lifts those loads are already treated as unverified and clamped by the calibration-week machinery, then corrected from what they actually lift — so training genuinely works, it just isn't honest about what it assumed.
