- [x] MACRO AUDIT (item 1 of Ashley's four-item meal queue), and the one real finding in it. **Audited, found no outright bug**: `computeBMR` is Mifflin–St Jeor and correctly implemented; `DYNAMIC_CSCS` is internally consistent (0 mismatches across 324 day-types); the apparent calorie "overshoot" was rounding — only **1 of 303** cases exceeded 10 kcal and that one is the disclosed calorie floor engaging; and the hardcoded 2.2g/kg protein in dynamic mode is deliberate, with the split control disabled and a `disabledReason` saying so. Measuring before claiming is the whole point of that paragraph — three of those four looked like bugs until counted.
  The real finding was the **flat 500 kcal fat-loss deficit**. The same arithmetic for everyone is a very different diet for everyone: a 45kg woman training moderately was cut **28%** below maintenance and a 100kg active man **14%**, so the harshness fell entirely on lighter people. Put to Ashley because it changes what live users eat; she chose **scale it, capped at 500**. Built as `FAT_LOSS_DEFICIT_FRACTION = 0.20` with `FAT_LOSS_DEFICIT_CAP_KCAL = 500` in `applyGoalAdjustment`.
  **THE CAP IS THE SAFETY PROPERTY**, and it is what made this shippable to people already on a plan: anyone whose 20% exceeds 500 keeps the 500 they had, so no existing target can move DOWN. Measured across 672 bodies (14 weights x 4 activity levels x 4 ages x 3 heights): **0 targets cut deeper**, deficits over 25% of TDEE **129 -> 0**, pinned to the calorie floor **135 -> 65**, and 375 of 672 (56%) move, all upward. NOTE THE DENOMINATOR CHANGED — the earlier "6 of 36" figure swept weights x activity only; this one adds ages and heights, so the two counts are not comparable.
  Hypertrophy's +300 was deliberately NOT scaled. The same disproportion exists (17% of a 45kg woman's maintenance, 10% of a 100kg man's) but Ashley's ruling covered the cut, and a surplus that runs slightly large costs a little unwanted weight where a deficit that runs too deep costs muscle and adherence. Flagged in a comment at the site rather than changed silently.
  **The gate was a liar on its first draft and mutation-testing caught it.** `test:fat-loss-deficit` recomputed the target from the two exported constants instead of calling `applyGoalAdjustment`, so every section proved the gate agreed with itself and would have stayed green through any mutation of the function. `applyGoalAdjustment` is now exported and the gate calls it, plus a new §7 that ties it to what the Nutrition tab actually displays via `getMacroDerivation` — because sections 2-6 prove the function, not the app. Five mutations confirmed biting: flat-500 restored (§3 red), cap removed (§2 red), fraction deepened to 30% (§3 red), `computeStaticMacros` bypassing the function (§7 red), and a stray `tdee - 500` left elsewhere in the file (§7 red).
  `test:macro-split` §5 went red at 58kg and it was **the gate, not the change**: its "old hardcoded formula" baseline re-encoded `Math.max(1500, tdee - 500)`, making that section a second, undeclared assertion about the deficit while its actual subject is the SPLIT. It now takes its calorie target from `applyGoalAdjustment` and reallocates within it; re-mutation-tested (default protein 2.0 -> 2.1) to confirm it still bites. Only 58kg failed because the other three weights sit above the cap and genuinely did not move — the cap working, visible in a gate that wasn't looking for it.

- [x] "ADD THIS MEAL TO MY PLAN" (item 2 of the meal queue) — the confirmed missing capability, now built. The coach had `propose_meal_swap`, `log_meal`, `add_to_grocery_list` and `check_off_grocery_item` and **nothing that put a new meal into the plan**, so asking for one landed where every request the app can't honour lands: a warm reply and nothing else. Planned first (docs/PLAN-add-a-meal.md) because it opens a new route into what someone eats, and CLAUDE.md puts dietary enforcement in the always-plan-first set.
  **The whole design is one decision: it adds no verification of its own.** A user-named dish is text the model wrote; had it reached `meal_plan_slots` on its own path, the app would have a second, unguarded door onto the allergen path — the "constraint asserted at N paths, missed at N+1" shape this codebase keeps producing. So `buildMealAdditionProposal` routes the dish into `verifyProposal`, the exact function every generated meal already passes: food-DB resolution, the 80% coverage floor (**a dish the app can't measure is refused, not guessed at**), the disliked-foods filter, `validateMealAgainstDiet`, rescaling to the slot's macro budget, and a re-measure afterwards. One enforcement point. It lives client-side because `verifyProposal` is a `src/lib` module Deno can't import; `chat-gemini` is a courier for this kind and writes nothing.
  Deliberate shapes worth keeping: the payload carries the **verified** option so confirm writes exactly what the card showed and the model's own quantities never reach the database; the executor **never deletes** (`persistPools` wipes a slot's pool before inserting — right for a regenerate, catastrophic for an add: asking for one curry would have silently deleted the other four dinners); a failed pick rolls the pool write back rather than leaving a meal the receipt is about to say couldn't be added; and undo removes the option AND clears the pick, since an undo that only dropped the pick would leave the meal in the options forever.
  Refusals say **why**, translated from `verifyProposal`'s own reject log rather than replaced — "I can't add X, it clashes with what you avoid" beats "I couldn't add that", which is the reply this feature exists to stop the app giving.
  Decisions taken without asking, all mechanical or already-settled policy: the dish is **re-portioned to the slot budget** (not new policy — `scaleToTarget` already does it to every meal, and one exempt meal would hole the day-total guarantees); a dish that can't be made to fit is **refused with the reason** (consistent with generation); an added meal **joins the pool AND becomes that date's pick**, because a meal that silently joined a pool of five alternatives nobody opens is a feature that looks built and does nothing — both effects are named on the card and reversible.
  `test:meal-addition`, safety section first: for **every one of the 21 preferences that forbid tags**, a dish built around a food carrying that tag is refused, and refused AS A DIET CLASH — not incidentally on an earlier check. `mediterranean` is exempted **by its empty tag set, never by its name** (it is a documented style preference, diet-rules.ts:53); key the exemption on the name and the gate becomes one that asserts a real gap as correct, which is what the five untagged allergens had going for them until earlier this session. Give mediterranean a forbidden tag tomorrow and the loop starts demanding a fixture for it.
  Four mutations confirmed biting: dietary preferences not reaching `verifyProposal` (**21 checks red**), the payload carrying the model's raw ingredient strings (the stored-shape check, added after the first draft caught it only statically), scaling skipped so the model's portions survive, and the executor deleting the slot's pool before inserting.

- [x] BUG, found while scoping item 3 of the meal queue: **"swap this for something else" could hand back the meal just rejected.** `swapPoolMeal` picked uniformly at random from the alternatives, so with the default pool of five, saying "no, something else" had a **one-in-four chance** of returning the option turned down a moment earlier — and nothing distinguished that from the app ignoring the request. Now rotates from the current option's own `pool_index` position, so N-1 swaps show N-1 different meals and the order is stable across calls and reloads. Mechanical, so built without asking. `test:meal-swap-rotation` (offline, stubbed client — a swap rule this simple shouldn't need a live TEST project that pauses after a week idle). Mutation: putting the random draw back went red on every run, and "4 swaps produced 4 different meals" failed on 2 of 3 — the repeat rate, visible.
  Its `Math.random` text check strips comments before scanning: the rotation's comment quotes the draw it replaced, which is worth keeping and is not code. Caught by the check failing on its own explanation.

- [x] **CORRECTION to the entry above, and the real bug underneath it.** I reported the swap fix as user-visible. It was not: `chat-gemini` fetched the pool itself and passed `chooseName` on every call, so `swapPoolMeal`'s no-`chooseName` branch — the one carrying the random draw I "fixed" — **was unreachable from the app**. How I got it wrong: I read the random draw in `meal-store.ts`, saw `executeMealSwap` calling `swapPoolMeal`, and stopped, without tracing that the payload's `chooseName` had already been filled in server-side. The same shape is worth checking wherever a client function takes an optional "or pick one for me" argument that a server always supplies.
  **The live bug was worse.** The server picked `alternatives[0]` — the first pool option that isn't the current one — so chat swaps ping-ponged: A → B → A → B, forever. With `DEFAULT_POOL_SIZE = 5`, **three of every five generated meals were unreachable through the chat entirely**. And because the server always chose, the rule existed in two places with the live copy being the broken one — the N+1 shape again (two food databases, four places a dietary preference must be listed, and now two meal choosers).
  Fixed by removing the duplication rather than patching both: `nextPoolOption` in `meal-store.ts` is now the ONLY chooser, and `propose_meal_swap` became a courier like `propose_exercise_swap` and `propose_meal_addition` — the client rotates and builds the diff. An edge function cannot import `src/lib`, so a chooser living there is a second copy by construction; that is the argument for moving it, not tidiness. Gated: 4 chat swaps must reach 4 distinct meals and cover the whole pool, and the edge function must no longer read `meal_plan_slots` or filter the options at all. Reintroducing `alternatives[0]` drops it to **1 distinct meal reachable**.
- [x] ASHLEY'S RULING (asked, because generating costs money per use): when someone has been through all their options, **offer to find new ones** — "that's everything I've got for lunch, want me to find you some new ones?" — rather than always generating, or leaving it bounded. Built exactly that way: nothing is generated unless they confirm, and **their existing meals are kept**.
  The offer is a confirmable CARD, not plain text, deliberately: an offer with no `pending_actions` row means a later "yes" has nothing to resolve and goes back through the model, which can misclassify it again and loop — the trap already documented here for `record_fact`. `propose_meal_pool_refresh` resolves client-side.
  "They've seen the lot" is a fact, not a guess: `mealOptionsSeenRef` records every option the conversation actually put in front of them, per slot. A ref rather than state because the swap builder reads it inside the same async turn that writes it — with `setState` the offer would arrive one swap late. Per-conversation on purpose: someone returning a week later should be offered their own pool again before being asked to spend on new ones.
  `generateMealPools` gained `appendToExisting`, which routes to a **separate `appendPools`** rather than a flag inside `persistPools` — the difference between them is a DELETE, and a flag deciding whether a user's meals survive is one misread argument from wiping them. Existing options are loaded up front so the duplicate-name and cuisine-coherence checks see them, or "find me something new" could hand back the five already sitting there. Four mutations confirmed biting, including append-becomes-replace and the dedupe losing sight of the existing pool.

- [x] SOFT FOOD PREFERENCES ARE READ BY SOMETHING (item 4 of the meal queue, and the other half of the finding the exercise side already fixed). `compileSoftFoodPreferences` had **zero call sites**, so "I love salmon" was recorded, compiled, shown back in the memory screen, and read by nothing. VISION-ARCHITECTURE.md §1.2 had already named `assembleDay`'s candidate scoring as the consumer, so wiring it executes an existing decision rather than inventing one.
  **A lean, not a filter**, the same line the exercise half draws. A day that works in nothing they said they liked takes `SOFT_FOOD_MISS_PENALTY = 0.01` — the same magnitude as the existing day-to-day repeat penalty, and a fifth of what a 5% calorie miss already scores. Both options stay in the pool; hard dislikes are a different channel entirely (filtered out at generation time, never reaching here).
  **Where it stops mattering is measured, not asserted.** Calories carry weight 1.0 in `macroDistanceScore`, so on a 1200 kcal day the crossover is ~12 kcal: a liked meal **8 kcal** worse still wins, one **16 kcal** worse already loses. Gated at both sides of that boundary, because "doesn't win when 200 kcal worse" is an easy bar that proves nothing about a nutrition path.
  At least ONE liked thing per day, not as many as possible — someone who loves salmon wants salmon once, not at every meal.
  Matching deliberately covers the dish NAME as well as ingredients, which is **wider than the hard dislike filter** (ingredients only). The asymmetry is the point: a false positive on a nudge costs nothing, a false positive on a filter takes food off someone's plate they'd happily have eaten — and a like far more often names a dish ("I love a curry") than an ingredient, so ingredient-only matching would leave most likes unable to match anything.
  Threaded through the GROCERY path too, and that is a correctness fix rather than tidiness: `assembleDay` is deterministic, and the shopping list is built by assembling the same days the Nutrition tab shows. Give one the preferences and not the other and they assemble different days — a shopping list for meals the app never serves. Gated that App passes the identical value to both.
  Four mutations confirmed biting: the bias computed but never added to the score, the penalty cranked to 0.5 so a preference beats macro fit, name-matching dropped, and the grocery horizon quietly not passing them.
  `memoryFacts`' state declaration moved above the assembled-day derivation — it was declared after the line that now needs it. Moved, not duplicated.

- [x] DEVIATION, flagged unprompted: **I committed a regenerated `audit-report.txt`** in the soft-food-preferences commit. A background `test:audit` run rewrote the file inside the window where `git add -A` picked it up. PROJECT-LOG is unambiguous — "Never commit a regenerated report — `git checkout --` it back after running" — so it is reverted here to the committed snapshot.
  Worth recording *why* the convention exists, because the diff looked like good news and wasn't: the audit is **deliberately non-deterministic run-to-run** (PROJECT-LOG §"exercise-plan.ts uses Math.random() for exercise variety by design"), so the snapshot moving from 54 failures to 0 is not a measurement of anything. It is a snapshot for diffing *shape*, and it is meant to be compared by failure CATEGORY, never by exact count. Committing a regeneration turns a diagnostic into a claim.
  The live run is the number that counts, and it was taken this session: `test:audit` **0 / 13,967**, 71s.

- [x] **WEIGHTS THAT DON'T EXIST, defect 1: four routing rules were written and never reached.** Found in `test:quality`'s console output, not in any gate — a completed sweep logged **574,937** instances of `prescribeLoad`'s own warning: *"computed 78kg, above the 48kg realistic ceiling — clamping. This is a safety net, not a fix: something upstream produced a wrong number and should be traced."* Nobody had traced it. Plan: docs/PLAN-weights-that-do-not-exist.md.
  `categorize()` matches names by substring **in order**, and four rules sat AFTER the generic `deadlift`/`squat` matches — unreachable for any name containing those words. **Romanian Deadlifts** was priced as a full conventional deadlift, **Bulgarian Split Squats** and **Split Squat (Bodyweight)** as a bilateral barbell back squat, and **Farmer Squat Hold** as a back squat rather than a carry.

  **Why the clamp made it worse, not better:** a clamp produces a plausible figure, so nothing downstream complains — but once it engages the prescription **stops telling people apart**. Measured: 19 of 48 sampled profiles land on exactly the 48kg kettlebell ceiling, so a 55kg lifter and a 110kg lifter are handed the same bell.
  Measured at the fix, whole DB swept: **exactly 4 of 145** categories change, and the three cases where an earlier rule winning IS deliberate (`Goblet Squats`, `Hack Squat`, `Overhead Carry`) all hold. Loads: Romanian Deadlifts **47/48 profiles, median 0.56x**; Bulgarian Split Squats **48/48, median 0.40x** — a male intermediate at 85kg goes from **44kg to 18kg per hand**. 44kg in each hand for a Bulgarian split squat is not a conservative estimate needing a trim; it is a weight almost nobody should be handed.
  **The measurement caught something reasoning didn't:** a first attempt moved `carry` above the generic rules without keeping `overhead_carry` ahead of it, silently reclassifying `Overhead Carry`. That is why the four rules now live adjacent and commented rather than sorted by topic, and why the gate has a whole-DB trapdoor and not just named cases.
  `test:categorize-precedence`: the four intended routes, the three deliberate shadows, and a **generated** frozen snapshot of all 145 name→category pairs. The first draft of that snapshot was hand-typed and wrong on 15 of 20 categories — the gate caught its own author, which is the argument for freezing a measured state rather than a recollection of one. Mutations: restoring the original ordering fails 5 checks; the `carry`-before-`overhead_carry` near-miss fails 2.
  `test:audit` held at **0 / 13,967** — this changes load, not structure. Load-adjacent gates all green (`test:load-ceilings`, `test:ceiling-units`, `test:load-suggestions`, `test:added-load`, `test:block-consistency`, `test:week-load-consistency`, `test:pattern-tags`, `test:main-lift-rest`).

It isn't — `exercise-db.ts` documents it at the entry: it falls through to the `hip_hinge` default and *"under-prescribes. - [x] **THE ARTIFACT RULE IS NOW ENFORCED, NOT REMEMBERED.** PROJECT-LOG has said *"never commit a regenerated report"* for a long time, and I violated it **twice in one session** — once with `audit-report.txt` (`3754730`, reverted in `8ea8934`) and again with `quality-report.txt` (`6d7378d`, which reached main). Both times identically: a background `test:audit`/`test:quality` sweep rewrote the file inside the window where `git add -A` picked it up. The second one happened *after* I had written the first up as a lesson, which is the whole argument — a sweep finishing at an unlucky moment beats anyone's memory, so the fix cannot be "be careful".
  `.githooks/pre-commit` now refuses any commit that stages `audit-report.txt`, `quality-report.txt`, `meal-quality-report.txt`, `differentiation-audit-report.txt` or `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo`, and prints the exact un-stage commands. A `prepare` script wires `core.hooksPath` on `npm install` so it installs itself. It **blocks rather than silently un-stages**: quietly dropping a staged file would make `git add -A && git commit` not commit what it said it did, which is a worse surprise than a readable error. `--no-verify` remains for a deliberate snapshot update.
  Also: **`meal-quality-report.txt` was never tracked before this session** (PROJECT-LOG §"Discard `meal-quality-report.txt` after — it was never committed"). It was swept in by the same mechanism in `dbc4c3a`. Now untracked and gitignored. The other two stay tracked — they are committed snapshots whose *regenerations* must not be, which is why they can't just be gitignored and need the hook instead.

Four mutations confirmed biting: name ask moved first, the wall restored, "or tap an option" reintroduced, and the component hand-rolling the opener again.

- [x] **TONE: the onboarding coach reads cold and clipped** (Ashley, from a real session; she picked that description from four options rather than me guessing at it). Diagnosed rather than assumed: the voice block opened with ONE word of warmth — "Warm" — followed immediately by four length constraints and then THIRTEEN prohibitions. A coach assembled purely out of things-not-to-do comes out as bare question, bare question, bare question. The anti-grading rule in particular (added for a real bug she found — "That 12% target is a classic, sharp goal to aim for") told the model not to comment on routine answers, and combined with NO STOCK CLOSERS and a 2-4 sentence cap, left nothing between the questions.
  New `=== WHAT WARM ACTUALLY SOUNDS LIKE ===` block placed BEFORE the prohibitions, on the principle that the model should read what it IS before what to avoid. Its load-bearing claim: **warmth is not a compliment bolted onto a question, it lives in HOW you ask** — "Three days and coming back from a year out, how long have you realistically got on those days?" is not a comment on their answer, it is proof of listening. Plus taking their side out loud when something sounds hard, contractions and asides, curiosity as warmth, and a self-test: if your last three turns would work word-for-word for a different person, you are being a form again. The anti-grading ban is untouched.
  **THIS IS SAFE TO ATTEMPT NOW IN A WAY IT WAS NOT BEFORE.** `fa683fc` was a tone rewrite that fixed grading 7/7 -> 0/7 and silenced the model on 4 of 7 turns, because "react every turn" turned out to be load-bearing for RELIABILITY, not voice. That load now sits in `reply-resolver.ts` (`resolveReply`), gate-tested against every measured failure shape — so the prompt is free to stop demanding a reaction without the turn coming back empty. `test:reply-guarantee` green.
  **STILL UNMEASURED, and that is the honest state.** No tone change in this project has ever been measured against the real model. The before/after is now set up rather than promised: run `probe-onboarding-tone.mts` BEFORE deploying to capture the cold baseline, deploy, run it again.

So `probe-onboarding-tone.mts` is safe against production today; it costs Gemini calls and writes nothing.

The probe's own header says "It creates no profile and writes nothing to any database" and I did not read it — the same "read the file's own comment before assuming its contract" failure as the Hip Thrust one, twice in a day.

- [x] **THE OPENER IS NOW ASHLEY'S, VERBATIM.** She supplied draft copy and iterated across three rounds: the intro must explain what the app can do; no question count ("don't say dozen questions" — a number makes the interview sound like a form with a length, where the topic list sets the expectation without a countdown); and, shown three complete candidates as previews, she rejected the capabilities-first five-message version outright ("i don't like it") and picked the one closest to her own draft — her flow (get to know you first, then what gets built), abilities in one plain sentence in the middle. Nothing changes without your okay." / "I'll ask about your goals, what you've got to train with, what your week actually looks like — and you can ask me anything as we go. First: what should I call you?"*

Every claim still maps to a shipped mechanism (log → NL logging, swap → proposals, injuries → adaptation path, remember → user_facts, okay → confirm cards, ask anything → the chat answers questions).

Mutations: stripping message 2 fails 3 checks; softening "okay" out of the promise fails 3. `render:screens` verified at 412px: three messages, question above the fold, composer visible with room.

- [x] **TONE CAN NOW BE MEASURED, not just asserted.** Every tone claim in this project's history — mine included — has been reasoning about a prompt, or a gate run against a scripted fake model. Ashley said the onboarding coach reads "cold and clipped"; the honest answer to that is a before/after on the DEPLOYED function, not a better-sounding prompt. `scripts/compare-tone.mts` (`npm run tone:compare -- before.json after.json`) reads two probe transcripts and puts numbers on it. Free to run — it reads JSON, no API calls, unlike the probe that produces the files.
  **What "cold and clipped" IS, made countable**: the signature is a turn that is nothing but a question. So: bare-question rate, sentences and words per turn, whether the reply reuses a distinctive word the user just said (the prompt's core warmth mechanic), contractions per turn, opener variety, chip rate.
  **Two metrics are guard rails, not warmth**, and they are why this exists rather than a vibe check. **Empty replies must stay 0** — `fa683fc` was a tone rewrite that fixed grading 7/7 → 0/7 and silenced the model on 4 of 7 turns, which is the exact failure mode of the change now queued. **Grading must stay 0** — the anti-appraisal rule was added for a real bug Ashley hit ("That 12% target is a classic, sharp goal to aim for"); warmth returning as compliments is not the fix, it is the old bug wearing the new one's clothes. Either breaking prints a STOP line naming the remedy.
  Validated against three synthetic fixtures with known answers rather than assumed correct: a deliberately cold transcript (100% bare questions, 0% echo) vs a warm one (0% bare, 60% echo, contractions up) moves every metric the right way; a **regression fixture** — warm-sounding but with grading back and one silent turn — is caught, 4 grading hits and 1 empty reply, STOP fires.
  Prints the sample size and what one turn is worth in percentage points (the persona is 9 turns, so ~11pp), because a 9-turn sample cannot support fine distinctions, and ends by saying outright that the numbers cannot tell you whether it sounds like a person — they say where to look.

- [x] **THE INJURY SUBSTITUTION WAS REPEATING ITSELF** (item 1 of three Ashley asked for in order). Chasing `test:injury-rebuild`'s two red assertions — "rebuild must keep more slots than substitution", failing 436 vs 444. The obvious reading is that the rebuild regressed. **It hadn't.** The loss was *spread, not concentrated*: 80 sessions in both, 16 gained a slot, 28 lost one, deload weeks untouched. Nothing failed to fill.
  The structural comparison is what the count was standing in for, and it inverted the conclusion: original 448 slots / 62 distinct / **0** duplicates; substituted 444 / 45 / **28 duplicates within a single session**; rebuilt 436 / 49 / **0**. A real substituted session, printed rather than counted: `Band Dislocates | Barbell Floor Press | Landmine Press | Landmine Press | Tricep Pushdowns | Side Plank | Barbell Floor Press` — seven "exercises", four movements. **Substitution's 444 was inflated by duplicates**, so an honest rebuild was being compared against a padded number.
  The defect: `substituteSlots` HAD a duplicate guard that could not work. It read the ORIGINAL `day.exercises` (so it only knew names already there, never ones chosen this pass) and every slot resolved inside `Promise.all` (so no slot could see another's choice). Two conflicting slots got the same ranked list and both took `candidates[0]`. The comment above it explaining the filtering gave false confidence over a mechanism that never ran — this codebase's signature defect, again.
  Fixed by sequencing **within a day** and accumulating chosen names; weeks and days stay parallel because that is the only place the collision occurs. Swept 96 profiles (4 equipment × 3 experience × 8 injuries): **224 duplicate placements → 0**.
  **The count assertions were RIGHT all along** and now pass on their own terms — substitution 444 → 432, rebuild unchanged at 436. No gate rewrite needed; the gate was vindicated. Added `[2b]` naming the real defect, so the next failure points at the duplicate rather than blaming the rebuild. Mutation: restoring the parallel form reproduces the exact original failure AND fails the new check.
  **The risk I flagged did not fire, and I measured rather than assumed:** dropping instead of repeating raises `dropped` 4 → 16 and `planLossRatio` 0.9% → 3.6%, which feeds `assessAdaptation`'s `shouldRebuild` threshold (15%). Across 96 profiles `shouldRebuild` stayed at **18/96, unchanged** — nobody is offered a different mode. Plan: `docs/PLAN-substitution-repeats-itself.md`.
- [x] **CALF RAISES: the ceiling was wrong, not the load** (item 2 — the last of the impossible weights). 45,239 clamp warnings, all 102.5kg against the generic 100kg `stack` ceiling. **A dedicated calf machine is not a cable tower** — it is plate-loaded or has its own stack and takes 150-200kg, and calves are genuinely strong. Cutting the formula to fit a ceiling never meant for that machine would have under-prescribed a whole muscle group to silence a warning. `CALF_MACHINE_CEILING_KG = 250`, following the `LEG_PRESS_CEILING_KG` precedent — a named exception with its reasoning, gated on loading mode so `Single-Leg Dumbbell Calf Raise` keeps its **correct** 48kg clamp (48kg really is the heaviest dumbbell most gyms own — the honest kind of clamp).
  ASHLEY'S RULING, put to her as weights rather than a number: lift the cap. The old 100kg cap flattened **every rep bracket to the same figure** for a 120kg advanced lifter — heavy triples and sets of twenty alike — the identical "stopped telling people apart" failure the kettlebell ceiling produced. 27 of 200 cases (14%) were capped; nobody lighter or newer changes. Top end 182.5kg ≈ 1.5× bodyweight.
  `SAFETY_CEILING_KG_TOTAL.isolation_calf` 140 → 230: it had been calibrated when the implement clamp held calf work at 100kg, so it was never exercised above that and only appeared consistent because the tighter limit bound first.
  **MY MEASUREMENTS FOR THIS ITEM WERE WRONG TWICE AND A GATE CAUGHT IT.** I called `prescribeLoad` with `reps`/`intensity` instead of `repRangeLabel`/`targetRpeLabel`, so it silently used defaults — every calf figure I quoted before `test:ceiling-units` failed was measured with the wrong API. Correct call: the load *does* scale by rep range (132.5 → 182.5kg), which is exactly what the cap was flattening. Wrong keys on an options object is a silent-default failure worth watching for; the gate disagreeing with me is what surfaced it.
  Mutation found a real gap: pointing the exception at the whole `isolation_calf` category — handing a dumbbell exercise a 250kg ceiling — **passed every gate**. Nothing asserted that a dumbbell lift gets a dumbbell-sized ceiling. New check: a named ceiling exception may only ever apply to `stack` mode, since the other four modes are physical facts about the implement. It now catches that mutation.
- [x] **The tone comparator would have reported a fabricated zero** (item 3). `compare-tone.mts` reads `turns[].actions` (onboarding shape); `probe-coach-tone.mts` writes `turns[].action` — singular, nullable, no array. It would not crash; it would confidently print "0 chip turns" forever. A fabricated zero that looks like a measurement is worse than an absent one, so the shape is now detected and inapplicable metrics are **omitted rather than zeroed** (chips are an onboarding concept — the main chat has no `present_slot`). Comparing a coach transcript against an onboarding one is refused outright: different probes, different conversations. Verified against both shapes plus the regression fixture, which still fires STOP.
  **CORRECTION, and it is the second of its kind today: `probe-coach-tone.mts` does NOT write to the live database.** I told Ashley it did and that it needed pointing at TEST first. It sends **no `profile_id`**, and every write in `chat-gemini` is guarded on `if (profileId)`. Both probes are production-safe. Both files say so in their own header comment. Same failure as the Hip Thrust call and the onboarding probe: reasoning about a file instead of reading what it says about itself — **three times in one day**, which makes it a habit rather than three incidents. Read the header first.

Original note: pre-existing and untouched by that work: `test:band-slots` — rehab-band placements in main slots at 318 against a floor of 335. Property checks all pass (48 of 48 knee-injured profiles still receive a knee-indicated movement), so no rehab is lost; the count proxy has drifted a third time (348 → 340 → 318). - [x] **THREE OF THE EIGHT INJURIES DID NOTHING AT ALL.** Found by generating two plans and diffing: a plan for someone reporting a bad **hip, elbow or ankle** came back BYTE-FOR-BYTE IDENTICAL to a healthy person's. A hip-injured user was still handed **Deadlifts**. The app collected the injury, stored it, showed it back on the profile screen, and threw it away — the one place it silently discards safety information a user deliberately gave it, made worse by knees and shoulders working properly so nobody would suspect.
  **It was known and written down.** `getFlaggedJoints`' own doc comment read *"Only 5 of the 8 INJURY_OPTIONS codes are mapped — hips/ankles/elbows are collected at onboarding but currently have no joint tag to filter on."* Nothing failed while that was true.
  Not a one-line map fix: the `hip` tag sat on 1 exercise and `elbow` on 2, `ankle` did not exist. **Coverage and effect are different claims** — mapping `hips: ['hip']` alone would have satisfied "is it mapped?" while still changing nothing. 30 catalogue entries tagged with `contraindicated_joints`, per the shoulder audit's rule (*"`loads_joints` answers 'does this joint participate?', and it is being used to answer 'is this dangerous?'"*).
  ASHLEY'S RULING on hips set the calibration and transfers to the other two: **drop the heavy loaded work, keep the controlled movements, the session stays whole.** Hip drops barbell squats/deadlifts/RDLs/good mornings/swings/walking lunges/Bulgarian split squats and KEEPS leg press, goblet squats, hip thrusts, glute bridges, step-ups, all curls/extensions/calves. Verified: **hip wipes no movement pattern at any of the four equipment tiers.** All eight injuries now visibly change a plan.
  **I INTRODUCED A SERIOUS BUG DOING THIS AND A GATE CAUGHT IT.** `contraindicatedJoints()` is `contraindicated_joints ?? loads_joints`, so writing that field AT ALL replaces the `loads_joints` fallback instead of adding to it. Tagging Barbell Squats for the hip therefore **un-banned it for the knee**. Measured damage: knees 25 → 15, wrists 21 → 13, lower_back 12 → 5, shoulders 47 → 40 — four existing injuries silently weakened while the new three looked like a success. Surfaced by `test:band-slots` moving 318 → 221; I chased the number instead of assuming it was noise, which is the only reason it was caught. Fixed by merging `loads_joints` into the tag (22 entries needed the carry-over); all five pre-existing counts restored exactly and band-slots returned to its pre-existing 318.
  `test:injury-coverage`: §1 every code maps to a joint, §2 every code visibly changes a plan (the two are different, and §1 alone would have passed the old bug), §3 **the shoulder lesson** — no exercise may be both rehab FOR a joint and banned for it, §4 a frozen wipe list **verified pre-existing by computing it at the pre-change state and diffing** (all shoulder/neck/wrist wipes predate this; the only five new ones are ankle→calves, which is CORRECT, plus content gaps where no safe variant exists), §5 **frozen per-injury removal counts** — added after a mutation took a hip-injured trainee's squat options from 8 to 2 with every other check green, because over-broad tagging that stops short of wiping a pattern is otherwise invisible.
  Four mutations bite: hips unmapped (2 red), mapped-but-untagged (§2 red — coverage without effect), over-broad hip tag (§5 red), and the override bug reintroduced (§4 and §5 red, naming all four weakened injuries). `test:audit` held at 0 / 13,967.

- [x] **YOUR FOOD, YOUR AMOUNTS, YOUR DAY — the meal half of Ashley's two-part ask, 1 Sep 2026.** Her words: *"I usually have eggs and greek yoghurt and fruit for breakfast the app should ask how much of each food I'm having so it can calculate and then add that as my breakfast and plan for the rest of my meals."* Built exactly that shape: tell the coach what you eat and how much, it computes the macros of YOUR portions (never adjusting them — `keepPortions` skips only the scaling and slot-band steps; every safety check still runs: allergens, diet rules, dislikes, ingredient recognition), sets it as the slot's meal for the day, and the day assembler treats the pinned slot as fixed and re-fits the OTHER meals around it (pins are excluded from repair-scaling, so your portions are never touched to make the day balance). A stated food with no amount is asked about, never guessed. Along the way the bare-count bug died: "3 eggs" used to parse as 3 GRAMS (5 kcal) because no entry named a 'whole' size — fixed at the `unitToGrams` chokepoint (piece weight = `units.medium ?? purchaseUnit.avgGrams`). Pool depth: "More options" now exists on the meal panel itself (same `handleFindMoreMealOptions` the chat uses — one path, both surfaces), and panel alternatives are restriction-checked at display so a blocked option says why instead of failing on tap. Gated by `test:custom-meal` (portions-are-facts, no-amount-no-guess, safety-never-traded, day-refits-around-pin, chain-wired), seven mutations red. **Needs a `chat-gemini` deploy — the `propose_custom_meal` tool ships client + function together.**

Fifteen entries added (185 → 200), each with joint tags mirroring a named free-weight sibling and deviating only where the implement genuinely differs, every deviation commented at the field; all gym-only by equipment except Landmine Row and Dumbbell Pullover, which genuinely work at home. The preacher curls exist under the depth-cost rule their absence used to document (gym-only implements keep them out of thin home pools; audit re-run and held). Belt Squat priced as leg_press by name rule, not as a barbell squat. The swap dialogs now SAY what the search box can do — "Machine busy? Search all exercises" — which was half of how "wasn't an available swap" happened on a phone in a gym. Incidental fix: `searchExerciseCatalog` no longer surfaces retired entries (the one retired row was selectable via search; now gated in `test:slot-replacement-hygiene` [6], derived from the DB not a name list). Every pinned gate moved honestly: categorize snapshot regenerated with all 15 verified against their DESIGNED buckets before pasting; injury counts re-measured with every delta accounted for by name (lower_back 12→15, hips 14→18, elbows 18→21, …); wipes recomputed and identical — no new wipe, hip still wipes nothing. `test:audit` 13,967 combos, 0 failures. Three catalogue-shift ripples landed in gates and each got the honest treatment rather than a loosened check: the frozen-weeks known-offender ledger (above), and `test:added-load`'s climb check reformed from per-lift-across-plan to per-(lift, block) — its second incidental-coupling failure from a catalogue change, now asserting the actual invariant (a lift occupying ≥2 weeks of a block must move its reps; single-appearance accessories have no lever to move; vacuity guards keep it from passing by the feature disappearing).
  **AND A CORRECTION.** Mid-build I destroyed my own uncommitted catalogue work: after a mutation test on `exercise-db.ts` I "restored" it with `git checkout -- <file>` — which reverts to HEAD, wiping the fifteen uncommitted entries along with the mutation. The right restore was re-inserting the one mutated line. Recovered by replaying every edit verbatim from the session log, then re-proving equivalence (tsc, 199/200 counts, all 15 designed buckets, identical injury numbers) rather than assuming the replay was faithful. The lesson is the standing one: before any destructive command, look at what the target actually holds.

- [ ] **OFF-PLAN MEAL IDS ARE INCONSISTENT: `custom:` prefix vs bare name.** Found during the custom-meals investigation: meals added through chat proposals get ids prefixed `custom:` while some off-plan paths store the bare name, so the same meal can exist under two identities — history lookups and dedup then treat them as different meals. Nothing user-visible is broken today (display goes by name), but the next feature that keys on meal id inherits the split. Worth unifying at the write sites before that happens.

- [x] **THE WEEK NOTE SAID EVERYTHING, SO IT SAID NOTHING — Ashley's screenshots, 1 Sep 2026.** Nine photographs of the new browse screen, and every defect was in the two lines above the workout. **Mine, and the cause was one decision: the screen concatenated four separate texts with a bare space** — phase_focus, the phase's coaching, the goal's programme-level framing, and this week's progression note — without reading what the four said together. Consequences: a run-on where they met (*"controlled tempo The volume phase"*, because phase_focus is a phrase and doesn't end in a full stop); ten lines, so **no complete workout was on screen** on four of the nine shots; week 1 telling her to find RPE 6 twice (a paragraph I hardcoded on top of the one the plan already carried); the same *"Diet drives the fat loss here…"* boilerplate on the first week of every block; and week 9 carrying *"drop the load if you need to"* and *"every later week adds load on top of"* in one breath.
  Now `week-note.ts`: **what this block is for, then what this week does differently** — phase_focus's first sentence, terminated, plus coach_note's last sentence, which is `buildProgressionNote`'s single sentence. Measured across four generated plans: **590 → 152-209 characters.** The safety-relevant sentences survive by assertion, not by luck (the calibration week still says how to find RPE 6; a deload still says it is not a stall).
  **A guard I wrote and then deleted, because removing it changed no test:** a de-duplicating filter for the doubled *"Deload week — … Deload week — …"*. Taking one sentence cannot produce two copies of it, so the filter sat over a problem the shape already solved — an un-exercised mechanism with a helpful-sounding comment, this repo's most familiar defect. The structural fix stays; the filter is gone.
  Two fixes at source, which **reach new plans only** — notes are persisted per week, so a trainee mid-programme keeps the text their plan was written with (asserted, so the claim stays true): the deload note no longer opens two sentences with *"Deload week —"* saying the same thing (the nudge is folded into the surviving sentence as a clause), and *"the working weight every later week…"* gains its missing "that". **Ashley's live plan keeps that one missing word until her plan is rebuilt; everything else is fixed by the display trim.**

- [x] **THE DELTA CHIP TOLD HER "+0" AND "+27" AND MEANT NEITHER.** Same screenshots: *"+0 sets vs last week"* on three weeks in four (volume holds flat inside a block by design while load ramps — so the one line whose job is explaining the week explained nothing, while the note underneath said the weight went up), and *"+27 sets vs last week"* opening block 3 — because the week before it was a **deload**, so a normal week beside it looks like an explosion. **Ashley's ruling: "same sets, heavier weights".** Built in `week-delta.ts` with two conditions she didn't have to ask for: the claim is **measured, never assumed** (a reps-emphasis or bodyweight block deliberately holds weight flat, and telling that trainee her weights got heavier is a plain untruth — so load is compared per exercise, reps are the fallback reading, and a week where some lifts rose and others fell gets no chip rather than a cherry-picked one); and a block's opening week compares against **the last real training week and says so** (`vs W7`, not `vs last week`), because a comparison the label misdescribes is worse than none. Measured on her own weeks: W13 `+9 vs last week` → `-3 vs W11`; W9 `+27` → `+3 vs W7`; W7 `+0` → `Same sets, heavier weights`.
  **THE GATE CAUGHT ME BUILDING THE SAME CLASS OF ERROR I WAS FIXING.** First version summed load × sets and called the result "heavier weights" — so a **bodyweight** plan reported heavier weights when the backpack had stayed at 8kg the whole time and the only change was Loaded Backpack Walk appearing on two days instead of one. Tonnage answers "how much work", which the set count already covers; "heavier" is a claim about the number on the implement, so it is now read off the implement. Found by the one check written specifically to catch a lie rather than a mismatch, on the one profile that has almost nothing to load.
  `test:week-note` asserts all of it against four generated plans plus **the exact strings off Ashley's screenshots** as a legacy fixture — the state her live plan is actually in, which a gate testing only freshly generated plans would have sailed past. Five mutations verified red.

The screen used the phase's lifting copy regardless.** A walking week now carries **the walk's own reason** — already written, already true, already the sentence shown on the day — instead of the phase note. Display-side, so it fixes plans already generated, which matters: notes are persisted per week.

Three things are tangled and each is hers: whether strength work should enter at some block; what the block headings should say when the plan has no lifting in it (today the hero reads "Hypertrophy" above four walks); and whether the walk ramp (20 → 35 min over 16 weeks, +5 per block) is right.

- [x] **"I WANT TO WORK OUT TODAY" REBUILT SIXTEEN WEEKS OF HER PLAN — Ashley's chat screenshot, 1 Sep 2026.** The whole exchange: *"I want to work out today"* → **"✓ Schedule updated · Training days: Tuesday, Wednesday, Friday, Saturday · Rebuilt 16 weeks from week 1 on"**. Then *"Update the workout that I should be doing today"* → *"Those are already the days you're training — nothing to change."* Then *"Not the schedule the workout what's the exercises I should be doing today"* → **the same sentence again, word for word.**

THE TOOL WAS SIMPLY WRONG FOR THE SENTENCE.** The no-op check fired on calls two and three, the confirm rail held, nothing was written twice — and none of that helps, because the failure was the model reaching for `propose_schedule_change` when asked what today's session is. Both now say the tool is for a LASTING change to which WEEKDAYS they train, name the sentences that are not one ("I want to work out today", "what should I do today?", "what are the exercises I should be doing today?"), and say what to do instead: read the day off this week's schedule and answer in text, no tool call. - [ ] **THE COACH HAS NO WAY TO SAY "TRAIN TODAY, JUST THIS ONCE."** Surfaced by the above and left open deliberately, because it is a coaching decision, not a bug. `propose_rest_day` exists for the subtraction — "I can't train this Tuesday" — and there is no matching tool for the addition. So when someone says "I want to work out today" on a day their plan has as rest, the honest answer is now "today's a rest day" and nothing more; the only way to actually train is a permanent schedule change, which is far too big a hammer. Options if Ashley wants it built: a one-off "train today instead" that swaps this week's rest day with a training day, or simply letting the session be logged off-plan without touching the plan at all.

- [x] **THE AUDIT NEVER BUILT THE PROFILE IT WAS MISSING — 2 Sep 2026.** Ashley, after four rounds of screenshots: *"why were these issues not found during the comprehensive audit?"* Answered with dates and line numbers rather than a plausible story, and the answer splits three ways. **Most did not exist yet**: the browse screen was built 1 Sep and I introduced its defects; the schedule tool only gained the power to rebuild anything at 30 Aug 12:20, **two hours and twenty minutes after the audit merged**, and was a declining stub before that. **One was genuinely missed and had been there since 16 Aug** — and the cause is one line: `dev-constraint-audit.ts` hardcoded `activity_level: 'moderate'`, while `isStartingOut` needs beginner AND sedentary, so **of 13,967 audited combinations not one was a walking plan**. The entire prescription for the most deconditioned user the app accepts sat outside the audit's reach because a single field never varied. **And even where it looked, it read numbers, not words**: every check is structural — equipment, injuries, duration, load caps, empty sessions — so all of them pass a plan that is correctly built and wrongly described.
  Both fixed. Activity level is now swept for the beginner tier (the only tier where it changes the plan — measured: `activity_level`'s other readers are all nutrition), taking the audit from **13,967 to 17,423 combinations**; prior failure counts are not comparable. `empty_session` learned that a day carrying a `plannedActivity` is prescribed work, checked on the field rather than the focus text.
  **The first run of the widened audit failed 3,456 of 17,423 — exactly the number of newly-covered combinations**, all on `pattern_coverage`/`style_pattern` ("this week contains no squat, no hinge, no push, no pull"). Every walking combination failed and no pre-existing one broke. Those two checks are now EXEMPTED for a plan with no exercises at all — not silenced: as rule violations they are false (no rule says a walk must contain a squat), and as a finding they are real and are the open question below. Keyed on the plan having no exercises, so the moment a starting-out plan gains one, both apply again in full.

The browse screen had stopped showing that text, so a check that only looked at a screen would have called it fixed. A walk week's stored note is now the walk's own reason. - [x] **A DESK JOB WAS DECIDING WHETHER SOMEONE GOT TO LIFT — 2 Sep 2026.** Asked when strength work should start for a beginner, Ashley refused the question and reframed it: *"it depends. we need to capture what the user wants. is this someone who just wants to be a bit more active or someone who wants to start exercising in a gym with weights"*. She was right, and the measurement is stark. `isStartingOut` was `beginner && sedentary` and **nothing else**, so:
  | Profile | Result |
  |---|---|
  | beginner · **sedentary** · **full gym** · **"Muscle growth — build size & strength"** | **0 exercises.** Walk / Walk / Walk |
  | the same person, "moderately active" | 16 exercises, Full Body Power ×3 |
  The only thing that decided it was *"How active is your day-to-day, **outside training**?" → "Sedentary — desk job, little movement outside training"* — a question asked to compute TDEE, whose own description presupposes they train. Anyone with an office job answers it honestly and was silently rerouted, **overriding the two answers that actually stated intent**.
  **Goal decides now.** "Muscle growth" and "Functional strength — move better, lift heavier" both say it outright, so those go straight to training and are never asked (Ashley's ruling: ask **only when it can't tell**). Fat loss and conditioning are true of a walker and a lifter alike, so those trainees get one conditional question — *"Where do you want to start?" → 🚶 Get moving first / 🏋️ Straight into training* — on the same `requiredIf` mechanism `knowsWorkingLifts` uses, placed after `equipment` so every input it reads is answered. Plan: `docs/plans/what-do-you-actually-want.md`.
  **The null case is deliberately conservative**: an existing beginner+sedentary profile with a fat-loss goal was never asked, so it keeps the walking plan it already has rather than being handed loaded sessions on its next rebuild. Only an explicit "straight into training" moves someone off walks. Three mutations red.
  **AND THE AUDIT HAD TO MOVE IN THE SAME COMMIT.** It was widened to cover walking plans that morning; that afternoon the rule for producing one went from a single field to three. Setting only `activity_level` would have left the sweep generating ordinary lifting plans **while still reporting that it covered the walking branch** — the identical silent-coverage-loss that hid this for a fortnight. `startingOutOverrides` sets all three together, spread LAST in the object so the literal fields cannot override it (spread-first was written first and did nothing — caught by reading it back). Pinned in `test:says-what-it-contains` §0c: the audit's own sedentary arm is asserted to still be a walking plan.
  **Needs `npm run db:push-both`** — migration `20260902120000_add_start_preference_to_fitness_profiles.sql` adds one nullable column.

- [x] **DO THE REP RANGES MATCH THE PHASE NAMES? MEASURED, 2 Sep 2026 — AND IT WAS ASHLEY'S CALL. SHE MADE IT; BUILT BELOW.** Her Full Program screen: weeks 5–6 read *Deadlifts 3×3-5* under a **Hypertrophy** heading whose own description promises *"moderate loads, higher volume"*. The mechanism is deliberate and simple: a phase shifts each exercise's BASE range (adaptation +3, hypertrophy **0**, strength −3, power −4, metabolic +4), and the base comes from the training STYLE per tier (combat main lifts 3-5; bodybuilding 6-8; functional 5-8; hybrid 6-10) with the GOAL's shift on top (fat loss −2 on main lifts). So a Hypertrophy block imposes no hypertrophy range; it leaves every lift where its style and goal put it. `scripts/report-rep-ranges-by-phase.ts` swept all 9,216 quality-grid plans and flagged any main-lift or secondary slot whose whole range sits outside what the phase's focus text promises:
  | phase | main-lift slots outside the promise | plans affected |
  |---|---|---|
  | Hypertrophy (top of range must reach 6) | **9.8%** — almost all 3-5 | **662 (7.2%)** |
  | Maximal Strength (bottom must not exceed 8) | 0% (secondaries 10.5%, the beginner floor + rep ramp) | — |
  | Metabolic Conditioning (top must reach 12) | **35.8%** — 7-9 and 9-10 | 1,246 (13.5%) |
  The 3-5 in a Hypertrophy block comes from exactly two places, both by design: **combat style** (main lifts based at 3-5) and the **fat-loss goal** (−2 on main lifts: functional 5-8 → 3-6 → snapped to 3-5). Bodybuilding and hybrid hypertrophy plans run 6-8/8-10 as the heading says. Top offenders by slot: Trap Bar Deadlift 3-5 (1,521), Barbell Bench Press 3-5 (1,338), Pull-Ups 3-5 (1,331), Chin-Ups 3-5 (1,175). **Nothing changed.** This is prescription, so the one question is put to Ashley in the session: should a main lift inside a Hypertrophy block be lifted to at least 6 reps, or is 3-5 the right exception for combat style and fat loss? The metabolic block's 7-9 main lifts are the NEXT question, asked only after this one is answered.
  **ASKED AND ANSWERED, same session.** Three options: lift every main lift in a Hypertrophy block to at least 6 reps (recommended); keep 3-5 and reword the heading for those plans; floor fat loss only. **Ashley chose "lift it to at least 6 reps."** Built as a floor on the BOTTOM of the range, spread preserved — 3-5 and 4-6 both become 6-8, 6-8 is untouched — because that is what "at least 6" means, and a floor on the top (the rule the measurement first used) would have let 4-6 through. **Correction to the number in the question:** the 662 plans quoted counted ranges whose top was below 6; her rule also moves 4-6, 5-6 and 5-7, so the true count is larger (re-measured under her rule, below). The floor lives on the hypertrophy phase config alone (`main_lift_rep_floor: 6`) and is applied at the one place the week's reps are shifted, through the same clamp the experience floor already uses — including the frozen-load rep bump, which re-derives its range from the same base and would otherwise have handed back 4-6 as the "bumped" version of 6-8. Load follows reps (`prescribeLoad` estimates lighter for more reps), which is the phase's promise rather than a side effect. Strength and Power set no floor and still go heavy — pinned in `test:block-phases` §6, which sweeps combat and fat-loss plans at every tier (the two sources of a 3-5) and asserts every Hypertrophy main lift starts at 6+ AND that Maximal Strength still asks for fewer than six, so a floor that leaked across phases fails the same section. Two mutations red (delete the floor: 486 under; apply it to every phase: strength drops to 0 below six). `test:audit` 17,423 / 0; nine neighbouring gates green; `test:frozen-weeks` §5 unchanged at 2.9%. What her ruling did NOT cover and this did not touch: SECONDARY lifts at 5-6 inside Hypertrophy blocks (1.9% of secondary slots — Hanging Leg Raises, Dumbbell Floor Press), and the Metabolic Conditioning block's main lifts at 7-9 (1,246 plans, 13.5%), which is the next question. **AND THE FIRST QUALITY RUN AFTER THE FLOOR CAUGHT A REGRESSION THE GATES DID NOT.** Overall held at 11.45 and nothing fell below the floor, but the frozen-week tally rose from 5,787 to **6,298 plans**: the floor had frozen the very lifts it lifted. The week's reps were one combined shift (phase + weekly ramp) clamped to the floor, and the clamp swallowed the ramp (a 4-6 pull-up under the floor: 6-8, then 5-7 clamped to 6-8, then 6-8); the frozen-load rep bump re-derived its range the same way and declined itself. **First fix tried and WITHDRAWN:** shifting in two steps so any binding floor is a minimum. It took frozen plans down to 4,138 and emptied the whole bodyweight class — and made the pre-existing beginner floor behave the same way, so a beginner's *"Maximal Strength"* main lift climbed 8-10 → 10-12 → 11-13 across the block: **17.5% of strength main lifts outside that phase's promise, from 0%** — the same kind of mismatch she had just objected to, in a block she was not asked about. **What shipped:** her floor applied as a constant lift added BEFORE the ramp and the bump (3-5 runs 6-8 → 7-9 → 8-10 under it; with no floor set the lift is 0 and nothing else changes), the experience floor untouched. Pinned in `test:block-phases` §6 (67 loadless main-lift week-1/week-2 pairs under the floor, beginners excluded on purpose; the clamp-only mutation leaves them stuck, red). **QUEUED FOR ASHLEY, BOTH SIDES MEASURED:** should the +1 rep ramp and the rep bump also climb above a beginner's floor of 8 in a Strength block? Yes = frozen plans 5,787 → 4,138 and the Pull-Ups/Chin-Ups/Glute Bridge/Push-Ups residual disappears; cost = a beginner's "Maximal Strength" block reads 8-10, 9-11, 10-12, up to 11-13 when the bar is pinned. Re-measured with what shipped: plans with any frozen exercise **5,787 → 5,471 (62.8% → 59.4%)** — down, not up, so the floor as shipped costs nothing on frozen weeks — frozen pairs 50,546 → 47,784; the bodyweight class 18,101 → 15,942 (Pull-Ups 3,372 → 2,199 and Chin-Ups 2,820 → 1,834, novices under the floor now ramping; what remains of it is the beginner floor), the loaded class 32,445 → 31,842 (Barbell Bench Press 2,314 → 1,910). For comparison, the withdrawn two-step variant measured 4,138 plans (44.9%) with the bodyweight class at 0 — that is the size of the queued beginner-floor question. That is also the shape of the four defects from her phone the day before and of the dead "How to do it" menu item, which is worth naming because it says where the next audit should start: not in the engine.
